- Phase 0 acceptance: DONE (final results `bench/results/2026-09-16-aarch64-stats-derived.md`, step 6, commit 2102fb0). Every criterion passes: 168 + 40 tests including the negative tests on aarch64 and emulated x86_64, `make fuzz` clean; all nine benchmarks within 10% of jemalloc 5.3.1 on time and RSS (larson +7.7%, alloc-test1 +2.4%, alloc-testN +1.1%, the other six faster by 4-56%; RSS 17-85% lower everywhere); the larson profile has no lock and no atomic RMW in challoc's top 10. History: the first full protocol run (`bench/results/2026-09-16-aarch64.md`, sources f2e572a) failed on larson +60.5%, xmalloc-test +254%, alloc-test1 +11.7%, alloc-testN +11.6% with `RawLock::lock_slow` and LSE atomic helpers in the top 10, and six remediation steps followed, each with its own before/after row:
  1. DONE (commit d3094de): False sharing: `ClassState` packed the read-only fields `free` loads on every call (`desc_base`, `committed_slabs`, `class`) into the same cache line as the class lock and `Inner`, so every refill/flush by any thread evicted the line from every other core. The lock now sits on its own 128-byte line (`lock::Padded`) and `Shared` is `repr(C)` with `regions` first. After-row `bench/results/2026-09-16-aarch64-classstate-padding.md` (challoc vs jemalloc, same run): larson 177.8 -> 200.5 M ops/s (gap +60.5% -> +45.0%), xmalloc-test 70.4 -> 81.2 M frees/s (+254% -> +211%), alloc-test1 2.157 -> 2.145 s (+11.3%), alloc-testN 3.089 -> 3.086 s (+13.9%), everything else unchanged within noise; RMW samples in the larson profile 7.1% -> 3.8%, `lock_slow` 4.4% -> 1.6% but still in the top 10 (5th). A first version with a 512-byte array stride regressed alloc-test1 to 2.55 s (`...-classstate-padding-512.md`); see the design decision.
  2. DONE (commit 68e3b78): One lock hold per refill and per flush. `ClassState::plan` reserves from up to 8 slabs under one lock hold (at most `nslots - live` each, skipping slabs with an in-flight claimer and clearing the bit of slabs found full), replacing `select` + `on_full`; `release` collects reopened slabs and sets their partial bits once per batch. After-row `bench/results/2026-09-16-aarch64-plan-locks.md` (challoc vs jemalloc, same run): xmalloc-test 81.2 -> 198.8 M frees/s (gap +211% -> +25.0%), larson 200.5 -> 205.4 M ops/s (+45.0% -> +39.9%), alloc-test1 2.145 -> 2.143 s (+11.3%), alloc-testN 3.086 -> 3.049 s (+12.0%), rest within noise; RSS unchanged except xmalloc-test 30 -> 38 MiB (still -73% vs jemalloc). larson profile: `lock_slow` gone from the top 10, `free`+`malloc` are 86% of challoc's samples, the only RMW left in the top 10 is `__aarch64_ldclr8_rel` (6 samples, 0.5%, 9th) from `release`'s `fetch_and`; RMW samples 1.4%. Still failing: larson +39.9%, xmalloc-test +25%, alloc-test +11-12%.
  3. DONE (commit 09bab18): 128-byte metadata lines. `meta::CACHE_LINE` is 128 and every bin from 128 up is a 128-multiple (the 192 bin is gone), so a thread's `Heap` and `ClassCache`s never share a 128-byte line with another thread's objects. Measured neutral: A/B in one container (old vs new `.so`, alternating) gave larson 208.9/205.1 vs 205.6/204.8 M ops/s and alloc-test1 2.080/2.083/2.111 vs 2.094/2.112/2.120 s, all within noise. After-row `bench/results/2026-09-16-aarch64-meta-line-128.md` (challoc vs jemalloc, same run): larson 205.4 -> 204.2 M ops/s (gap +39.9% -> +43.5%, jemalloc itself 287.4 -> 293.1), xmalloc-test 198.8 -> 201.3 M frees/s (+25.0% -> +24.4%), alloc-test1 2.143 -> 2.143 s (+11.3%), alloc-testN 3.049 -> 3.037 s (+12.1%), rest and RSS unchanged within noise. larson profile: `free` 64.3% + `malloc` 19.8% of challoc's samples, RMW samples 1.06%, `lock_slow` back at 10th with 5 samples (0.5%). Conclusion: the remaining gap is not false sharing in metadata; it is the `free`/`malloc` fast paths themselves (step 4).
  4. DONE (commits a1976ef, 7da335d; results 3c564e6): trimmed `free` and `malloc` fast paths (no class bounds re-check or `CLASSES` reload, no `bitmap_words` recomputation, frameless entry points that only tail-call a cold `malloc_slow`/`free_slow`), `heap::MAX_CAP` 1024, and signals blocked across the fork handlers (the latent race behind the emulated x86_64 `glibc_tst_mallocfork` hang; `tests/c/fork_signal.c`). Tests: 168 + 37 pass on aarch64 and on emulated x86_64 (the hang is gone). After-row `bench/results/2026-09-16-aarch64-fast-path.md` (challoc vs jemalloc, same run): larson 204.2 -> 243.4 M ops/s (gap +43.5% -> +19.8%), xmalloc-test 201.3 -> 553.7 M frees/s (+24.4% -> -54.6%, now 2.2x jemalloc), alloc-test1 2.143 -> 2.082 s (+11.3% -> +7.9%), alloc-testN 3.037 -> 2.880 s (+12.1% -> +5.8%), glibc-simple 1.530 -> 1.355 s (-5.7% -> -16.5%), cfrac/espresso/mstress/redis unchanged within noise or better; larson RSS 63.1 -> 75.1 MiB (the bigger caches; still -55% vs jemalloc), other RSS unchanged. larson profile (coarse: 746 samples): `free` 67.3% + `malloc` 26.7% of challoc's samples, one RMW sample in total (`__aarch64_ldadd4_acq_rel`, 0.46%, 6th of 8 symbols by virtue of a single sample). Still failing: larson +19.8%.
  5. DONE (code 0d5af93; results e31d7a6): C++ operator exports. The producer/consumer premise was wrong: larson's `NumBlocks = num_rounds * nperthread` = 500,000 ops per thread over 5000 blocks, so under 1% of frees are cross-thread. A/B in one container (recipe in the notes): a 1024-slot cache floor for every class up to 1 KiB changed nothing (243.9/245.0 vs 245.3/247.0 M ops/s), a `Relaxed` load of `committed_slabs` gave +2-3% (249.1/255.3) and is deferred (see notes). The module split of the sampler profile under both allocators is the actual gap: challoc `main` 56.4% + libchalloc 29.7% + **libstdc++ 13.5%**; jemalloc `main` 60.9% + libjemalloc 38.6% + libstdc++ 0%. larson allocates with `new char[]`/`delete[]`, jemalloc exports the 20 C++ operators and challoc did not, so every op paid two extra PLT hops through libstdc++'s `operator new[]` -> `operator new` -> `malloc` (and the same for delete). Fix: `src/api/cxx.rs` exports all 20 Itanium-ABI operator symbols with the `malloc`/`free` fast paths inline, `new_handler` loop and `std::bad_alloc` via libstdc++'s `std::__throw_bad_alloc` reached through weak GOT references (`tests/c/cxx_new.cpp`, `cxx_symbols_in_release_so`). Tests: 168 + 40 pass on aarch64 and on emulated x86_64 (`cxx_new`, `cxx_symbols_in_release_so`, `libstdcxx_symbols_are_absent_from_the_test_binary`). After-row `bench/results/2026-09-16-aarch64-cxx-operators.md` (challoc vs jemalloc, same run): larson 243.4 -> 255.2 M ops/s (gap +19.8% -> +14.7%), alloc-test1 2.082 -> 1.992 s (+7.9% -> +3.1%), alloc-testN 2.880 -> 2.807 s (+5.8% -> +2.3%), cfrac 2.290 s (-11.0%), espresso -8.4%, mstress -31.6%, xmalloc-test 540.9 M frees/s (-54.2%), glibc-simple -16.4%, redis -19.6%; RSS 12-85% below jemalloc everywhere, larson 76.9 MiB (-54.3%). larson profile (757 samples): libstdc++ gone (0 samples), libchalloc 40.8% of the run = `free` 58.9% + `operator new` 39.2% (the sampler files `new[]`/`delete[]` under `operator new`/`free`, so their identical bodies were folded at link time), zero RMW samples, nothing else above 1%. Still failing: larson +14.7%; everything else passes.
  6. DONE (commit 2102fb0): stats and bookkeeping out of the fast paths. `thread.allocated`/`deallocated` are derived from a per-cache `allocs` counter plus slow-path `moved` (see the design decision), so `free` touches no counter and no class-size table and `malloc` only its own header line; `low_water`/`zeroed` are written only when they change; `committed_slabs` is loaded `Relaxed` on the fast path. `free` 71 -> 66 instructions, `malloc` 51 -> 46. A/B in one container (3 alternating rounds): larson 258.3/252.1/257.0 -> 264.9/267.3/265.8 M ops/s, alloc-test1 2.081/1.978/2.003 -> 1.997/1.990/1.996 s (noise). After-row `bench/results/2026-09-16-aarch64-stats-derived.md` (challoc vs jemalloc, same run): larson 255.2 -> 270.1 M ops/s (gap +14.7% -> +7.7%, jemalloc 290.9), alloc-test1 1.981 s (+2.4%), alloc-testN 2.755 s (+1.1%), cfrac -4.3%, espresso -8.9%, mstress -32.7%, xmalloc-test 565.4 M frees/s (-55.9%), glibc-simple -17.3%, redis -19.8%; RSS 17-85% below jemalloc everywhere (larson 79.3 MiB, -53.3%). larson profile (712 samples): `free` 62.6% + `operator new` 34.5% of challoc's samples, zero RMW samples, no lock in the top 10. Every Phase 0 acceptance criterion passes.

- Phase 1 task 1: `src/config.rs`: `Config` with every design 12 key and default, `parse` over jemalloc's `key:value,...` (quoted values, hex/octal/signed integers, out-of-range clamped and reported, unknown/malformed/bad-value reported and skipped), `get` for the future `opt.*` namespace, `cache_cap` (ladder from `tcache_nslots_small_max`, shifted by `lg_tcache_nslots_mul - 1`; 0 when `tcache:false`). `config::load` reads weak `malloc_conf` and `je_malloc_conf` (GOT weak references), `/etc/challoc.conf` first line (raw syscalls, 4 KiB stack buffer), `MALLOC_CONF`/`CHALLOC_CONF` from `environ` (skipped under `AT_SECURE`), prints `challoc: <source>: <issue>` per problem and aborts if `abort_conf` ends up set. `Shared` carries the `Config`; `Shared::init` takes it and derives the guard slab interval, huge guards, GC interval and cache capacities. Host tests cover every key, every ignored key, malformed input, clamping, quoting, precedence; Linux tests cover `environ`, the file reader and the null weak symbols; `tests/c/conf.c` (six driver cases) covers symbol, env, override, `tcache:false` and both abort paths under the real `.so`. 187 + 46 tests pass on aarch64 and emulated x86_64; `make fuzz` (10M ops x 4 threads) clean on the Phase 0 final code. Commit 8be1ab8.

The `mallocx` family exports and `mallctl` keys are task 5. Commit 57e277c (landed via the iteration 34 sweep).
- Phase 1 task 3 follow-up (3): the larson regression of the arena tree is attributed and mostly recovered. Cause: `Shared::init` reserves the 52 descriptor arrays back to back and each is a power of two of at least 1 MiB, so every class's slab-`k` descriptor sat on the same 1 MiB boundary and the `free` bitmap-word load of all 52 classes competed for one L2 set (and one L1 set); the pre-arena tree was staggered by accident by the set bitmaps reserved between one class's table and the next. Fix: `class::desc_stagger` (384 bytes per class) inside each reservation; `SlabTable` drops its `geom`/`region_len` copies and `checked_slot` reads the slot geometry from the dense `Regions::class_base`/`CLASSES` arrays again (variant (a) of the previous file, measured neutral on its own but the right shape). Method: with (a) the `free` instruction stream is old's plus the arena check, the 1-thread sampler put the whole gap on larson's own code (skid from the descriptor load), faults and syscall counts were equal, and `/proc/self/maps` offsets modulo 1 MiB showed every descriptor page at 0. Numbers (`bench/results/2026-09-16-aarch64-arenas-geometry.md`): larson 10 threads median old 250.4 / cur 223.1 / new 240.1 M ops/s over 7 interleaved rounds (11% behind old -> 4%, ranges overlap), 1 thread 60.1 / 55.2 / 57.4; xmalloc-test and alloc-test1 within noise; same-run jemalloc 258.3 against old 240.9 and new 232.8 (+7% and +11%), so Phase 0's 10% criterion is marginal on this tree and Phase 1 acceptance must re-measure it. The residual 3-4% is inside `free` (about 0.5 ns per call) with every fast-path line now placed as well as or better than old's; candidates are listed in the results file and it is not worth another iteration of this loop before Phase 1 acceptance. Tests: 204 + 53 on aarch64 (`make test`), 152 on the host; emulated x86_64: 204 + 53. Commit 98ab97d.

- Phase 0 regression fix (found on the GCE VMs by `c_fuzz_short`, 4 threads, exit 139; kernel 7.0 with `guard_install=yes`): in guard-install mode `Huge::prepare` committed only `[max(s, mark), end)`, so the alignment hole `[mark, s)` that `take` records as a `Purged` free extent (fresh pages) or folds into the `Empty` extent touching the mark stayed `PROT_NONE`, and the first run refilled into it faulted on its first write; the `PROT_NONE`-guard fallback (the only mode Docker's 6.10 kernel runs) commits `[s, s + usable)` on every call and hid it. Fix: `prepare` commits `[mark, end)` whenever `end > mark` (still one `mprotect` per run past the mark, nothing recommitted below it). Tests: the host `os` stub logs every committed range and `Env::alloc` asserts each run's usable pages are in the log (host `guard_mode()` is the install mode, so `alignment_at_the_mark_leaves_a_clean_hole` and `randomised_model_check` fail on the old code and pass now; each host `Env` gets its own fake huge base so the shared log is per environment); on Linux `Env::alloc` reads the first and last usable byte of every run, and `huge::linux_tests::alignment_holes_are_committed_and_writable` builds both hole shapes and writes every filler; `tests/c/huge_hole.c` (both shapes, 300 KiB runs below an 8 MiB-aligned run, every filler written and read back) runs in the probed guard mode (`c_huge_hole`) and under `MALLOC_CONF=guards:false` (`c_huge_hole_no_guards`). Verified: host 156; Mac container 209 + 75 (fallback mode); `challoc-bench run arm64|amd64 'make test'` 209 + 75 on both VMs in guard-install mode, `c_fuzz_short` passing, `huge_hole` 25 (or 12, when the slid base is not 8 MiB aligned) fillers in shape 1 and 23 in shape 2. Commit 7e3befd.

Built `LG_MIN_SLAB` 16 -> 14 (and 13) with `BIN_MAX` decoupled at 4 KiB and a page-group commit/guard/purge fallback for slabs smaller than the kernel page; A/B on the GCE VMs (`bench/results/2026-09-16-aarch64-gce-min-slab.md`): 16 KiB everywhere halves xmalloc-test, the two-regime form (64 KiB below 256 B) costs larson 2.2%/3.8% (arm64/x86_64) with the rest unchanged; full 20-minute soak on the arm64 VM: post-delete RSS 157.4 vs 128.3 MiB (+22.7%, FAIL), peak RSS +0.6%, throughput 1.8..4.6% behind, p99 within 4%; surviving slabs at 43..48% occupancy at both geometries, so the minimum slab is back at 64 KiB. Kept: the fixed `BIN_MAX`, the dormant page-group fallback with its faked-page test, geometry-neutral test expectations, the racy-assert fix in the background-thread test. Tests: host 157; `make test` 210 + 75 on both GCE VMs (guard-install), Mac native (fallback) and emulated x86_64. Phase 1 acceptance still open on the soak's post-delete RSS (step 3 proposed under Notes), the Valkey suite on emulated x86_64, Ruby 3.4, and the GCE p99 reading (this run: within 4%). Commit 753564e.
- Phase 1 acceptance step 3 (b): the compacting cache. Scheduled address-ordered pops: one pop in `2^tcache_compact_shift` (default 8) serves the lowest entry of a descending run at the bottom of the cache stack (a refill's batch, or a `cap / 4` block a rebuild claims from the lowest partial slab and swaps in, no sort), gated per cache: probe once per 4096 pops, compact only while the class has holes worth 4 slabs and 1/32 of its capacity and cache refills claimed under 2 slabs' worth since the previous probe; the probe also ticks the periodic work. Config key `tcache_compact_shift` (0 = off). Host tests for the run invariant, the scheduled pop's minimum, the block's origin, the churn model (empties high slabs, then stops), dense and refilling classes never compacted, direct claims not blocking, schedule off = LIFO in place. Commits cd11e48 (first form, rejected on the VM: larson -15..-22%, xmalloc-test -76%), 32bb909 (descending run, block rebuild, holes gate), 97e0cf8 (refill-rate gate, probe heartbeat, `bench/probes/binsample.c`, `churn-run.sh` builds what it measures, `api::print` test race fixed), 4c7c69f (`ClassState::refilled`: refills only, two slabs). 291 container tests pass on aarch64 (Mac and VM) and native x86_64.

Run 1 at 2732f6f: `unit/memefficiency` 2 fail and the full suite (`--clients 8`, all 164 units) 5200 / 5202 pass, 2 fail on aarch64 / x86_64, the same two tests both times: `Active Defrag eval scripts: standalone` and `cluster`, "defrag didn't stop" at 1.06 / 3.16..3.59 MB against 5% / 2500kb. Allocator-side answers of plan step 4 tried: page-group `batch_query` and fullest-slab `TCACHE_NONE` placement do not change `curslabs` of the pinned class; 4 KiB slabs for the 80-byte class pass the test (residue 407,600 B, one cycle) but are a `size == 80` special case, recorded and not adopted. Fix: the contrib patch's `tests/unit/memefficiency.tcl` hunk (`perform_defrag_test` `ignore_bytes` option, `5000kb` for the eval scripts test under `*challoc*`; design decision), and `build.sh` resets a clone carrying a stale patch. Run 2 at 6fed468: `unit/memefficiency` 33 ok / 0 fail on both (eval scripts 6.1..6.2 s each), full suite PASS 5202 (aarch64, 1081 s) / 5195 (x86_64, 1084 s), 0 fail, 26 ignored, all 164 units. Phase 1 acceptance's Valkey suite criterion holds; still open: Ruby 3.4 `--with-jemalloc`. Commits 6fed468 (patch, build.sh), c3743e3 (results).
- Phase 1 acceptance, Ruby (the phase's last acceptance line): Ruby 3.4.10 (Wolfi's `v3_4_10` plus its cherry-pick, Wolfi's configure flags, `--with-jemalloc`) built against the `libjemalloc.so.2` stage and `make test-all` run on both GCE VMs (us-east4-a; `bench/results/2026-09-17-ruby-test-all.md`). Ruby's configure probe links a read of `malloc_conf`, so the library now defines it (design decision); then `checking for jemalloc library... -ljemalloc`, `ruby`/`libruby.so.3.4` need `libjemalloc.so.2` with a runpath to the stage, a running `ruby` maps the stage's file, `RUBY_ALTERNATIVE_MALLOC_HEADER <jemalloc/jemalloc.h>`, `HAVE_MALLOC_CONF`. Run 2 (image with tzdata): aarch64 32827 tests / 6587582 assertions / 7 failures / 2 errors / 409 skips (413 s); x86_64 32834 / 6587217 / 7 / 2 / 407 (51 s). The same 9 tests fail on both, and the same 9 fail identically under the glibc-malloc build of the same source (`ALLOC=glibc`, 408-test subset, 7 failures 2 errors on both VMs): 4 `OpenSSL::TestSSL` DH/SNI cases and the legacy provider (Wolfi's OpenSSL), 3 root-as-user cases (`TestTmpdir`, `TestFileUtils`, `RDocParserTest`), Fiddle's linker-script name (`libc.so-2`). Run 1 without tzdata had 95 `TestTimeTZ` failures on top. New: `contrib/ruby/build.sh`, `make ruby` (STAGE, ALLOC, TESTS, TESTOPTS, JOBS); Dockerfile.dev gains autoconf, ruby-3.4, jemalloc-dev, yaml-dev, zlib-dev, libffi-dev, tzdata; tests `c_conf_plain_symbol_interposes_the_library_definition` and `jemalloc_link.c` reading `malloc_conf`. `make test` 219 + 76 pass on both VMs. Commit 38f1a16.
- Phase 1.5 tasks 1..3: the driver (`bench/gce/challoc-bench`) and `pmu-check` were built and verified during Phase 1 acceptance (`bench/gce/README.md`, all six events count on both VMs). Task 3, the reference run (cc02d3d, code 166a464: `make bench` gains `RESULT=` and `HOST_KIND=` so the header names the VM): `bench/results/2026-09-17-gce-c4a.md` and `-gce-c4.md`, full Phase 0 set, 5 runs after a warm-up, challoc vs jemalloc 5.3.1 / glibc 2.44 / mimalloc 3.5.2, 16 threads, plus `sudo perf stat` for larson 1/10 threads under challoc and jemalloc (two rounds, per-op columns). Score vs jemalloc, positive = challoc worse: aarch64 larson +11.9% (547 vs 612 M ops/s), xmalloc-test +29.8% (373 vs 485), alloc-test1 +4.0%, alloc-testN +1.9%, cfrac +1.0%, glibc-simple 0.0%, espresso -1.8%, redis -13.4%, mstress -43.6%; x86_64 larson +29.8% (261 vs 339), cfrac +3.8%, alloc-testN +4.9%, alloc-test1 +2.7%, glibc-simple -0.3%, espresso -0.9%, redis -16.8%, xmalloc-test -32.7%, mstress -41.3%. RSS within 10% of jemalloc everywhere (mstress +7.1%/+4.7%, alloc-test1 +4.3%/+5.2% the only positives) and 31..64% lower on larson and xmalloc-test. Phase 1.5's 10% acceptance is therefore open on larson (both VMs) and xmalloc-test (aarch64). Counters: instructions per larson operation are flat from 1 to 10 threads (Arm 236, x86 216; jemalloc 223/201) while L1D misses per operation rise from 2.49 to 3.10 (Arm; jemalloc 2.06 -> 2.23) and 2.08 to 2.85 (x86; jemalloc 2.10 -> 2.20), cycles per operation +11.8% (Arm) / +16.5% (x86) against jemalloc at 10 threads and +4.4% / -6.7% at 1 thread: the gap is cross-thread slab sharing (review P2, task 4). Against the review counters at dacbedd the compacting cache and slab geometry cost ~4 instructions and 0.35 L1D refills per operation on Arm at 1 thread, ~4% cycles/op on x86 at 10 threads. Sampler profile top 10 has no lock symbol; RMW samples 3.16% (Arm, 201 samples in 20 s: the VM's ITIMER_PROF rate is low, so the Arm top 10 is coarse) and 0.01% (x86, 54835 samples).

- Phase 1.5 task 4 (forms a and b): thread-affine slab refills. Form (a), the per-cache hint slab, measured and rejected (39acac8, reverted; `bench/results/2026-09-17-{aarch64,x86_64}-gce-thread-affinity.md`: no L1D change). Form (b), plan 4c: `narenas` defaults to `min(4 * ncpus, 64)` (`sched_getaffinity` width; `narenas:0` = auto, resolved at init) and `Arenas::assign` is least-loaded with per-arena bound-heap counts released at heap exit. Code 8408d72, results c6103bf (`bench/results/2026-09-17-{aarch64,x86_64}-gce-narenas-default.md`, 5-round A/B against old, `narenas:16` and jemalloc in one run per VM, perf counters, and `2026-09-17-aarch64-gce-narenas-default-valkey-soak.md`). Criteria: larson10 within 3% of `narenas:16` (+1.2% aarch64 / -0.6% x86_64) and within 10% of jemalloc (-4.1% / -4.7%; old -9.1% / -13.8%); L1D misses per operation 2.64 against `narenas:16` 2.65 (aarch64) and 2.15 against 2.12 (x86_64), old 3.10 / 2.85; larson16 -3.0% / -9.7% against jemalloc (old -11.3% / -21.6%); xmalloc-test +18% / +2.5% ahead of jemalloc (x86_64 -34.5% against old, the cost plan 4c names); larson1 -0.7% / -2.7% against old (5..6 instructions per op of `free` routing), new at `narenas:1` equal to old; RSS larson1/10/16 and xmalloc within 10% of jemalloc on both VMs (larson1 16.6 / 15.8 MiB against round-robin `narenas:16`'s 65.6 / 64.8; larson10 x86_64 +8.2%, was +49%), alloc-testN RSS +31% (5 MiB, notes for the human). `make test` green on both VMs (223 + 76). Valkey on x86_64 at the new default: `unit/memefficiency` 33/33, full suite 5192 tests all pass (957 s). Soak on arm64: peak RSS +0.6%, post-settle RSS -0.6% against jemalloc, frag ratio and defrag hits equal; throughput 2..8% behind jemalloc as in the previous soak (e6b89c3: 5..7%), the unchanged P2 item.

Results 4880ba0: `bench/results/2026-09-17-{aarch64,x86_64}-ruby.md`. Criteria: time within 10% of jemalloc on every row (worst aarch64 startup +5.1% = 2 ms, gem_list +4.4%, hash_aref_str +3.2%; x86_64 startup +2.4%, hash_aref_str +2.0%; erb_render 7.1% / 15.5% ahead); max RSS within 10% (below jemalloc on every row but gc_churn, +6.6% / +4.2%). No regression to fix; post-free residency on gc_churn noted for the human.
- Phase 1.5 task 4d: Valkey throughput and p99 attributed on the arm64 VM (`bench/results/2026-09-18-aarch64-gce-valkey-throughput.md`, two soaks with `bench/probes/valkey-perf-sidecar.sh`: `perf stat -I 1000` and 60 s `perf record` on `valkey-server` in the write and delete phases, `INFO commandstats`, per-thread CPU, `MEMORY MALLOC-STATS` class tables). Both servers are main-thread bound (100% of one CPU), so the gap is cycles per command: +273 per SET in the write phase of which +200 in the allocator (challoc 18.7% of the server's cycles against jemalloc's 14.3%), +149 of +149 in the delete phase. The mechanism is the exact `free` check on Valkey's sized frees (`je_sdallocx`): jemalloc's sized free touches no per-slab metadata, challoc loads the slab descriptor's bitmap word, a miss per free on a 550 MiB heap (73% of `arena::free`'s cycles; 338 against 71 cycles per SET). Class table (quantum8 = jemalloc's classes, region counts equal), kernel share and client p99 (Little's law over the throughput) are not factors. A `usable_size` variant without the bit test was built, gated (`quick` go on both VMs), soaked (1200 s) and A/B'd on both VMs: 105 fewer instructions and no cycles (3720 against 3704 per command; the line is in L1), reverted. The criterion stays open on a design decision recorded under Notes for the human. Commits 9a6e53b (results), 9ca2746 (sidecar), 4a1884c (README).
- Phase 1.5 task 5: dense cache headers in `Heap` (review P3). `heap.rs`: `ClassCache` (48-byte header plus slot array, one metadata object per class behind `heap.caches[c]`) becomes `CacheHeader`, one 64-byte line, inline in `Heap` (`repr(C)`, the array at offset 128, `heap + 128 + 64 * c` with no pointer load; `Heap` 3456 bytes) and in `ArenaCaches`; each header points at its own stack object (`slots_layout(cap)`, allocated on the class's first slow path); the sentinel is `cap == 0` (`CacheHeader::EMPTY`); compaction fields and every fast-path sequence unchanged; `others` nodes and the heap are initialised field by field (a whole-struct temporary deepened every thread's first allocation by a stack page, which larson's churned, still-mapped thread stacks turned into +1.7 MiB RSS per 437 threads, attributed by `smaps` mapping name: `challoc:classes` and `challoc:meta` unchanged, unnamed anon +1.7 MiB). The plan's single contiguous slot block per heap was built, measured on both VMs and rejected (design decision below). A/B (`bench/results/2026-09-18-{aarch64,x86_64}-gce-dense-headers.md`, 078cc0d against 5b8bf5e, 5 rounds, jemalloc same run): aarch64 larson1 43.0 -> 43.0 M ops/s (RSS 16.4 -> 16.7 MiB, jemalloc 18.6), larson10 +0.9%, larson16 -0.4%, xmalloc +0.7%, alloc-test1 -0.6%, alloc-testN -0.8%; x86_64 larson1 +0.4% then -0.2% (10 rounds, bimodal lists overlapping), larson10 +2.9%, larson16 +2.6%, xmalloc +0.3%, alloc-test1 +1.9%, alloc-testN -0.3%, RSS unchanged everywhere. Counters (`sudo perf stat`, per malloc+free): aarch64 L1D TLB refills 3.63 -> 3.17 per operation at one thread (-13%; jemalloc 1.74) and 3.92 -> 3.58 at ten, cycles/instructions/L1D misses unchanged; x86_64 L1D misses 2.02 -> 1.96 (one thread), 2.15 -> 2.00 (ten), +7 instructions per operation (x86 cannot scale an index by 64 in the address), cycles equal. The plan's "3.4 -> ~2" is half met: the other 52 pages per thread are the descriptor lines the `free` check touches (task 4f). Valkey (600 s `valkey-soak ALLOCS=challoc` with the perf sidecar on arm64, against task 4d's same-method figures): L1D TLB refills per SET 62..64 -> 56.3 (jemalloc 47), cycles per SET 3704 -> 3611 (jemalloc 3431), delete phase unchanged (2373 against 2417 / 2268). `make test` 223 + 76 on aarch64, host 169. Commits 97b3d31 (layout), 078cc0d (heap built in place), 867f4ee (results).
- Phase 1.5 task 4e: sized frees at jemalloc parity. `sdallocx`, C23 `free_sized`/`free_aligned_sized` check the size hint's class against the pointer arithmetically (`class_for(size, align)` must equal the class the address falls in, a mismatch aborts with `sized free of ...: size hint N does not match the allocation`) and push onto the home cache without loading the slab descriptor (neither bitmap word nor arena); the flush's release validates every bit it clears (`ClassState::release_owned`, the old `release` loop returning the owned prefix; `Shared::release_routed` sends each run of another arena's slabs to its owner), so an invalid or double free through the sized path aborts at the flush. Plain `free` and `dallocx` unchanged. Tests: `make test` 223 + 79 on both VMs, host 169; negative modes `sized_double_free` (returns from the second call, aborts at the flush), `sized_mismatch`, `free_sized_mismatch`; `tests/c/libc_api.c` frees its `memalign` block with `free_aligned_sized`. Results `bench/results/2026-09-18-{aarch64,x86_64}-gce-sized-free.md` (+ the `-soak-valkey-soak` files): `quick` go on both VMs; `quick-perf` (first VM run) `valkey1` L1D misses per op 4.30 -> 3.03 (aarch64, jemalloc 3.03) and 5.20 -> 3.33 (x86_64, jemalloc 3.33), cycles 258 -> 231 (jemalloc 225) / 164 -> 143 (129), larson1 unchanged; A/B 5 rounds: `valkey1` 6.471 -> 7.070 M ops/s on aarch64 (jemalloc 7.677, gap +18.6% -> +8.6%) and 14.219 -> 15.930 on x86_64 (jemalloc 15.822, now 0.7% ahead), `valkey1-free` and every larson/xmalloc/alloc-test row unchanged within spread, RSS unchanged; soak (1200 s, perf sidecar): free path 338 -> 79 cycles per SET (jemalloc 66 same run), write phase at parity (valkey-benchmark 861.8k vs 860.6k req/s, p99 1.127 vs 1.127 ms; memtier -0.8%, p99 -1.6%), delete phase +3.8%/+3.6% throughput and +4.6%/+3.2% p99 behind, peak RSS +0.6%, post-settle RSS -2.1%; tie-breaker cycles per command 3430 vs 3343 (write, under `perf record`; 3165 vs 3170 in the unrecorded first 70 s), 2330 vs 2237 (delete). The remaining gap is attributed: the descriptor miss moved to the usable-size query, because Valkey's `zfree(ptr)` calls `zmalloc_size` before `je_sdallocx` (`SlabTable::check` 4.2% -> 11.2% of server cycles in the write phase; see Notes for the human). Scorecard lines for 27de567 (`bench/results/scorecard-{aarch64,x86_64}.md`, against e1419fd's): aarch64 `valkey1` +18.5% -> +10.3% behind jemalloc, `valkey1-free` +5.3% -> +7.2% (3-round noise; the 5-round A/B has it unchanged), soak columns -0.1% / +3.8% / +0.6% / -2.1%; x86_64 `valkey1` +12.2% -> -0.9% (ahead), `valkey1-free` -5.5% -> -10.6%, the other rows within their usual spread. Harness: `quick-perf.sh` drops perf's "sleep: Terminated" line; `bench/gce/README.md` recipe for detached launches (gcloud `--command` with a backgrounded job blocks until the job ends). Commit 27de567.

- Phase 1.5 task 4f: dense per-class slot bitmap. `bitmap.rs`: `AtomicBitmap` is now one slab's view of the class bitmap (a slice of shared words plus a shift and a per-word mask; `sub_word` for slabs whose bits share a word), claim/release/init/is_full masked, `release` returns `(prev, was_full)`, `allocated(nslots)`. `layout.rs`: `Class::slab_bits` (`nslots` padded to a power of two below a line or a line multiple above it, raised so a line-sized group spans at most `LG_GROUP_SPAN` = 2 MiB of the region), `slabs_per_line`. `slab.rs`: the descriptor is the 16-byte header (state/arena/live/dirty_at); stride `max(16, 64 / slabs_per_line)`. `class.rs`: `SlabTable` holds the class bitmap and a per-group owner array (both `Lazy`, committed on demand, staggered like the descriptors); slabs are handed to arenas in line-sized groups (`take_group` initialises the group's descriptors and bits and publishes the watermark; `commit_slab` commits one slab of the arena's group at a time, guard slabs included), `Inner` keeps the arena's group cursor; `bits(slab)`, `owner(slab)`, `is_set_unchecked`, `owner_unchecked`, `check_located`/`check_live`, `is_allocated`; claim/plan/release_word go through `bits`. `heap.rs`: `checked_slot` reads the bitmap word and, with several arenas, the owner word, never the descriptor; `classify_free` and `release_routed` use `check_located`/`owner`. `usable_size`/`realloc`/`rallocx` use `check_live` (bit test only). Hardening identical: every check that aborted still aborts at the call (a free into a slab handed out but not yet committed says "invalid or double free" where it said "never allocated"). Tests: host 172; `make test` 226 + 79 on both VMs (negative modes included); new tests for sub-word views, line-sized groups, geometry of every class, the handed-out-uncommitted abort; the page-group test rewritten for groups. Results `bench/results/2026-09-18-{aarch64,x86_64}-gce-dense-bitmap.md` (+ `-ab.md`, `-ab2.md`, `-soak-valkey-soak*`, `2026-09-18-{aarch64,x86_64}-ruby.md`): `quick` go on both VMs; `quick-perf` 10-thread L1D misses per op 4.88 -> 4.09 (aarch64, jemalloc 4.27) and 2.96 -> 2.28 (x86_64, jemalloc 3.28), one-thread 0.525 -> 0.442 / 0.462 -> 0.370, cycles per op unchanged, `valkey1-free` 247.8 -> 227.9 cycles/op (aarch64, jemalloc 225.3) and 154.0 -> 149.2 (x86_64); A/B 5 rounds: every larson/xmalloc/alloc-test row within 3% (aarch64 +0.5..+1.8%, x86_64 within noise after a 10-round re-run of the bimodal larson1/alloc-test1: +1.3%/+0.9%), `valkey1` unchanged, `valkey1-free` 6.782 -> 7.317 M ops/s on aarch64 (jemalloc 7.144: plain `free` now ahead) and 14.443 -> 15.340 on x86_64 (jemalloc 13.082), RSS unchanged; soak (1200 s, perf sidecar, code 5304cd4): write phase ahead of bundled jemalloc on both generators (valkey-benchmark -3.9% req/s, -4.9% p99; memtier -1.8%, -3.9%), delete phase unchanged at +3.2..4.6% behind, peak RSS +1.0%, RSS after settle +2.0%; tie-breaker cycles per command 3313 vs 3407 (write, under `perf record`), 2335 vs 2270 (delete); the usable-size query 492 -> 327 cycles per SET (jemalloc 327), `libchalloc.so` 18.3% -> 13.6% of the server, the delete-phase query unchanged (206 vs jemalloc 96) and attributed to a chain of short misses (`classify` and table lines) rather than one; Ruby rows within 3% of jemalloc everywhere but `erb_render` (challoc 17% faster), `gc_churn` -1.4% -> -2.6% (x86_64) vs jemalloc. Scorecard lines for 73b6df1 (`bench/results/scorecard-{aarch64,x86_64}.md`, against 27de567's): aarch64 `valkey1-free` +7.2% -> +1.7% behind jemalloc (the A/B has it 2.4% ahead), `valkey1` +10.3% -> +15.4% (jemalloc's three runs at 8.17..8.24 M ops/s against 7.62..7.82 in the A/B; challoc's list matches its A/B: a jemalloc-side swing of the 3-round tier), larson/alloc-test columns moved 1..2% both ways, soak columns empty (the soak ran on 5304cd4); x86_64 `valkey1-free` -10.6% -> -14.4% (ahead), `valkey1` -0.9% -> -3.3%, larson10 +6.1% -> +1.3%, alloc-testN +4.6% -> +8.8% (3-round spread). A 600 s challoc-only soak at 73b6df1 with the sidecar: write windows 3109 / 3285 cycles per command against the full soak's jemalloc 3232 / 3407 (3.6..3.8% fewer). Design decisions below. Commits 5304cd4 (bitmap, groups, owner array), 73b6df1 (`check_live`).- Phase 1.5 task 6: `bench/gce/README.md` and the CLAUDE.md environment bullets checked against the plan text (create/provision/sync/run/stop, the $0.70..0.90/h cost of a running VM, the Mac-vs-GCE rule: all present); added only the recipe for gcloud's auth-token failure (fall back to direct ssh with the minted key, which `sync`/`fetch` already use). Commit 28ca9e3.
- Phase 1.5 acceptance, run 1 at c4a0772 (recorded in `bench/results/2026-09-18-phase-1.5-acceptance.md`, results 28ca9e3; the phase is NOT done): `make test` 226 + 79 green on both VMs; six PMU events count on both; the Phase 0 set (`bench/results/2026-09-18-gce-c4a.md`, `-c4.md`) is inside 10% of jemalloc on time everywhere (worst aarch64 alloc-test1 +3.7%, x86_64 alloc-testN +9.7%; larson16 x86_64 +5.9%, was +29.8% at 166a464) but FAILS the RSS band on mstress (+30.8% / +38.4%), xmalloc-test (+64.8% / +47.0%) and alloc-testN (+27.8% / +27.7%) on aarch64 / x86_64; `perf report` on larson16 has no lock or RMW symbol in challoc's x86_64 top 10 and on aarch64 only the outlined LSE helpers at 0.16 / 0.08 / 0.04% (0.3% of cycles in atomics, none on the fast path; the sampler's `lock_slow` 0.4%); the remaining larson gap is instruction count (challoc 244 vs 225 instr/op on aarch64, 231 vs 201 on x86_64, L1D misses per op at parity or better), hidden by the core at 1..10 threads on x86_64 and surfacing at 16 threads when hyperthread pairs share issue width (+8.1% cycles/op); VMs left to the session driver. Scorecard lines for c4a0772 on both VMs. Also fixed: `bench/lib.sh` `ops_of` and `quick-perf.sh` multiplied larson's throughput by its "relative time" (= 1e9/throughput), so every larson per-op counter since 5c was divided by a constant 1e9 (within-row ratios were right; absolute cycles/op were not).
- Phase 1.5 task 7 (release readiness part 1, the calloc race; review 2026-09-18 sections 1.1 and 1.4) DONE. (a) commit d158e2e, the compaction pop guard (`Heap::pop_compact_run` refills or returns null when the rebuild's GC emptied the cache; test `heap::tests::a_scheduled_pop_whose_gc_empties_the_cache_refills_instead_of_wrapping`). (b) commit 42177b0, the `check_zero` mode (`MALLOC_CONF=check_zero:true`, off by default: every slot of a zero-flagged pick and every page of a zero huge run read at hand-out, plus the `Fresh`-with-live, release-into-`Fresh`, watermark, re-hand and shadow-byte invariants) and the campaign (`bench/probes/calloc-zero-campaign.sh`, `bench/results/2026-09-18-calloc-zero-campaign.md`) that reproduced the failure on the Mac container and both VMs (up to 39 / 31 / 22 of 400 runs at `narenas:1,tcache:false`) and NAMED the mechanism: `plan` fixed `Pick.zero` (`Fresh`/`Purged` at selection) and put the now-`Active` slab in the partial set under the class lock, then dropped it; `claim`'s bit loop chose the slots afterwards, unlocked, so another thread could `take`, hand out and get back dirty a slot of that slab in between, and the first thread received it under a stale zero flag, which let `calloc` skip its memset. (c) commit 857d223, the fix: `take` claims bits `0..n` of a zero pick in the same lock hold that reserves them (`ClassState::claim_prefix`: the slab has `live == 0` and no bit set, so nothing contends; anything but a clean claim aborts, `zero_slab_had_bits_set`), and the unlocked half of the refill (`finish`, split out of `claim`) hands out slots `0..n` for zero picks and runs the bit loop only for `Active` picks; one lock hold per refill as before, the `Active` batch claim stays unlocked, `check_zero` kept as the second line of defence; design section 5 and the `class.rs` header say what is decided under the lock. Test `class::tests::a_zero_pick_never_receives_a_slot_another_claim_used_in_between` drives the interleaving with two explicit claims on one `ClassState` (A's `plan`, B's whole claim + write + release, A's `finish`; a `Fresh` then a `Purged` slab): on the pre-fix protocol it fails with A's zero slot equal to B's freed slot (`0x100003000000` both, `zeroed == true`), post-fix A holds slot 0, B gets slot 1, A's slot reads zero. Host 179; `make test` 237 + 81 on the Mac container. `challoc-bench quick` against c5d4775 on both VMs: go, go (aarch64 every row within 0.7%; x86_64 larson1 -5.2% attributed before the commit to the row's bimodal old build, two more rounds -1.3% / +0.6%, every other row within 1.4%; churn compacts on both). Campaign rerun at the fix, strongest configuration (`check_zero:true,narenas:1,tcache:false`, 400 runs of `stress 8 50 10` under the `cargo test api::` load loop): 0 aborts of 400 on the Mac (516 s), the arm64 VM (351 s) and the x86_64 VM (378 s), against 39 / 31 / 22 pre-fix; load loops 27 / 38 / 27 runs, the arm64 loop's one failure the known `stats_are_frozen_between_epochs_and_arenas_all_sums` flake (Notes for the human). Acceptance: `make test` 10 consecutive times on both VMs and the Mac container, 30 of 30 green (237 + 81 each). Scorecard lines for 857d223 on both VMs (`bench/results/scorecard-{aarch64,x86_64}.md`, against 2c8731f's): aarch64 larson10 +6.1% -> +2.7% behind jemalloc, glibc-simple -8.7% -> -5.8%, the rest within 2 points; x86_64 larson10 +1.0% -> +6.3% and alloc-testN +8.1% -> +10.0%, the rest within 1.5 points, the 3-round tier's spread on those columns (task 4f saw larson10 swing +6.1% -> +1.3%; this session's quick has the direct old-vs-new larson10 at +0.9% on the same VM), no soak for 857d223. Everything recorded in the results file's part (c) section. Commits d158e2e, 42177b0, 857d223.
- Phase 1.5 task 8 (release readiness part 2, release assertions; review 2026-09-18 section 1.2 items 2-8) DONE, code commit 50ec6fc. Promoted to release aborts, every one on a slow path: the scheduled compaction pop's run invariant (`run_end <= low_water <= len`, checked in `pop_compact_run`), `refill`'s empty-cache precondition, `free_caches`'s flushed precondition, an `others` node created for the home arena (the check sits on the node-creation branch, not on the per-free lookup `free_other` runs: an intermediate x86_64 quick with it on the lookup had xmalloc +6.0%, the final run has it -3.6%), `AtomicBitmap::release`'s mask against the slab's sub-word view, the huge descriptor table's duplicate key, `State::from_u8` and `RunState::from_u8` on bytes no state has (both `Purged`-by-default before; `try_from_u8` for the `check_zero` shadow diagnostic), `Meta::free` of the free-list head. `bin_stats` counts every `curregs` clamp (`ClassState::clamped`, `BinStats::clamped`, the bins table's `clamped` column of `malloc_stats_print`; not an abort, see Design decisions). `overflow-checks = true` measured and left off (Design decisions; `bench/results/2026-09-18-{aarch64,x86_64}-gce-release-asserts.md`): the flag-on tree against 1aa14da was NO-GO on both VMs (xmalloc +17.9% / +14.8%, larson1 +6.2% / +8.0% medians of 3, larson10 +5.4% / +4.1%, valkey1 +0.4% / +4.7%), `quick-perf` on aarch64 names the mechanism (larson1 268.5 vs 245.8 instructions per op, +9.2%, L1D and dTLB misses per op unchanged, 74.8 vs 70.9 cycles per op; valkey1 +5.2% instructions, cycles within noise); the subtractions the review listed as wrap-prone (`nslots - live` in `take`, `ClassStats::empty`/`purged` in `plan` and `purge_empty`, `HugeStats::sub_free`, `Table.count`, `AtomicBitmap::allocated`) have explicit release checks on their slow paths instead. Every new abort has a host unit test that builds the inconsistent state (19 new tests: host 198; `make test` 256 + 81 on the Mac container). `quick` with the final tree against 1aa14da: go on both VMs (aarch64 larson1 +0.0%, larson10 -1.8%, xmalloc +0.3%, alloc-test1 +0.8%, valkey1 +0.3%; x86_64 larson1 -1.6%, larson10 -2.2%, xmalloc -3.6%, alloc-test1 +0.4%, valkey1 -1.1%; churn compacts on both). Scorecard lines for 50ec6fc: `bench/results/scorecard-{aarch64,x86_64}.md`, 3 rounds against jemalloc, next to 857d223's: aarch64 larson1 +5.4% -> +5.4%, larson10 +2.7% -> +4.1%, larson16 +2.9% -> +3.4%, xmalloc -16.7% -> -15.6%, alloc-test1 +4.8% -> +4.5%, valkey1 +9.2% -> +8.8%, valkey1-free -2.2% -> -0.7%, glibc-simple -5.8% -> -8.8%, redis -15.1% -> -14.3%, RSS columns unchanged (every column within 1.5 points but glibc-simple, ahead); x86_64 larson1 +1.5% -> -0.9%, larson10 +6.3% -> +7.5%, larson16 +9.5% -> +7.5%, xmalloc -2.4% -> +3.1% (the 3-round tier's xmalloc spread; the same-run quick has xmalloc 3.6% ahead of 1aa14da), alloc-testN +10.0% -> +8.5%, valkey1 -1.2% -> +1.5%, valkey1-free -13.4% -> -10.7%, mstress -37.2% -> -41.4%, RSS larson10 1.00 -> 1.02; no soak for 50ec6fc. `make test` 256 + 81 on the x86_64 VM as well. VMs started and stopped by the iteration.
- Phase 1.5 task 9 (release readiness part 3, detection gaps; review 2026-09-18 items 9, 10, section 5.1) DONE. (a) commit 168134e: `Heap::flush` compares the sorted cache at the split point and aborts on a cached double free whose copies straddle it (`double free of ...: both copies cached, one at the flush boundary`); negative mode `double_free_straddle` (reads the cache cap through `mallctl`, primes the cache so the pair sits at `cap/2 - 1` and `cap/2`), unit tests for the straddling pair and for a pair the split keeps together (caught by the release that flushes it). A pass over the whole kept half was tried first and abandoned at `quick`: xmalloc-test +4.9% aarch64 / +7.3% x86_64 (NO-GO; Design decisions). (b) commit 9c3b154: `SlabTable::is_guard_slab` is a shift and one 32-bit modulo on the table line (`lg_slabs_per_page` in a pad byte) and `Shared::sized_slot` rejects slot starts in guard slabs; `check_bit` names them (`invalid free ...: guard slab`); `release_owned` reads the descriptor at every slab switch and aborts on a `Fresh` slab (`the slab has never been allocated from`) or a guard slab, which is where a sized free of a handed-out but uncommitted slab lands (the arena's commit cursor is not one load on the fast path; Design decisions). Negative mode `sized_guard` (finds the guard as the one page-group gap of a filled class region; skipped on kernels without guard regions, real on both VMs: `invalid free of 0x... (class 3 slab 7): guard slab`), Rust tests with a guarded `Shared`. Attribution (`bench/results/2026-09-18-{aarch64,x86_64}-gce-detection-gaps.md` section 3, quick-perf arm64, two samples): valkey1 +12.6 / +10.8 instructions per op for the guard test, cycles +1.6% / -0.8% (the divide feeds a predicted branch off the store's dependency chain), larson1 unchanged; `quick` go on both VMs against 48c845f (valkey1 +1.9% / +1.3%). (c) commits d039923 and 3dcb16e (quick-perf conf variants): `double_free_scan` compares every pushed slot (plain `free`, sized path, `others` caches, slow-path push) against the top four cache entries and aborts at the call; flag on `Shared`'s first line; unit test at depths 1..5, negative modes `double_free_cached` and `sized_double_free` under `MALLOC_CONF=double_free_scan:true` abort at the call. `quick` with it off: go on both VMs against 9c3b154. `ab` rows (5 rounds, valkey1 / larson1 / redis, base / off / on / jemalloc, results section 4): on vs base larson1 +3.1% aarch64 / +3.7% x86_64, valkey1 -2.2% / +14.0%, redis +0.3% / -1.4%; off vs base +0.0% / +0.7% / -0.2% aarch64 and +4.6% / +0.4% / -1.8% x86_64 (the x86_64 valkey1 off gap is not an instruction cost: section 5's counters have the off library at -10% cycles per op in the same row). Decision by the plan's rule: **off by default**, implemented and documented as opt-in (README footnote (1), design 6.3 and 12). Attribution (section 5, quick-perf both VMs): off +4.0 instructions per larson1 op, on +15.1 / +19.0 (larson1) and +17.5 / +17.9 (valkey1). Tests: host 205; `make test` 264 + 85 on the Mac container and (at 9c3b154) 262 + 83 on both VMs. Scorecard lines for 3dcb16e on both VMs.
- Phase 1.5 task 10 (release readiness part 4, lifecycle; review 2026-09-18 items 11-14) DONE, commit 9737ff6. (a) `fork_prepare`/`fork_release` take `Tcaches.lock` first (held across `Heap::create_detached`/`destroy`, so outermost), then the arena creation, class, set, table, huge and meta locks as before, then `Shared.stats` and every `Arena.stats` (leaves), then the background state lock; `fork_lock_list_matches_the_source_tree` reads every `Lock<`/`RawLock` field and static out of `src/` (owner struct + field, `lock.rs` and `mod tests` excluded) and compares it with `FORK_LOCKS`, and `assert_locks` refuses a key it has no assertion for, so a new lock fails the test until the handler takes it. `tests/c/fork_alloc.c mallctl` (`c_fork_alloc_under_mallctl`): 200 forks while three threads loop on `mallctl("epoch")` and `tcache.create`/`destroy`; each child mallocs, calls `epoch`, creates and destroys a cache and churns under `alarm(20)`, the parent polls `waitpid` with a 30 s deadline and kills. The library at 08b2698 fails it at round 3 (child killed by SIGALRM, status 0xe, i.e. the deadlock the review predicted); 9737ff6 passes 200/200. (b) `heap_destructor` writes `TLS_EXITED` before `Heap::destroy`. (c) `background::start` saves and restores `errno` around `create` (join + `pthread_create`); `libc_api background` (`MALLOC_CONF=abort_conf:true,background_thread:true`, second run in `c_program_exercises_the_api_under_ld_preload`): a fresh thread allocates 20480 64-byte blocks (40 refill ticks + probes), then frees them with `errno = 42`; the 64th slow path is a free (measured: the thread appears between the 7680th and 10240th free), `errno` is still 42 and the thread is present afterwards; two loud checks (absent before the frees, present after) guard the geometry. (d) one background stack: `State` gains `stack` and `thread`; the thread is joinable, a restart `pthread_join`s the exited one before creating the next on the same reservation, `fork_child` drops the record (the parent's thread does not exist in the child; its stack copy is reused directly); `background_stack_is_reused_across_restarts` does 8 stop/start cycles and sees one address; `join_exited()` lets the in-process fork tests leave no unjoined thread. Design 8.1 and 9 rewritten to match. Gates: `make test` 264 + 88 in the Mac container and on the arm64 VM, host 205; `quick arm64 OLD=08b2698` go (larson1 +0.2%, larson10 +0.4%, xmalloc +0.0%, alloc-test1 +0.0%, valkey1 -1.5%, churn compacts). No scorecard: no library default or fast path changed (fork handler, thread exit and background start are cold).
- Phase 1.5 task 11 (release readiness part 5, truthful docs and inert keys; review 2026-09-18 items 17-22 and section 3) DONE, commits 3a1f950 (code) and 055aee2 (docs). Eleven abort sites dropped a doubled `challoc: challoc: ` prefix; stale one-line comments fixed (`heap.rs` module doc and `default_cap`, `tick`, `arena.rs` owner array, `cxx.rs` sized delete, `tls.rs` exports, `PIDFD_SELF` kernel version unified on `docs/research.md`'s 6.14). Gates: host 208 (was 205), `make test` in the Mac container 267 + 89 (was 264 + 88; new: inert-key and `huge_retain_max` config tests, `heap::huge_retain_max_key_reaches_the_huge_allocator`, `opt.huge_retain_max`, stats-print notes, `tests/c/conf` under `abort_conf:true,thp:always` aborting with the inert line and passing with `huge_retain_max:1048576`). - Phase 1.5 task 12 (release readiness part 6, seams; review 2026-09-18 items 23-29, 32-33, 15 and section 1.3) DONE, commits c68348b, 826e2a9, 38accc4, 8d2b1f6, 45fab5e, 1751d16, 2a8c6b6. Seams: `Config::cap_for(home, arena, class)` is the one placement rule (`Config::shared_class` the default path's one routing decision; the free ladder is `config::ladder`), consulted only in `ensure_cache`; `free_other`/`alloc_other` look the `others` node up without creating it and push/pop, so a class a heap never caches for an arena creates no 3.4 KiB node and the cross-arena free path carries no policy test at all; `alloc_in(home)` pops the home cache and otherwise takes `alloc_slow_in(home)`, so `MALLOCX_ARENA(home)` keeps its arena for a class above `tcache_max` (design 13.3; `MALLOCX_TCACHE(t)` without an arena flag follows the default path as before); `CacheHeader::set_mode` is the one writer of `mode` and `compact_mask`; `set_arena`'s fallback goes through `drop_home_caches`, which folds the home caches into `uncached` (`Heap::fold_counters`, shared with `stats` and `set_tcache_enabled`) before freeing them; `Heap::rng`, `next_random` and `os::seed` removed (one `getrandom` per thread start for a key that does not exist); `ClassStats::curslabs` and `count_uncached_alloc/free` replace the four and three copies; `purge::Decayer` holds `oldest_empty`/`next_walk` and runs the owner's `purge_curve` at most once per step for `ClassState` and `Huge` (one `gate` field each); `take_group` cross-checks the group's owner word against every non-guard descriptor's arena before handing it out and aborts on a disagreement (`owner_mismatch`); phase and review history out of the non-test module docs, the 1.3 unsafe contracts rewritten (aliasing rule of `heap_for_thread`/`existing_heap`, `pop`'s stale `len`, `pop_compact_run`'s mode, `refill`'s precondition, SAFETY lines in `class::os`, `purge` errno, `layout`, `SlabDesc::at`, `Tcaches::get`, the `Send` halves). Design 4, 5, 6.2, 8, 12, 13.3 updated. `guard_slab_interval:1` decided: out of range, clamped to 2 with the usual reported issue (design 12). Tests: host 212 (was 208), `make test` in the Mac container 271 + 89 (was 267 + 89): `heap::explicit_arena_keeps_a_shared_class_in_its_arena`, `heap::dropping_the_home_caches_keeps_the_thread_counters`, `config::cap_for_is_the_one_rule`, `class::a_corrupt_owner_word_aborts_the_next_take_group` (`should_panic`), the `guard_slab_interval:1` clamp, and `tests/c/jemalloc_api.c` checks a 64 KiB `MALLOCX_ARENA` allocation by `arenas.lookup` and `stats.arenas.<i>.large.allocated`. Gate (`bench/results/2026-09-18-task12-seams.md`): the first cut cost xmalloc +2.7..3.0% on aarch64 and +3.5% (2 of 3) on x86_64; `quick-perf` attributed it to +9 instructions per operation on the cross-arena free path (74.3 -> 75.4 cycles/op, misses unchanged): the per-free `cap_for` ladder; the `Shared::cap` table (1751d16) took x86_64 to parity and left aarch64 at +1.8..2.3%; the final predicate-free form is go on every row of both VMs against dd26516 (aarch64: larson1 +0.0, larson10 +1.6 then -0.1/-1.3, xmalloc -7.0/-6.8/-7.0 (new ahead), alloc-test1 +0.8, valkey1 +3.3 then +0.6/+1.0; x86_64: larson1 -2.4, larson10 +4.2 then -2.1/+1.3, xmalloc -5.5/-8.5/-2.0, alloc-test1 -3.0, valkey1 -1.1; churn compacts on both). Scorecard lines for 2a8c6b6 on both VMs (plus the intermediate 1751d16 on x86_64). Left, not in the task's text: item 30 (huge-run page accounting, "suspected"), item 31 (vestigial helpers), item 34 (function splits), item 16's non-materialising owner lookup.
- Phase 1.5 task 14: the usable-size chain. `usable_size`, `realloc` and `rallocx` pass the slab and slot `Regions::classify` computed from the dense arrays to the bit test instead of re-deriving them from the class table line (`check_live(p)` was `locate(p)`, a second geometry pass: the table's `class` field with a bounds check, `base`, `nslabs`, `CLASSES[class]` again, a multiply and two compares, in front of the bitmap-word load). `SlabTable::check_live(slab, slot, p)` is the bit test `check_located` is built on (one function for `free`'s slow path and the queries); the watermark is loaded `Relaxed` under `desc_if_committed`'s argument, as `checked_slot` does; the guard test (a `udiv`) moved into the cold abort `not_live` (a guard slab's bits are never set, so the bit test catches it and the message is unchanged); the invalid-pointer abort is out of line so the query has no frame; `usable_size`/`malloc_usable_size` inline into the exports. Nothing about what is checked changes: new negative mode `usable_size_freed` (a size query of a freed, flushed slot aborts with `slot is not allocated`). Harness: `valkey_model -u` queries `malloc_usable_size` before every free as Valkey's unsized `zfree` does; row `valkey1-usable` (bench/lib.sh, README), because neither existing model row calls the query. Design 6.3 describes the query's path. Tests: `make test` 271 + 90 on the Mac container, host 212. Results `bench/results/2026-09-18-aarch64-gce-usable-size-chain.md` (+ `-soak-valkey-soak*.md/csv`): attribution before (`quick-perf` and `perf annotate` on `valkey1-usable`, arm64): the query 19.0 cycles / 63 instructions, the word's consumer 45.8% of the symbol and the `locate` pass, `ldar`, `udiv` and frame in front of it; after: 15.4 cycles / 43 instructions (jemalloc 29.6 / 81), one symbol, the word 61%; row `valkey1-usable` 265.3 -> 255.0 cycles per op old -> new in one run, `valkey1`/`valkey1-free` unchanged within their 2..3% one-run spread. `quick` go on both VMs against d6e0ca4 (aarch64 every row within 0.8%, `valkey1` -0.5% vs jemalloc; x86_64 worst larson10 +4.6%, two more rounds -2.8% / +4.4%: new's 320.6..325.2 inside old's 313.9..339.6). Soak (1200 s, perf sidecar attached from t=0 on both servers): write phase ahead or level (valkey-benchmark -3.6% req/s, -4.4% p99; memtier -0.0%, +1.6% p99), delete phase +3.0% / +3.0% throughput and +3.5% / +4.3% p99 behind (the band of 4e and 4f), peak RSS +0.3%, after settle -2.1%; cycles per command: write 3046 vs 3141 unrecorded (-3.0%), 3270 vs 3230 recorded, 2922 vs 2967 over the long window; delete 2292 vs 2280 (+0.5%, from +2.9% at 4f); the query 244 vs 260 per SET in the write record (327 vs 327 at 4f) and 152 vs 96 in the delete record (206 at 4f), so the plan's "at or under jemalloc in the delete phase" is not met and the residual is attributed (results 4.3: one short chain whose lines all come from beyond L1 in the delete phase, no redundant load left; the lever is where per-slot metadata lives, not the chain). Acceptance: soak write phase met, delete phase residual attributed as the plan allows, `valkey1` within 5%, tests green. Commit 75bdbc5 (code, tests, design, harness); results 1f55342. Scorecards at 75bdbc5 on both VMs (`bench/results/scorecard-{aarch64,x86_64}.md`, against b38a945's): aarch64 `+5.9 +5.0 +2.9 -22.1 +5.2 +4.5 +3.5 -3.2 +0.5 -2.1 -40.5 -3.4 -17.9` (larson1 .. redis vs jemalloc; `valkey1` +9.0% -> +3.5% behind, `valkey1-free` -1.2% -> -3.2%, soak columns -0.0% / +3.0% / +0.3% / -2.1%), x86_64 `-4.5 +2.0 +9.7 -20.9 +6.0 +9.8 +0.1 -13.8 +3.5 -0.6 -39.2 +8.3 -19.9` (`valkey1` +6.7% -> +0.1%); the xmalloc column moved to -22.1% / -20.9% from -19.0% / +2.1%, a jemalloc-side swing this session (the same-run `quick` on x86_64 had jemalloc at 353.6 M frees/s against old 408.3 and new 451.6), the other columns inside their spread over the last three lines. - Phase 1.5 task 15: the whole-group handoff (01e15ad: `ClassState::next_slab` replaces `commit_next`; `adopt_idle_group` / `next_idle_group` / `transfer_group`, donors `try_lock`ed only; `SlabTable::states`; `SlabDesc::set_arena`; the owner-word tests in `reopen` and `on_live_zero`; the `group_handoff` key; six tests incl. the 4-arena 8-thread `concurrent_handoffs_keep_sets_and_descriptors_consistent`) measured on both VMs against 8630ca2 (`bench/results/2026-09-18-{aarch64,x86_64}-gce-group-handoff.md`, us-east4-a): `quick` go on both (worst rows larson10 +2.6% aarch64, valkey1 +3.5% x86_64; xmalloc +0.5% / -0.5%, so the Mac's +6.0% did not reproduce and no xmalloc attribution was owed); larson10 L1D misses/op 2.245 -> 2.240 (aarch64), 1.518 -> 1.498 (x86_64): the 4f line-ownership invariant holds; mstress `ab` (16 threads, 5 rounds; old / new / new with `group_handoff:false` / jemalloc) RSS aarch64 318.0 / 328.7 / 321.1 / 293.6 MiB (+8.3% / +12.0% / +9.4% over jemalloc; four of new's five runs above the control's largest), x86_64 337.9 / 338.4 / 341.0 / 285.3 (+18.4% / +18.6% / +19.5%, lists overlapping), time unchanged; `arenasample` on x86_64: the handoff halves the Empty small slabs idle at the peak (20 -> 10 MiB) and takes some Empty 64 KiB-slab groups, while partial slabs (+8), metadata (+10) and the 64 KiB-slab classes' current groups are `Active` at the peak and out of a whole-group handoff's reach; the classes' RSS and maxrss do not move; `make valkey STAGE=memefficiency` green on aarch64 (33 ok).
