- Phase 1.5 acceptance, RSS remediation history (moved from In progress when the phase closed; the record of runs 1..3) (the failed criterion; nothing relaxed). Attribution done (iteration 27, `bench/results/2026-09-18-{aarch64,x86_64}-gce-arena-rss.md`): the narenas A/B (4/8/16/default, 3 rounds, both VMs) prices the arena count (n4: larson10 +4.8..7.5% and larson16 +5.1..10.8% behind the default, xmalloc faster; n8: mstress +22..31% RSS, alloc-testN +13..15%), no count passes every row on both VMs; the `bench/probes/arenasample.c` sidecar (malloc_stats_print per second plus /proc/self/smaps by mapping name) splits the RSS: xmalloc-test's 70 MiB is 8 cached 40 KiB batch structs per (consumer, producer arena) pair (16 x 16 x 8 x 40 KiB) plus 40 MiB of Empty slabs waiting for decay, mstress's 190 MiB the 8 cached 4..32 KiB slots per (thread, arena, class), alloc-testN's 2.7 MiB is metadata pages (one page-aligned set table per (arena, class, set), 2..4 touched pages per (arena, class) at 17 arenas; the class regions are equal at 1 and 17 arenas). Three slow-path changes, no fast-path line touched: `tcache_max` (32 KiB, jemalloc's) so classes above it are uncached; foreign-arena (`others`) caches hold nothing above `BIN_MAX`; one chunked, interleaved set table per (arena, slab size) so a small heap touches one page per slab size an arena uses. Plus two found on the way: the `others` nodes are staggered by a cache line each (the first tree lost xmalloc -w 16 15..25% to L1 set aliasing of the node heads once the 40 KiB foreign stack no longer sat between them; `2026-09-18-aarch64-gce-xmalloc-isolation.md`), and classes above `tcache_max` are allocated from arena 0 on the default path (their 1..4 MiB partial slabs per arena were mstress's next term). Code commit d10fb6d. A/B at d10fb6d against 523ad84 and jemalloc (`-arena-rss-fix2.md`, 3 rounds): every throughput row within noise of old on both VMs; RSS xmalloc -w 16 179 -> 107 MiB (jemalloc 111) and 182 -> 110 (118), alloc-testN 20.9 -> 16.3 (16.5) and 21.7 -> 17.1 (16.7), mstress 375 -> 319 (293, +8.9%) on aarch64 and 395 -> 338 (285, +18.5%) on x86_64. The x86_64 mstress residual is attributed (`2026-09-18-x86_64-gce-arena-rss.md`): small-class Empty slabs waiting out the whole flat `dirty_decay_ms` window in every arena (+29 MiB; design section 8 is a flat window where jemalloc's curve purges progressively), one partial small slab per class per arena (+15 MiB), foreign small caches' stacks (+9 MiB of metadata). Acceptance run 2 at d10fb6d (`bench/results/2026-09-18-phase-1.5-acceptance-2.md`, protocol files `2026-09-18-gce-c4a-2.md` / `-c4-2.md`): time worst +4.3% / +7.6%, RSS xmalloc-test +3.8% / -11.2%, alloc-testN +0.3% / +1.6%, larson10 L1D misses/op equal to the previous default's (2.235 vs 2.244; 1.567 vs 1.558); mstress RSS +10.01% on aarch64 and +21.06% on x86_64, so the phase still FAILS on that one row and stays in progress. Next: the decay question in Notes for the human (progressive purge of Empty slabs against the flat window), then rerun the mstress row and re-record; the Valkey memefficiency stage and the soak have not been rerun since the cache changes and are due before the phase can be called done. Iteration 28 (2026-09-18): the driver's decision (a) is landed as 2c8731f, progressive decay along jemalloc's smoothstep curve (`purge::Curve`, `WEIGHTS`, `ClassState::purge_curve`, `Huge::purge_curve`; design section 8 rewritten to match): `make test` 231 + 80 on both VMs, host 177, `quick` go on both, `tests/c/decay.c` `curve` mode shows 16 of 48 MiB back at mid-window and all at the end. Acceptance run 3 at 2c8731f (`bench/results/2026-09-18-phase-1.5-acceptance-3.md`, protocol files `2026-09-18-gce-c4a-3.md` / `-c4-3.md`): every time row inside (worst +4.4% / +9.0%), RSS xmalloc-test -5.6% / -13.5%, alloc-testN +1.4% / +1.3%, mstress +9.8% on aarch64 and **+19.1% on x86_64**, so the phase still FAILS on that row, unchanged from run 2 (+21.1%). The decay change could not move it and iteration 27's attribution of the residual was wrong: mstress runs 0.63 s wall (the "0.62 s per iteration" was a misreading of the rt line), so no decay window acts before exit; `dirty_decay_ms` 1000 / 10000 / -1 give the same 337..351 MiB, jemalloc's own RSS is 284..286 with decay on or off, and only `dirty_decay_ms:0` (purge on `Empty`) lowers challoc's peak, to 262 at 1.12 s wall (jemalloc 1.05 s). `narenas:1` is 283 = jemalloc: the +60 MiB is 16 arenas times what an arena keeps per class and cannot lend (Empty small slabs +18 MiB, partial small slabs +9, touched Empty large-class slabs +21, metadata +9.5; the huge region is 155 MiB at 1 and 17 arenas alike), where jemalloc's one dirty pool per arena serves every class. Design question for the human below; STATUS set to BLOCKED on it. Valkey, both reruns owed since iteration 27: `make valkey STAGE=memefficiency` green on x86_64 (32 tests), the 20-minute `make valkey-soak` on arm64 at 2c8731f with the perf sidecar (`bench/results/2026-09-18-aarch64-gce-decay-soak-valkey-soak.md`): peak RSS +0.1%, post-delete RSS -2.4%, write phase at parity or ahead (valkey-benchmark -2.2%, p99 -2.9%; memtier +0.1%), delete phase 2.8..3.3% behind with +2.6% cycles per command (2314 vs 2256; write 3320 vs 3286, +1.0%), the residue task 4e attributed. Scorecards at 2c8731f and every results file of this run are commit 0623f26.
- Phase 1.5 task 16, acceptance run 4 at 2f218f9 (tree e18fbb4; `bench/results/2026-09-18-phase-1.5-acceptance-4.md`, protocol files `2026-09-18-gce-c4a-4.md`, `-c4a-4b.md`, `-c4-4.md`, `-c4-4b.md`, plus `2026-09-18-x86_64-gce-glibc-simple-acc4.md`): VMs started, synced, driven and stopped by the iteration (`TERMINATED` at the end). (1) `make test` 278 + 90 green on both VMs; (2) six PMU events count on both; (3) two full protocol runs per VM (the second by accident: `make bench BENCHES=... ALLOCS=...` ignores those variables): time inside 10% everywhere on aarch64 (worst alloc-test1 +5.5%) and on x86_64 in run 4b (worst alloc-testN +8.0%, glibc-simple +7.0%; interleaved `ab` +6.5%), with x86_64 glibc-simple at **+10.4% in run 4a**, unreproduced (history +4.1..+7.5%, counters: challoc +11.3% instructions / +6.4% cycles / fewer L1D misses than jemalloc, a constant), recorded and flagged; RSS: mstress +11.0% / +9.6% (aarch64) and +19.2% / +18.4% (x86_64), the accepted row (task 15, design 13.3), now attributed on aarch64 too with `arenasample` (`narenas:1` 286.6 MiB = jemalloc 291..294; the 16-arena excess is Empty small slabs +12..25, partial waste +6..16, metadata +10, large-class Empty pages), every other RSS row inside on its own (alloc-test1 +4.0..5.0%, alloc-testN -1.2..+1.9%, xmalloc-test -2.2..-10.7%); (4) `perf record` on larson 16 threads: x86_64 no lock or atomic RMW symbol in the top 15, aarch64 the outlined LSE helpers at ranks 6/7/10 (0.15/0.10/0.06%, none on the fast path; PASS on substance as run 1), profile shape unchanged since run 1 despite tasks 9/12/14; (4b) larson10 361.1 vs 381.1 M ops/s (-5.2%, aarch64), 295.9 vs 292.4 (ahead, x86_64), L1D/op 2.244 vs old 2.235 (equal within one-run resolution; task 15 read 2.240 vs 2.245) and 1.495 vs 1.519; (5) both VMs `TERMINATED`. Also `make valkey STAGE=memefficiency` green on x86_64 (33 ok; aarch64 in task 15); the soak cited at 75bdbc5 (task 15's code is default-off and Valkey is single-arena, so the soak path is task 14's). **Phase 1.5 DONE.** Two rows for the human in Notes.

- Phase 2 task 2: `mseal` of the never-committed parts of the reservations. `Regions::never_committed` (pure, host tested: page aligned, disjoint from every region, tiles the three mappings exactly, at most `MAX_SEALED = NCLASSES + 5`) lists the gap on either side of every class region and the alignment-plus-slide slack around the huge and metadata regions; `Regions::seal` (Linux) issues one `mseal` per range and `api::init` calls it right after `Regions::reserve` when the probe says `mseal=yes` (57 ranges at quantum 16, 61 at quantum 8). The regions themselves, the fallback guard pages and the region table are not sealed (design decision). `malloc_stats_print` prints `Sealed ranges: N of M`; `api::sealed_ranges()`. Tests: `layout::linux_tests::seal_locks_the_gaps_and_leaves_the_regions_usable` (forked child: every sealed range refuses `munmap`, `mprotect` and `MAP_FIXED` with `EPERM`, smaps shows `sl`, the first and last class slab, the first huge page and the first metadata page still commit, purge and reprotect, VMA growth exactly +108 for 57 ranges, bound `2n + 2`); `tests/c/mseal.c` (`c_mseal`: sealed VMAs found through `/proc/self/smaps` and compared with the allocator's own count, `munmap`/`mprotect`/`MAP_FIXED` `EPERM`, then every tier allocates, `arenas.create`, a thread exits and a fork child allocates; asserts nothing is sealed where its own probe fails). Docker's default seccomp profile filters `mseal` on the Mac (EPERM) and on the GCE VMs' Docker 29.1 (ENOSYS), so `make test` exercises the skip path and `make test DOCKER_FLAGS='--security-opt seccomp=unconfined'` the sealing path: 280 + 91 pass on the Mac (both profiles) and on the arm64 VM (both profiles; one `c_mseal` bound fixed after the VM run, rerun alone on both hosts). `quick arm64 OLD=50f0610`: go, every row within 5% (larson1 42.2 -> 42.2, valkey1 6.312 -> 6.659, churn compacts). Init cost on the VM (`bench/results/2026-09-18-aarch64-gce-mseal.md`): first malloc 100 -> 188 us median with 57 ranges sealed (+88 us, about 1.5 us per `mseal`), `/bin/true` under the preload 664..672 -> 738..743 us; unchanged (108 us) where sealing is skipped; under the 0.2 ms budget, so one call per contiguous range stays. Design 2, 10 (step 5, steps renumbered) and 14 and the README kernel table row say exactly what is sealed and why the rest is not. No fast path touched, no scorecard. Commit dd63704.
- Phase 2 task 1: guard slabs verified end to end. `tests/c/guard_slab.c` (driver `api::ctests::c_guard_slab_fault`, `c_guard_slab_vmas`), under `LD_PRELOAD`, geometry from the public keys only (`opt.guards`, `opt.guard_slab_interval`, `arenas.page`, `arenas.bin.<i>.{size,slab_size}`), no new `mallctl` key. `fault`: fills the 64-byte class past its first guard group, finds the guard slab as the one page group of slabs in the sorted addresses that no block came from, requires that the highest block below it ends exactly at the guard and the lowest block above it starts exactly one group later, then with a `SIGSEGV` handler that `siglongjmp`s: the last byte of the last slot before the guard is writable and sticks, the guard's first byte and the last byte of its first page fault on write (`si_addr` = the guard address), the first byte faults on read, the first slot after the guard is writable; skipped by the driver (and the program's own `madvise` probe) where the kernel has no `MADV_GUARD_INSTALL`. `vmas`: 40.96 M `malloc(16)` (10,000 slabs' worth) with a per-slab seen map, then counts the `/proc/self/maps` lines intersecting the span: bound `VMA_BOUND` = 8, and the guard-slab count in the span must be `span / 8` (plus or minus one) on a guard kernel and 0 otherwise; runs on every kernel. Results: GCE VMs (kernel 7.0.0-1011-gcp, `guard_install=yes`), both architectures: fault at the expected guard slab, `read faults 1`; vmas 10,001 slabs allocated from over an 11,429-slab span (714 MiB), 1428 guard slabs (expected 1428), **1 maps line** intersecting the span (1 rw), process maps lines 58 -> 58 (aarch64) and 66 -> 66 (x86_64). Mac container (Docker Desktop 6.10): fault skipped (`guard_install=no on this kernel; skipping guard_slab fault`), vmas 10,001 slabs over a 10,001-slab span, 0 guard slabs, 1 maps line, 56 -> 56. For scale, the `PROT_NONE` alternative would have put 1428 guards and 1429 rw pieces in that span, about 2857 lines. Wording (review item 19, section 2 row 4b, section 3 rows 5 and 14) was done by task 11: README's kernel table says `no guard slabs` in the fallback column and the paragraph below it names 6.13 and Docker Desktop's 6.10; design 5 and 14 say "when the kernel has `MADV_GUARD_INSTALL` (6.13+), otherwise absent". Design 7 amended: the huge-run `PROT_NONE` fallback costs one VMA per live huge run (decision below). Gates: `make test` 280 + 93 green in the Mac container and on both VMs; `challoc-bench quick arm64 OLD=e0962c2` reports old and new byte-identical (test-only change) and go: larson1 42.4 -> 42.0 M ops/s (+1.0%), larson10 369.5 -> 368.5 (+0.3%), xmalloc 608.5 -> 608.1 M frees/s (+0.1%), alloc-test1 2.695 -> 2.685 s (-0.4%), valkey1 6.942 -> 6.935 M ops/s (+0.1%), churn 6801 -> 3462 slabs at 97.0% occupancy under both (one round, aarch64, load 3.3 at start). No fast path touched, no scorecard. VMs started, synced, driven and stopped by the iteration. Commit e8dba00.
- Phase 2 task 4 (cgroup discovery and sampling, the measurement layer of design 11; the policy is task 5) DONE, commit 21c0509. `src/sys/cgroup.rs`: cgroup path from the `0::` line of `/proc/self/cgroup`, the cgroup2 mount from a chunked line scan of `/proc/self/mountinfo` (2 KiB buffer, no allocation), the directory capped at 2 KiB, the effective-limit walk from the leaf to the mount keeping the minimum of `memory.max` and of `memory.high` per level (`max`/absent = unlimited, re-walked at most once per second), `memory.current` and `memory.events` (`high`/`max`, with rise counts) sampled at most once per 10 ms of `CLOCK_MONOTONIC_COARSE` (one CAS on the timestamp picks the reader) from `purge::on_tick`, the background thread's loop, a `stats.cgroup.current` read and `malloc_stats_print`; `cpu.max` and `cpuset.cpus.effective` parsed at init for design 15. PSI: the background thread, and only it, opens `memory.pressure` read-write and writes `some 150000 1000000`, then the unprivileged `some 300000 2000000` on `EINVAL`; armed, it sleeps in `ppoll` on the trigger (`POLLPRI`) and an eventfd that `wake` writes (new `sys::ppoll`, `sys::eventfd`, `PollFd`, `POLL*`/`O_RDWR`/`EFD_*` constants, `EROFS`; `ppoll`/`eventfd2` header-checked in `tests/c/sysnr.c`); a fire counts `pressure_events` and samples at once; the descriptor is closed on thread exit and in a fork child. `Shared.cgroup` (last field, cold); `stats.cgroup.{limit,high,current,pressure_events}` (`size_t`, read-only, `ENOENT` when untracked); the `cgroup:` report line and a JSON `"cgroup"` object; `cgroup` removed from `INERT_KEYS` (accepted by `abort_conf`, `cgroup:false` reads nothing). Tests: 10 host parsers (`/proc/self/cgroup` v2/mixed/namespace/v1-only, `mountinfo` default/Docker/hybrid/absent/variable optional fields, limits, events, pressure, cpu files, the walk with a lower parent, a lower leaf, nothing, the mount itself and a malformed level, `join_dir`, the chunked scanner at 1/7/64/4096-byte chunks with an over-long line, the display strings); 5 container tests (discovery cross-checked against std reads of the same files, the 10 ms rate limit: 10 reads in ~600k calls over 100 ms, `current` against the kernel's `memory.current` after a touched 64 MiB, the PSI probe, eventfd+ppoll); `tests/c/cgroup.c` in `plain`/`off`/`bg` modes; `make test-cgroup-limit` (`--memory=512m`, `CHALLOC_EXPECT_CGROUP_LIMIT=536870912`) chained into `make test`. What the containers expose: Docker Desktop (Mac) and the C4A VM (kernel 7.0) both give `0::/`, cgroup2 at `/sys/fs/cgroup` mounted read-only, `memory.max`/`memory.high` `max` (536870912 under `--memory=512m`, read as the effective limit on both), `memory.current` live, `/proc/pressure/memory` and `memory.pressure` present; the trigger write is `EROFS` under Docker's default profile on both, and under `--privileged` the 1 s trigger `some 150000 1000000` arms on the Mac kernel (6.10.14-linuxkit, cgroup2 mounted without `nsdelegate`; the background thread reports `psi armed` and `pressure_events` stays 0 under no pressure) but is `EPERM` on the VM (7.0.0-1011-gcp, root with full capabilities, every window tried): the host mounts cgroup2 with `nsdelegate`, under which `cgroup_file_write` refuses writes to non-delegatable files at a cgroup namespace's root; the same trigger written from a child cgroup made inside the container (`mkdir /sys/fs/cgroup/x`, move in) succeeds. So on systemd hosts a container arms a trigger only if its process sits in a child cgroup; the tick's sample covers the rest (design 11 records both facts). Gates: host 230 (was 208); `make test` Mac container 295 + 96 + 18 (default profile and `--memory=512m`); VM arm64 295 + 96 + 18. `challoc-bench quick arm64 OLD=6c9b0e2`: go (larson1 +0.5%, larson10 -0.8%, xmalloc -0.2%, alloc-test1 +0.2%, valkey1 +1.9%, churn compacts 96.9%); no default fast path changed (the tick gains one rate-limited sample in the slow path), so no scorecard. Docs: design 11 rewritten (implemented part present tense, policy "planned, task 5"), 10 (step 7), 6.4, 12, 13.4, 16, 17; README goal 4, memory-return paragraph, kernel-table PSI row, status.
- Phase 2 task 5 (the design 11 policy) DONE, commit 927578e. `src/policy.rs`: the table as pure functions (`band`: no limit / under 70% / 70..90% with the position in the band / 90% and above, on the last `memory.current` against `min(high, max)`; `effective_decay_ns`: the configured period, scaled linearly to 1000 ms across the band, 0 above 90%, `-1` untouched; `retention_cap`: 5% of the limit; `keep_ratio`: `cap / retention`), `Policy` (atomics on `Shared`, after `gc_interval_ns`: band, keep ratio, cap, retention, the `high` baseline, a pending full purge and its count, `thp_forced`), `evaluate` (called by `purge::sample` only when a sample was taken: the periodic tick, the background loop, a fired PSI trigger; sums the `Empty` bytes under each class lock that has any and the huge lock, only under a limit; wakes the background thread when the band changed or a purge is owed) and `empty_bytes`. Wiring: `purge::decay` runs the owed full purge, then every arena's walk with `policy.effective_decay_ns(arena.decay_ns())` and the keep ratio (`Arena/ClassState/Huge::decay_with`; `purge_curve` clamps the curve's allowance to `total * keep16`; the huge walk purges oversized extents whole under the cap, see the decision); `Heap::tick` reads `Shared::gc_interval()` (0 in the immediate band); `Shared::init` applies the policy once on the discovery sample and forces `thp` to `never` under an effective limit below 1 GiB. Keys: `stats.cgroup.{decay_ms,retention_cap,empty_bytes,full_purges}`; the `cgroup policy:` report line (band, `decay_ms`, `retention_cap`, `keep`, `empty_bytes`, `full_purges`, `thp`), the JSON fields, the `opt.thp` note. Tests: 8 host (band edges 0/70/80/90/95/100% and unlimited, `memory.high` below `max`, the scaling at the edges and its monotonicity, `-1` and sub-floor periods, the cap arithmetic and the shares summing under it, `apply` through every band with the `high` rise taken once and the limit going away, the capped huge walk over a coalesced extent, `gc_interval`/period/full purge on a `Shared`); the mallctl mib test covers the new leaves; `tests/c/cgroup_policy.c` (`c_cgroup_policy_bands`, `c_cgroup_policy_high`): without a limit the defaults and a skip line; under `--memory=512m` (Mac and VM alike): `retention_cap` 26843545 = 512 MiB / 20; filled to 81% `decay_ms` 4985 (Mac) / 80.3% 5381 (VM) against the table's 5005 / 5365; freed, `empty_bytes` 0 after 31 ms (Mac) / 52 ms (VM) and RSS 401 -> 1 MiB (the whole coalesced 400 MiB extent went; `keep 6%` shown); usage then 3%, `decay_ms` back to 10000; filled to 95.3% / 94.4% `decay_ms` 0 and `over 90% (purge at once, cache GC every tick)`; freed, one tick later (5 ms Mac, 25 ms VM) `empty_bytes` 65536 (the tick's own last slab), RSS 1-2 MiB. The `memory.high` row: read-only mount under the default profile (skip); `--privileged` alone gets `mkdir` but `+memory` on the container's own `cgroup.subtree_control` is `EBUSY` (skip, both hosts); `--privileged --cgroupns=host` makes a sibling cgroup under the parent (`/sys/fs/cgroup/docker/challoc-policy-<pid>` on Docker Desktop, `/sys/fs/cgroup/system.slice/challoc-policy-<pid>` on the GCE host), moves a child in with `memory.high` 96 MiB, and the child allocating past it sees `events high 22 (+1)` / `1127 (+1)` and `full_purges 1` after 19 ms / 16 ms; both recorded in design 11. `make test-cgroup-limit` now builds the release library itself (the C tests preload it; without the build they ran a stale one, which cost one false diagnosis) and runs `--test-threads=1`. Gates: host 238 (was 230); `make test` Mac 304 + 98 + 21 (default profile; `--memory=512m` 21); VM arm64 304 + 98 + 21. `challoc-bench quick arm64 OLD=13c434d`: go (larson1 +0.0%, larson10 +0.7%, xmalloc +0.3%, alloc-test1 -0.6%, valkey1 -0.4%, churn compacts 97.0%); no default fast path changed (the tick gains one load on a line it reads; the no-limit case returns after two loads), so no scorecard. What task 6 will use: the retention cap (a churn's freed 400 MiB is purged at the first walk after one step), the zero period above 90%, and `stats.cgroup.empty_bytes` to watch it. Docs: design 11 (policy in present tense with the retention, evaluation-point, THP and `memory.high` findings), 8, 8.1, 6.4, 13.4, 16, 17, the config table; README goal 4, memory-return paragraph, PSI row, status.
- Phase 2 task 6 (the container tests) DONE, commit 6e33399. `tests/c/cgroup_churn.c` (`purge|keep|report [MiB] [threshold] [--any-allocator]`: half 1 MiB runs, a quarter 32 KiB, a quarter 1 KiB, all touched; freed; 2 s of idle as an idle event loop, 4096 `malloc(48)`/`free` per 10 ms, RSS from `/proc/self/statm` and the cgroup's `memory.current` printed every 100 ms plus a RESULT line; `purge` passes when RSS goes under the threshold inside the window, `keep` when it does not), `tests/c/cgroup_oscillate.c` (`[seconds] [low] [high] [hold_ms] [--any-allocator]`: 200..480 MiB live, by bytes 15% 1 MiB runs, 20% each 64/16/4 KiB, 15% 1 KiB, 5% each 256/64 B, 64 KiB batches of one class, random frees, each end held 1.5 s with the trickle, `memory.current` sampled every 8 MiB and at both ends, `memory.events` before/after, `stats.cgroup.*` at the end, exit 0 if alive), both single-threaded with no `malloc_trim` and no background thread; `scripts/cgroup-tests.sh` (in-container driver: compiles into `target/cgroup-tests/`, one allocator of challoc/challoc-off/jemalloc/mimalloc/glibc, prints kernel, limits, `memory.events` before and after, `memory.peak`, a `timeout -s KILL` watchdog of 360 s, exits with the program's status, 137 + `oom_kill` = OOM-killed); Makefile: `DOCKER_BASE` split out of `DOCKER_RUN`, `cgroup_docker`/`cgroup_run` (one `--memory=L --memory-swap=L` container per run, swap off because Docker Desktop's VM has 1 GiB of swap), `make test-cgroup-churn` (512m purge/keep at 400/100 MiB, 256m purge/keep at 200/50 MiB, scaled to the limit; ~40 s; chained into `make test`), `make test-cgroup-oscillate` (`OSC_SECS` 300, `CONTROL=1` adds the `cgroup:false` run, recorded not judged; opt-in for its length), `make test-cgroup` (both); `src/api/ctests.rs` runs each program small under the default profile (`c_cgroup_churn_program`, `c_cgroup_oscillate_program`). Results (`bench/results/2026-09-18-aarch64-gce-cgroup-container-tests.md`), VM 7.0.0-1011-gcp: 512m churn RSS 403 -> 13 MiB under 100 MiB 46 ms after the frees (`memory.current` 460 -> 69, the compile's page cache), `cgroup:false` 319 MiB at 2 s; 256m 202 -> 8 MiB in 79 ms, off 161 MiB; oscillation alive 300 s, 98 cycles, peak `memory.current` 502 MiB, `memory.events` all 0, `stats.cgroup.current` 293 MiB at the low end, `empty_bytes 20 MiB` under the 25 MiB cap; the control also survives (99 cycles, peak 507, RSS 485..501 MiB at the low end: `Empty` memory reused in place). Mac (6.10.14-linuxkit): 402 -> 13 MiB in 48 ms / 329 MiB off; 202 -> 8 MiB in 82 ms / 169 MiB off; oscillation 97 cycles, peak 506 MiB, one `max` event (a reclaim at the limit, no kill), control 98 cycles. Gates: `make test` Mac 304 + 100 + 23 (+ the four churn rows), VM arm64 304 + 100 + 23 (+ the four churn rows); `challoc-bench quick arm64 OLD=e7dfb95`: go (larson1 +0.2%, larson10 -0.8%, xmalloc -0.2%, alloc-test1 +0.8%, valkey1 -1.3%, churn compacts 97.0%; the library is unchanged, the run is the plumbing check), no scorecard. Docs: design 11 (the container-test paragraph: the tick fact and the reuse-in-place finding), README memory-return paragraph and status.
- Phase 2 task 7 (the comparison run) DONE, commit 98dc2ac (results in the same file). `make test-cgroup-compare` (`CGROUP_ALLOCS` jemalloc mimalloc, `report` churn at both limits and the oscillation, OUTCOME lines, never fails the target). - Phase 2 task 3 (the opt-in tunables) DONE, commits 5c2c741 (the keys) and 7087c44 (the clear moved off the fast push). `zero_on_free`: `Shared::push_flags` (one byte on the first line next to `multi_arena`; `PUSH_SCAN` = `double_free_scan`, `PUSH_ZERO` = this key) is tested once per push; with the bit the push declines and `free_slow_in` clears the slot's class-size bytes (`heap::zero_slot`) before caching it, on the plain path (after the bit test), the sized path (`sdallocx`, `free_sized`: the class from the size hint, as the push trusts it) and a direct release; a huge free clears the run's usable bytes (`Shared::zero_huge`) before `free_run`. `slot_randomize`: `ClassState::claim_random` (same `plan`, each non-prefix pick's bit scan starts at a random word, Fisher-Yates over the batch: order changes, the slot set and `Claim::zeroed` do not), routed by `Heap::claim` from the configuration on the slow path; per-heap xorshift `Heap::rng` seeded from `getrandom` on the first randomised claim (no syscall per heap creation by default); direct claims stay ordered; with the key the compacting run is a shuffled block (design 4). Both keys left `INERT_KEYS` (now `thp`, `stats_print`; `abort_conf` accepts them); README's table rows say opt-in with footnote (2); design 4, 6.3 step 3, 12 and 14 updated. `double_free_scan` was priced by Phase 1.5 task 9c (`bench/results/2026-09-18-*-gce-detection-gaps.md` section 4) and is cited, not redone. Tests: host 243 (new: `push_flags_encode_zero_on_free_and_double_free_scan`, `slot_randomize_seeds_the_heap_lazily`, `slot_randomize_shuffles_the_batch_and_keeps_the_slot_set`, `slot_randomize_keeps_the_compacting_run_valid`; Linux only: `zero_on_free_clears_the_slot_on_the_plain_sized_and_direct_paths`, `zero_on_free_clears_a_huge_run`); container `make test` 310 + 105 + 23 (cgroup) green, new drivers `c_tunables_zero_on_free` / `c_tunables_slot_randomize` (`tests/c/tunables.c`, `zero`/`dirty`/`random`/`ordered` modes: the freed block reads zero and comes back zero through `free`, `sdallocx`, `free_sized` and a 1 MiB run, the control keeps its bytes; 512 first allocations of the 64-byte class 263 steps up / 248 down with the key, 511 up / 0 down without), `c_fuzz_short_under_zero_on_free`, `c_fuzz_short_under_slot_randomize` (and all three keys at once), `mimalloc_test_stress_check_zero_slot_randomize`. Measurement (both VMs, started/synced/stopped by the iteration, `us-east4-a`): `quick` keys off go on both (7087c44 vs 320bbfb: aarch64 larson1 +0.2%, larson10 -1.4%, xmalloc -0.7%, alloc-test1 -0.6%, valkey1 +2.2%; x86_64 -7.9% / +2.6% / -3.0% / -0.6% / -1.6%; churn compacts); `ab` 5 rounds `base=320bbfb off zero rand jemalloc` on larson1, larson10, redis, valkey1 (`bench/results/2026-09-18-{aarch64,x86_64}-gce-tunables.md` section 4): off vs base aarch64 +0.0% / +0.9% / -0.1% / -0.9%, x86_64 +4.8% / +1.3% / -1.7% / -6.7% (overlapping lists, a noisy run); `zero_on_free` aarch64 +145% / +237% / +11.2% / +84%, x86_64 +180% / +231% / +13.8% / +242%; `slot_randomize` aarch64 +1.4% / +1.4% / -0.7% / -1.1% (+12% / +7% RSS on larson), x86_64 +5.3% / +2.3% / -1.3% / -6.3% (+11% / +11% RSS). - Phase 2.5 task 1b (the flush sort, mechanism M2) DONE, commit 3ed77b7. `Heap::flush` no longer sorts the whole slot array: a slot array already in address order is split directly (`is_sorted`, the old sort's O(n) case, xmalloc-test's consumer caches); otherwise `select_nth_unstable` puts the highest half on top and `group_by_slab` (an in-place American flag sort on the slab number when the half spans fewer than 256 slabs, nothing for one slab, a sort beyond) groups it by slab; the kept half is sorted only on a cache's first two consecutive unsorted flushes and every 32nd after (one byte in the cache header), so a cache pushed in order heals back onto the sorted path. `ClassState::release_owned` accumulates each slab's bits in a scratch bitmap (`layout::MAX_SLAB_WORDS`) so a slab's slots may arrive in any order; the straddling double-free check is one compare of the kept maximum with the lowest released address. Attribution before/after on both VMs (`bench/results/2026-09-19-{aarch64,x86_64}-gce-flush-sort.md`, new `bench/gce/perf-record.sh`): the sort's share of glibc-simple 8.8..9.1% -> `flush` 3.5..4.0% (aarch64), 8.4..9.0% -> 3.7% (x86_64); python1-malloc 5.3% -> 2.0%, 4.7% -> 1.9%; cycles per glibc-simple op 107.2 -> 102.3 / 120.9 -> 110.4. Rejected with numbers (results section 2): selection + sort of the released half (xmalloc +51%), a bucket pass over the whole array (+49%), the selection alone (+21% / +9..13%, no self-healing), an unconditional kept sort (go, but no gain). `quick` go on both VMs (churn 97.0%); `ab` 5 rounds vs 1339eb6: glibc-simple -4.7% / -9.0%, python1-malloc -3.0% / -2.1%, larson1 +0.0% / +0.0%, larson10 -0.3% / +0.7%, mstress -2.1% / -1.8%; dict_list-malloc (two python-bench runs) -6.7% / -7.2%. Tests: host 248, `make test` Mac 315 + 105 + 23 and both VMs 315 + api set incl. `double_free_straddle` and `double_free_cached`; calloc-zero campaign default 200/200 clean on x86_64. Scorecard lines for 3ed77b7 on both VMs. Acceptance reading in Design decisions.

- Phase 2.5 task 3 (the floor, proven): DONE, commits 675ab63 and d152a64 (driver, test modules on the runtime page size, docs), f53bc3d (the matrix results and logs) and 0969afa (the second iteration: the 64 KiB cell's term measured and resolved, `matrix lab|ssh|sync`, the composition lines of `tests/c/cgroup_oscillate.c`, the rerun logs). Evidence `bench/results/2026-09-19-matrix.md`: every cell of the table PASS, 464/0 tests, fuzz and `make test-cgroup` green, on 5.10 (Debian 11, x86_64), 6.1 (Debian 12, both architectures), 6.8 GA (both, x86_64 also `--privileged`), 7.0.0-31-generic (the 6.13+ non-GCE kernel, `--privileged`: `mseal=yes process_madvise=pidfd_self`), 7.0.0-1011-gcp (both) and 64 KiB pages (6.8.0-139-generic-64k, arm64: 464/0, fuzz PASS, cgroup PASS in the rerun, 584 s). The 64 KiB oscillation (F1): measured on a kept cell VM against 4 KiB with the same seeded object sequence, the term is not retention: at the top of a climb `empty_bytes` is 0 at 4 KiB and 0..2 MiB at 64 KiB, and those are 4 KiB slabs of the 1 KiB class scattered through 64 KiB page groups that are never all idle (`dirty_decay_ms:0` leaves the same 571 slabs; at 4 KiB they are `Purged`); `active` is identical (493 MiB at 480 live, cycle 5 on both); the 64 KiB top is +5 MiB of anon (page-rounded metadata tables +1, the stuck `Empty` +2, the program's own segments +2) and +2 MiB of page tables. The session's three candidates (bands on the page-rounded figure, thresholds or cap scaled by the page, purging sooner above 90%) would purge nothing more and were rejected by that control; `src/` is unchanged (byte-identical to 9c4a4b7, so no quick tier and no scorecard). The oscillation program lowers its default target by the measured envelope (8 MiB: 472 instead of 480) when the page is larger than 4 KiB (design decision below, flagged for the human); the 4 KiB controls with the same program: gcp-arm64 rerun 464/0, PASS, `current_peak` 502 unchanged; the Mac `make test` 334 + 107 + 23 pass and its 300 s oscillation at 480 MiB survives 98 cycles (peak 511, one `max` event reclaimed, no kill); host 264 pass. - Phase 2.5 task 4 (the CPU matrix on GCP): DONE, commits 7b2c50b (driver: cells by machine type and zone list, the `cpu-*` cells, the `scorecard` cell flag, the cpuinfo/`cpuid-dump.c` capture, cross-zone delete/clean/lab; the `uai` probe), 24d4130 (the `la57_enabled` test fix, captured fixtures, `MATRIX_PMU`), e06efb5 (the `matrix clean` wait fix) and 6891b74 (results, logs, per-CPU scorecards). Evidence `bench/results/2026-09-19-cpu-matrix.md`, logs `bench/results/matrix/2026-09-19-cpu-*.log`, scorecards `bench/results/scorecard-{c4a,t2a,c4,c3,c3d,n2d,c2,n4}.md` (8 vCPU, 3 rounds, 7b2c50b; the two reference files untouched). Eight cells, all Ubuntu 24.04 + 7.0.0-1011-gcp, `-standard-8`: C4A Axion V2, T2A Altra N1 (us-central1-a), C4 8581C, N4 8581C, C3 8481C, C3D EPYC 9B14 Genoa, N2D EPYC 7B13 Milan, C2 Cascade Lake; no PENDING cell. `make test` 465/0 on seven cells; the C3D cell failed one test at 7b2c50b (`probe_matches_proc_cpuinfo`: the test equated `/proc/cpuinfo`'s `57 bits virtual`, which is cpuid 0x80000008's width, with five-level paging, while the GCE Genoa guest hides the LA57 bit; fixed in 24d4130, rerun on a C3D lab VM 467/0). The answers: no GCP CPU exposes MTE (Axion `hwcap2=0x3f3ff`, Altra `hwcap2=0`), none exposes LAM (7.1 `EAX=0x1c30` on 8581C/8481C, `0x20` on Genoa, no subleaf on Milan/Cascade Lake), and Genoa does not expose UAI to the guest (`0x80000021 EAX=0x10000067`, bit 7 clear; it also hides LA57). Scorecards versus the reference CPU of the architecture: the 8-vCPU controls are within 0..7 points of the 16-vCPU reference lines; the CPU-specific rows are valkey1 on C3/C3D/N2D/C2 (+41.7/+43.3/+21.0/+34.4% against jemalloc, reference +3.9%), valkey1-free and xmalloc on C3D/N2D/C2, and the larson rows on T2A (+25.6/+15.5/+11.8%) and C2. None is a probed feature's cost (no code path is CPU-conditional; the kernel halves are identical on every cell), so nothing is gated; they are recorded as needing attribution: GCE refuses the PMU on every family but C4/C4A, so the lab VMs (C3, C3D) ran the SIGPROF sampler instead: two thirds of the valkey1 gap is the model program's own time per operation (61 against 48 samples/Mop on the 8481C) with challoc's tighter layout (147 MiB active against jemalloc's 298 for 141 allocated), one third the allocator's (18 against 11), and challoc's sized and plain paths cost the same while jemalloc's `sdallocx` is 12..23% faster than its `free` on these CPUs. No matrix VM exists at the end; the reference VMs stayed TERMINATED. Next: Phase 2.5 task 5 (tagged pointers part 1: probes and design; note that no GCE CPU can test either mechanism).

- Phase 2.5 task 4b DONE (2026-09-19; probe flags, results and plan task 4c in commit 41fea35). The valkey1 gap on the non-reference x86 CPUs is attributed with a PMU: GCE refuses `--performance-monitoring-unit` for `c3/c3d/n2d/c2-standard-8` at every level, zone and API track ("PerformanceMonitoringUnit is not supported for c3-standard-8 on API version v1"), so a `c3-standard-192-metal` (8481C, `us-east4-c` after a stockout in `-a`, 61 minutes, deleted) gave the counters and a `c3-standard-8` lab VM the VM side (software events, tracepoints, `perf record -e cpu-clock`, `MALLOC_CONF` variants). `bench/results/2026-09-19-8481c-valkey1-attribution.md`: on the metal the write phase is at parity (290 vs 283 cycles per SET, 13.0 vs 13.1 M SET/s) with every miss column equal (L1D 4.68/4.83, L2 1.44/1.44, L3 0.51/0.52, RFO-to-DRAM 1.89/1.92, dTLB walks 0.95+1.90/1.03+1.95 per SET) and only instructions apart (391 vs 266, +125 in the two mallocs and two sdallocx); the `-A` address histograms are uniform modulo 4K/128K/2M under both allocators (placement refuted); the same binaries in the 8-vCPU C3 VM are -28% (7.2 vs 10.0 M SET/s), user time, unmoved by background_thread/decay/compaction/cgroup/narenas/tcache_max/pinning; the `-x K` probe flag (about 80 independent instructions per round) reproduces the gap with jemalloc itself: +160 instructions per SET drop it from 10.2 to 6.5 M SET/s on the VM (cliff between 350 and 430 instructions; on the metal the same step raises `memory_activity.stalls_l3_miss` 115 -> 145 cycles per SET), so the mechanism is the instruction window: the second SET's dict-entry DRAM miss stops overlapping the first when the SET no longer fits the ROB/load buffer, and the small-L3 CPUs (SPR 1.9, CLX 1.4 MiB per core, half a Genoa/Milan CCD per 8-vCPU VM) take the miss to DRAM where the reference C4 (5 MiB per core) mostly hits L3. The largest single term is `is_guard_slab`'s `% guard_interval` in `Shared::sized_slot`: a `divl` by a run-time divisor, 12.5% of `je_sdallocx`'s samples, 12 `arith.div_active` cycles and 16 instructions per SET; `guards:false` gives 9.7 M SET/s on the VM (jemalloc 10.1), `guard_slab_interval:4194304` (division kept) 7.3, plain free unaffected, which is why valkey1 and not valkey1-free showed the gap. T2A (`2026-09-19-altra-larson-attribution.md`, perf cpu-clock on a t2a lab VM): the program's time per larson1 op is equal (22.9 vs 23.2 ns), challoc's malloc+free pair 22.5 vs jemalloc's 13.2 ns; challoc's plain `free` is a six-load dependent chain (region table, class geometry, ClassState, bitmap base, bitmap word, owner word; 125 instruction lines vs jemalloc's 45) whose bitmap-word consumer holds 24% of free in larson1 and 68% in valkey1-free; LSE helpers 0.25%, `guards:false` no effect. Fix proposed as plan task 4c (power-of-two guard interval with a mask, then the sized path's instruction budget), not implemented (more than one line). Also: 60k page-group `mprotect` commits in the fill (equal fill time), noted. Every VM of the iteration deleted; the reference VMs stayed TERMINATED.

- Phase 2.5 task 4c: the sized path's division and instruction budget. (a) `guard_slab_interval` is a power of two (a configured value rounds up to the next one and is reported as the new `Issue::Rounded`, an abort under `abort_conf`, `opt.guard_slab_interval` returns the rounded value; 1 still clamps to 2), `SlabTable` stores `guard_mask` and `is_guard_slab` is `(page_group + 1) & mask == 0` (all ones when off, nothing matches): the `divl` twice per Valkey SET is gone, guard density unchanged. (b) `Shared::sized_slot` checks the pointer against the hint's region alone (`Regions::slot_in_class`, no superblock classification or class compare), the guard test is one 8-byte load from the dense `Shared::guard_test` (`(slab + spp) & mask`), the class table line is not touched and the watermark not read (a slot past it aborts at the flush by name, `SlabTable::owner`; new tests `guard_test_agrees_with_the_tables`, `sized_free_past_the_watermark_aborts_at_the_flush`); `sdallocx_routed` takes the raw flags, `class_for`'s align loop has no bounds-check call; new `valkey1-write` row (per-SET counters); `quick-perf.sh` prints extra event columns. Numbers (`bench/results/2026-09-19-{x86_64,aarch64}-gce-sized-path.md`): C3 lab (8481C, 8 vCPU) write phase parent 7.65..7.98 / (a) 9.79..10.08 / (b) 10.83 vs jemalloc 10.49..10.83 M SET/s (-27% -> -6.9% -> +3.2%, acceptance: within 10% for (a)); C4 `arith.div_active` per valkey1 op 40.1 -> 33.0 (jemalloc 34.2); instructions per SET on the C4 384.6 -> 344.6 (jemalloc 268.2; target under 330 not reached, 15 short, the frame kept by the scan path's `cached_twice` call is the next 14), C4A 383.8 -> 345.5; static fast path x86_64 101 -> 78, aarch64 88 -> 69 instructions per `sdallocx`; `valkey1-free` and larson rows unchanged; `quick` go on both reference VMs and the lab for (a) and (b); `make test` 340 + 107 + 23 on the Mac and both VMs (268 host). Commits 102049d (a), a7059bb (b).

- Phase 2.5 task 4d DONE as a measurement, commit 25977ca (`bench/results/2026-09-19-aarch64-64k-valkey-soak.md`, the two samples CSVs, challoc's `MEMORY MALLOC-STATS` at t=1200). - Phase 2.5 task 4f DONE as an attribution, no `src/` change, commit 2be28cd (`bench/results/2026-09-19-aarch64-64k-defrag-attribution.md` with the 1 s samples, the server's defrag lines, stats prints; `bench/probes/valkey-defrag-sidecar.sh`; design 13.4; plan 4f). A shortened soak (`DURATION=600 INTERVAL=1 OCCUPANCY=1 MS_INTERVAL=5`) on a fresh `MATRIX_MACHINE=c4a-standard-16 MATRIX_PMU=1 matrix lab 64k-arm64` cell, both allocators, with the sidecar switching the server to `loglevel verbose` (defrag.c's "Starting active defrag ... frag_bytes" / "Active defrag done ... reallocated" lines), read against Valkey 9.1.2's `allocator_defrag.c` and `defrag.c`. (a) The rule reads `nfree`/`nregs`/`size` from `batch_query` and `curregs`/`curslabs`/`nonfull_slabs` from `stats.arenas.4096.bins.<j>`; `bin_nfree`, `bin_nregs`, `slabcur_addr` are not read. challoc's answers are exact (`live != bits 0` in every print); the one semantic difference, `nonfull_slabs` including the current slab where jemalloc's excludes `slabcur`, errs toward moving more. (b) `MALLOCX_TCACHE_NONE` copies land in the lowest partial slab; jemalloc's in `slabcur`, the lowest non-full slab by (sn, address): the same placement, and the same waste after the second cycle in the classes with identical 64 KiB slabs (512 B 1.9 vs 1.9 MiB, 1024 B 7.3 vs 5.5). (c) The stop is `active-defrag-ignore-bytes` (100 MB) checked between sub-second cycles against a 35 s deletion: challoc 2 cycles (154k + 143k moves), jemalloc 3 (163k + 137k + 74k; cycles 2 and 3 starting at frag_bytes 105.8 and 104.9 MB). jemalloc's second cycle leaves 62.4 MiB of `frag_smallbins` against challoc's 40.6 (its 192..448 KiB slabs of the 320..896 B classes: 640 B 13.7 vs 6.5, 896 B 10.3 vs 6.2, 768 B 8.2 vs 5.2 MiB), which re-crosses 100 MB as the last 40 MiB are deleted; challoc's 64 KiB slabs purge (5,067 in the 640 B class) and its waste plateaus at 97.7 MiB of `frag_bytes` / 89.6 of `frag_smallbins`. Result: RSS after settle 172.2 vs 142.9 MiB (**+20.5%**, criterion 10%: FAIL, as 4e's +20.0%), `active` 159.6 vs 137.9, hits 297k vs 374k, peak RSS +1.7%, throughput at parity. Diagnostic (not a product setting): the same run under challoc with `CONFIG SET active-defrag-ignore-bytes 83886080` gives three cycles (155k + 151k + 74k = 380k moves) and 145.6 MiB after settle, +1.9% against jemalloc's stock 142.9: the whole gap is that one cycle. Second-order term recorded: after the last cycle the balanced DEL:SET churn drains jemalloc's `active` by 12 MiB and challoc's by 4 over 210 s (the compacting cache's claims and releases circulate within the dense slabs; a `heap.rs` pop/flush question for a task of its own). Nothing in `src/` changed, so no `quick`, `make test` or scorecard; the acceptance soaks are not owed (the library is e7c8551's). The lab VM was deleted (`matrix clean`); the reference VMs stayed TERMINATED.
- Phase 2.5 task 4g DONE as an attribution, no `src/` change, commit e32d3f3 (`bench/results/2026-09-19-aarch64-64k-compaction.md`; churn probe log, two shortened soaks' CSVs, stats prints at t=245/325/385 for both allocators, the servers' defrag lines, the idle-key sample). On a throwaway 64 KiB C4A lab (`matrix lab 64k-arm64`, deleted after): `bench/probes/churn.c` at the 64 KiB geometry drains a 469-slab class at 128 regions (512 B), 102 (640 B) and 64 (1024 B) to 214..216 slabs at 97% occupancy within 2 s, the Phase 1 figure (469 -> 216 at 64 regions); schedule off and jemalloc stay at 469. The four hypotheses of the task are ruled out by 4f's own prints: every class `c_on` 1 / `c_off` 0 / `gated` 0 with 9.3k holes against a 512 threshold (i, iv), `refilled` constant for 270 s (ii), and the probe's drain at 128 regions (iii). New `PIN` parameter of the probe (a fraction of survivors never freed, scattered at random; default 0 is the quick row): the end state is the address gradient's, predictable as the slabs the churned values fill from the bottom plus the pinned slabs above (368/372/375 predicted vs 361/371/367 measured at one pinned block per slab), and at the soak's density of four per slab challoc ends at 468..469 against a floor of 464..465. The residue named: memtier's fixed-seed stream leaves an age tail, 16..18% of each class's live values not selected for 60..150 s at the end of the delete phase (2.1..2.3k per 640..1024 B class, 1.2k at 512 B; 57..122 per class older than the delete phase; one `EVAL` over every key with `OBJECT IDLETIME` and `STRLEN`), placed LIFO into random slabs at 4.7 per 64 KiB slab (1024 B), so no slab of the class is free of them; the schedule packs the other 82..84% into the lowest slabs within 30..45 s of the last cycle (`compacted` 28k -> 209k while `curslabs` sits at 123; 80k -> 422k at 497) and `curslabs` is at the floor; the 4..6 slabs per class that did drain are the Poisson 1% without an old value. jemalloc's larger post-cycle drift (-7.7 vs -3.4 MiB of `active` here; -12 vs -4 in 4f) is its 320 KiB-slab 640 B class emptying at the settle (77 -> 62 slabs) plus the slabs its third cycle left without old values; at equal 64 KiB geometry both drift 1..6 slabs. A fullest-partial-slab rebuild claim would gain 19..20% of slabs only at about one old value per slab (probe 361 vs floor 291), a regime neither soak is in, and is not built. Soak rows (300 s protocol, both allocators): peak RSS 592.0 vs 588.0 (+0.7%), RSS after settle 180.9 vs 153.8 (+17.6%, criterion FAIL stated; 2 vs 3 cycles as in 4f), throughput within 2%. Acceptance taken on its second branch: the residue attributed to `active-defrag-ignore-bytes` alone (4f's diagnostic at 80 MB: +1.9%); the 4 KiB soak unchanged (no library change), no `quick`/`quick-perf`/scorecard (fast paths untouched). design 13.3 corrected: direct claims land in the lowest partial slab, not the fullest. No reference VM started; lab VM deleted, `gcloud compute instances list --filter='name~challoc-m'` empty.
- Phase 2.5 task 7 (lane A, 2026-09-19): PAC-signed cache pointers on arm64. `src/sys/pac.rs` (`pacda`/`autda`/`xpacd` via `asm!` with `.arch_extension pauth`, `available()` from `HWCAP_PACA`+`PACG`, `fpac()` from `ID_AA64ISAR1/2_EL1`, `mask()` from one `xpacd`); `Shared::push_flags` bit `PUSH_PAC`, set by `init` when the probe says yes and `pac_pointers:true` (new key, default false); every slot pointer in a thread cache's stack signed with the stack base as modifier (push, refill, run rebuild) and authenticated on pop, scheduled pop and flush (the flush orders by address bits and authenticates only the released half); the TLS heap pointer signed with `tpidr_el0` (`tls::get_tp`), sentinels compared first and never signed; `existing_heap(shared: *const Shared)`. Tests: `corrupted_cache_entry_or_heap_pointer_faults` (forked child: `SIGILL` on FEAT_FPAC, `SIGSEGV` otherwise; both the entry and the TLS slot), `cache_entries_and_the_tls_heap_pointer_are_signed`, `pac_flag_follows_the_probe_and_the_key`, `sys::pac` round trip / mismatch / mask, `tls::get_tp`, C `tls_threads` and `double_free_cached` under `pac_pointers:true`; the heap tests' `Shared` builders turn the key on. `make test` green on the Mac (349 + 110 + 23 + cgroup churn), the C4A and the C4. `quick` go on both VMs for the shipped default; with the key on the C4A is NO-GO (xmalloc +11.0%, larson +1.2..1.9%), so the key is opt-in. Design 6.1, 6.2, 6.3, 12, 14, 17; README table row. `bench/results/2026-09-19-lane-a-pac-pointers.md`. Commit 9157450.
- Phase 2.5 task 12 (lock) DONE (lane C, 2026-09-19, 5f0a138; results ce3173f): the futex mutex's spin phase waits with a `SpinHint` chosen once at init (`src/lock.rs`): `Baseline` (`pause`/`isb`, what it always did), `Umwait` (x86_64 WAITPKG: `umonitor` the lock word, re-check, `umwait` C0.1 with a 1024-tick TSC deadline), `Wfe` (aarch64: `ldaxr` arms the exclusive monitor on the word, `wfe` until the holder's store, an interrupt or the event stream's tick). `sys::cpu::spin_hint(isa)` is what `auto` installs (`umwait` with WAITPKG, else the baseline, never `wfe`), suggested by `Cpu::probe` (the one thing the report acts on); the `spin_hint` key (`auto|baseline|umwait|wfe`, `opt.spin_hint`, design 12) forces one, `umwait` refused without WAITPKG and `wfe` without aarch64 plus the timer event stream (`HWCAP_EVTSTRM`, `sys::cpu::event_stream()`), each as `Issue::Unavailable`. Measured (`bench/results/2026-09-19-lane-c-spin-and-geometry.md`, raw ab logs `2026-09-19-lane-c-ab-*.log`, the one surviving raw `.md` `2026-09-19-lane-c-spin-c2-2.md`): WAITPKG is hidden by GCE's hypervisor on every cell, so only the fallback exists on x86_64: the mechanism is within noise of the old lock on the C4 (larson16 +1.1 / -1.0%, larson10 +0.4 / -2.0%, xmalloc -1.5 / +0.8%, two runs) and the C4A (+0.4, +0.3, -0.5); on the Cascade Lake larson16 +0.9 / +0.7%, larson10 +1.7 / -2.5% inside the lists, xmalloc +3.5% with disjoint lists and then +2.7% with the lists overlapping (open, section 2.4). `wfe` against the same binary at `baseline`: larson16 and larson10 within noise on the Altra and the Axion, xmalloc-test -3.2 / -3.3% with RSS +27 / +31% (50.7 to 64.4, 54.9 to 71.7 MiB): a `wfe` waiter is handed the lock later than a `pause` re-poller and the allocating threads' residue grows. So `auto` never chooses `wfe`; it stays as the opt-in `spin_hint:wfe`. The item's named row (larson16 on two-threads-per-core x86) is not moved: recorded as no gain, code kept at zero cost. Design 5 (the lock and the hint), 12 (the key), 17 (WAITPKG and event-stream rows split from CLDEMOTE). `make test` 357 + 108 + 23 = 488/0 on the C2, C4, T2A and C4A (final tree; the C4A needed a rerun after `api::tests::fork_handlers_take_and_release_every_lock` saw the huge lock held once, a snapshot race with the other api tests, not this change); host 284.
- Phase 2.5 task 12 (geometry) DONE as a measurement, code dropped (lane C, 2026-09-19, 23bb3a9; results ce3173f): the model (design 6.2, "Cache geometry": the L1D does not bind, the fast path touches one line of the header and one of the stack per class; what scales with the capacity is the descriptor and bitmap lines a flush of `cap / 2` slots streams, at worst 32 KiB at 1024, which must stay L2-resident; hence the ladder's top and `tcache_max` halve per full halving of the L2 below the reference 2 MiB, floors 128 and 8 KiB, the constants exactly on both reference geometries) was implemented in `config.rs` (`nslots_for_l2`, `tcache_max_for_l2`, `Config::fit_to_caches` from a second `Cpu::probe`, explicit-key bits, a host test asserting the reference ladder class by class) and priced against the constants through `MALLOC_CONF` on the Cascade Lake (32K / 1M; 512 slots: larson1 +1.3% disjoint, larson10 +5.4% noisy, valkey1 +0.7%, redis and alloc-testN flat; 256 slots: larson1 +6.2%; RSS larson10 80.4 to 68.6 MiB) and the Altra (64K / 1M; larson1 +2.9%, larson10 +3.5%, valkey1 +3.2% all disjoint, redis +0.6%, alloc-testN +0.3%; RSS larson10 86.1 to 77.1 MiB). No throughput row improves, so per the acceptance the derivation was removed: the constants stand on every CPU, `opt.tcache_nslots_small_max` and `opt.tcache_max` report them, and only the model and the numbers are recorded (design 6.2 and 17, the results file section 3).
- Phase 2.5 task 13 DONE (lane C, 2026-09-19): `src/model.rs`, test-only (`#[cfg(test)] mod model;` in `lib.rs`), an explicit-state model checker on stable Rust with no dependency, run by `cargo test --features std model::`. One class, two slabs of four slots, one or two arenas, two or three threads. Shared state: per slab the state byte, `live`, the bitmap word, the descriptor arena and the slot memory (a dirty bit per slot); per arena the class lock, the partial/empty/purged sets and the group cursor; the owner word per group and the class-wide next group. Operations mirror `class.rs` step by step with the lines quoted: `Refill(n)` = lock, `plan_locked` (the partial walk with the full-slab `clear_partial`, `next_slab`: empty, purged, the group cursor, `adopt_idle_group`/`transfer_group` with `group_handoff`, `take_group`; `take` with `add_live`, the partial bit rule, the zero flag and `claim_prefix`), unlock, then `finish` (`claim_word`'s `fetch_or` per step, waiting on a clear bit instead of `MAX_EMPTY_PASSES`), and the `claim_with` re-plan when short; `Write(i)`; `Release` = per slab `fetch_and` (double-free, guard/Fresh and owner checks), `sub_live`, `on_live_zero` (lock, owner check, `Active` with `live == 0` to `Empty`), then `reopen` (lock, `Active` and owner) for full words; `Purge` = lock, every `Empty` slab to `Purged` and its memory to zero. DFS over every interleaving, memoised on the whole state (`HashSet<Node>`), invariants at every state: no slot held by two threads, a held slot's bit set, a slot reported zero and unwritten reads zero, `live == popcount(bits) + reservations in flight + releases between fetch_and and sub_live`, a slab in at most one set and only its owner's, partial implies `Active`, `Empty`/`Purged` in exactly their owner's set with `live 0` and no bits (`Purged` and `Fresh` read zero), descriptor arena equals the owner word; a protocol abort (`zero_slab_had_bits_set`, `double_free`, `idle_group_in_use`, underflow) and a deadlock are violations too. Results: pre-fix order (`Variant { prefix_claim: false }`) finds the calloc race in 58 states and prints the 23-step interleaving (T0 plans a zero pick and unlocks; T1 plans, `fetch_or`s slot 0, writes it, `fetch_and`s it; T0's `fetch_or` takes slot 0 under the stale zero flag), found on the `Purged` round of the two-round scenario and on a dedicated `Purged` scenario (41 states); the test asserts the mechanism's order. Handoff without the owner check (`owner_check: false`, three threads, two arenas, groups of one slab) finds slab 0 owned by arena 1 in arena 0's empty set in 561 states (T0's `on_live_zero` pending across T1's reuse, arena 0's empty, arena 1's adoption and reuse). Current protocol passes: calloc race 2153 states (0.03 s), churn over two slabs 11214 (0.09 s), handoff 5759 (0.07 s), three threads 59925 (0.80 s), `Purged` scenario 755; eight tests in 0.99 s on the Mac (rustc 1.94.1). A `MAX_STATES` budget (5 M) aborts a scenario that outgrows the 30 s rule: three threads with five operations each did not finish in ten minutes. Design 17's table has the "Model-checked protocol" row, the source layout lists the file, plan task 13 has the Done text. Full host suite: 279 passed.
- Phase 2.5 task 5 DONE as design, commit d8225a8 (lane C, 2026-09-19). The probes ran in task 4 (`bench/results/2026-09-19-cpu-matrix.md` section 1: `mte=no` on Axion and Altra, `lam=no` on every x86 cell, `uai=no` on the Genoa guest, which hides it and LA57). `docs/design.md` section 18 "Tagged pointers" (source layout is 19 now): 18.1 the hardware table with confidence per vendor (AmpereOne bare metal confirmed by Ampere's documentation and measured numbers; Graviton4 and Cobalt 100 unconfirmed, expect no, since the same Neoverse V2 hides it on Axion; LAM on Granite Rapids/Sierra Forest with kernel 6.4+, no cloud guest known; UAI in Zen 4 silicon with no Linux enable path, the 2022 series unmerged; TBI on every arm64 host); 18.2 MTE: `PROT_MTE` on every commit `mprotect`, huge runs untagged, tag 0 excluded, the cache holds pointers whose tag equals the slot's so the pop is untouched, `free` does `ldg` + compare (abort on mismatch) + `irg` + `st2g`/`dc gva` retag + push, refills `ldg` per `Active` slot and tag zero picks at pop (`stzg`/`dc gzva` for `calloc`), `realloc`/`usable_size`/`sallocx` check, `mallctl` lookups classify only; `tag_check` `async` default (no faulting address, `SEGV_MTEAERR`), `asymm` (MTE3) recommended, `sync` for the tests; a `SIGSEGV` handler for the two MTE `si_code`s chaining everything else, best-effort under Ruby's own handler; fork copies tags with the page on COW; cost expected in `zero_on_free`'s envelope (+87%/+102% larson1, +55%/+183% valkey1, aarch64/x86_64) plus one `ldg` per free, so opt-in; the cheaper no-retag variant named for part 2 to price and what it loses; the consumer contract (no data in top bits, mm syscalls untagged, glibc strings MTE-safe). 18.3 the software tag: six bits at 57 on both architectures, `tag = gen(slot) ^ key[class]` from a per-slot byte tag map (8x the bitmap), verified by `free` (both paths, one byte load and store), `realloc`, `usable_size`, refills tag from the map, pop untouched; `arch_prctl(ARCH_ENABLE_TAGGED_ADDR, 6)` in init step 3 (`EBUSY` when multi-threaded, off with a reason); what is caught (stale free even after reuse, forged 63/64, cross-class 63/64) and not (loads and stores). 18.4 the strip: `a = p & addr_mask` from the hot `Shared` line, one `and`, same instructions with the key off; every pointer-taking entry point and `Heap::flush` strip, `class.rs` never sees a tag. 18.5 keys `tag_pointers` (`off`|`mte`|`lam`|`auto`) and `tag_check`, their `opt.` strings and the `Tagged pointers:` report line with reason; `challoc_features()` unchanged. 18.6 part 2's tests (four negative cases, fuzzer, both suites, calloc campaign, Valkey and Ruby, encode/strip host test), cost rows next to `zero_on_free`'s, the fork row, and the cheapest path. Design 14's "not attempted: MTE" replaced by the planned, hardware-gated paragraph; design 17 rows for MTE/LAM/UAI/LA57 point at 18; README security table row plus footnote (3) and the feature table's MTE/LAM cells; plan task 5 "Done as design".
- Phase 2.5 task 10: two-advice purge (089c465; test fix 9a19632; results c6e3823). Under the cgroup policy's `Low` band (usage under 70% of a known limit) `Empty` slabs and huge extents are returned with `MADV_FREE`, everywhere else (no limit, scaled and immediate bands, every explicit purge: `malloc_trim(0)`, `arena.<i>.purge`, the `high`-rise full purge) with `MADV_DONTNEED`; `lazy_free:true` is the override, new key `lazy_band` (default true) turns the saving off; the advice is read at each `Batch::flush` from `policy::evaluate`'s mirror of the band (`purge::set_headroom`). Init probes `MADV_FREE` through the `process_madvise` target the `MADV_DONTNEED` probe found (`Features::process_madvise_free`, feature-line key `process_madvise_free`, yes on both 7.0 reference kernels; lazy batches alone fall back to the `madvise` loop where it fails). `purged_is_zero()` is sticky (true until the first lazy batch of the process, published before the discard under the walk's lock); `class.rs`/`huge.rs` untouched. `stats.cgroup.purge_advice|lazy_purges|eager_purges` (readable without a tracked cgroup), `purge advice:` report line, JSON fields. `tests/c/cgroup_lazy.c` + `make test-cgroup-lazy` (in `make test` and `make test-cgroup`): 200 MiB freed at 39% of 512 MiB is purged lazily 80 ms after the frees, RSS/`memory.current` stay 202/205 MiB while `memory.stat` `pglazyfree` rises by 189 MiB, realloc of the same 200 MiB 5 ms vs 44 ms eager (C4A; 42 vs 72 ms C4), a fresh 380 MiB set makes the kernel reclaim 60..64 MiB of lazy pages (`pglazyfreed`, `current` 511/512, alive); the `lazy_band:false` control purges eagerly (RSS 13 MiB at 80 ms). `make test` green on both VMs at 9a19632; churn rows unchanged (their frees are at 80..90%: eager); oscillation 98/97 cycles, no OOM, as before. `ab` 5 rounds mstress/alloc-testN/larson10 in `--memory=8g` (base a3ddd6d, eager, lazy): every cell within noise (-1.3..+0.6% on arm64, -2.7..+0.0% on amd64); the same with `dirty_decay_ms:0`: mstress 1.002 -> 0.516 s (-48.5%, disjoint lists) on the C4A and 1.087 -> 0.915 s (-15.8%) on the C4, larson10 +3.4%/+1.0% throughput, alloc-testN 0.0%/-1.6%, with maxrss 15..59 MiB higher under the lazy advice (the pages stay charged until reclaim). `quick` vs a3ddd6d go on both VMs (worst row alloc-test1 +1.2% arm64, +0.6% amd64). Design 8, 10, 11, 12, 16, 17 and README updated. Files: `bench/results/2026-09-19-lane-b-two-advice-purge.md` (+ `-ab-{aarch64,x86_64}.md`, `-ab-decay0-{aarch64,x86_64}.md`).
- Phase 2.5 task 9: multi-size THP for slab regions behind `thp` (cc4aa34; results and the design's measured notes in the following commit). `Features` reads the kernel's THP table at init with raw syscalls (`enabled`, `hpage_pmd_size`, every `hugepages-<n>kB/enabled` from two pages to the PMD by path, `inherit` resolved; a listed size without an `enabled` file, `hugepages-8kB` on 7.0, is absent; pre-6.8 kernels list the PMD alone) and the feature line gains `thp=never|madvise|always|none mthp=64K,2M|none collapse=unprobed|no|<size>`; `MADV_COLLAPSE` is probed at the PMD size on a populated aligned range only when `thp` is not `never` (the syscall is a silent no-op below the PMD through 7.0, so the smallest enabled size cannot be probed; it costs a PMD of memory for a moment): `collapse=2M` on both 7.0 VMs, reported, not used. New `src/thp.rs` decides the mode from the key and the table: `thp:auto` (new value) advises each slab group (`take_group`, the group still `PROT_NONE`) of a class whose slab holds an enabled folio below the PMD, folio-aligns the metadata tables (`Lazy::reserve`) and rounds and advises their commits (`Lazy::ensure`, `meta::Inner::commit`, `commit_bump_to`), never a 2 MiB page, and refuses (reported) where no such size is enabled or the PMD size is `madvise`-gated; `thp:always` with the PMD size enabled commits and advises PMD-sized chunks in `commit_slab` (guard slabs inside a chunk left unused and unguarded: the first form's markers split every 2 MiB page, `AnonHugePages` 0 after 8 MiB touched, 10240 kB after the fix), advises huge runs of a PMD or more (`Huge::prepare`, design 7) and the metadata with the sub-PMD folio; `never` (default) issues nothing; design 11's forced-never under a limit below 1 GiB is mirrored after init. Report `thp:` line, JSON `"thp"`, `stats.thp.{mode,class_ranges,class_bytes,huge_ranges,meta_ranges,failures}`, `opt.thp` reads `auto`; `thp` leaves `INERT_KEYS`. Tests: host fixtures for the sysfs parsing (never/madvise/always, the 7.0 layout, 64K madvise with the PMD never, pre-6.8, missing directory, garbled), `thp::decide`, the chunk arithmetic (host 283 pass); `tests/c/thp.c never|always|auto` (VmFlags `hg`, THPeligible, AnonHugePages, the per-size `anon_fault_alloc` counter, `stats.thp.*`) and `tests/c/fork_cost.c` (a Valkey-shaped heap forks, the parent's burst is copied while the child is alive; other modes behind `CHALLOC_THP_TESTS=1`); `make test` 355/113/23 green on both lane VMs at cc4aa34, the gated tests green in the auto sysfs state. Measured (`bench/results/2026-09-19-lane-b-mthp.md` and the three `-ab-*.md`): on the C4A `auto` takes larson1's dTLB refills from 4.01 to 3.05 per operation (jemalloc 1.75), valkey1's from 2.81 to 2.44 (below jemalloc's 2.69), valkey1-free's from 3.91 to 2.90 (below 3.35), with cycles within noise; `fork()` of a 500 MiB heap 18.9 -> 15.5 ms on the C4A and 7.9 -> 10.2 ms on the C4, the copy-on-write burst identical in faults and within noise in time in every mode, fork-to-exit -6% / +2.5%; `ab` five rounds on the C4A: valkey1 -0.1%, redis -0.3%, mstress 3.5% faster, larson1 +1.7% and larson10 +1.6% worse with disjoint lists, RSS +14% / +7% / +19% on the 16..100 MiB heaps and +0.3% on valkey1; `always`: valkey1 +10% (C4A) / +15% (C4) throughput, dTLB 0.65 per operation, RSS +9.6% on valkey1 and 2..4x on small heaps, `fork()` 25x cheaper. `quick` go on both VMs under the default and under `auto`. Decision: `never` stays the default, `auto` is the documented opt-in (design 12, 17, README). Both `challoc-lane-b-*` VMs deleted.

- Phase 2.5 task 8 follow-up (lane A, 2026-09-20), the fast path of the
  mode, two mechanisms from the predecessor's attribution (the mode was
  instructions, not misses). (a) `ee0ecd8`: the per-CPU wrappers
  (`api::libc::percpu_malloc`, `percpu_free`, `percpu_free_sized`) inline
  the hit path (`Heap::percpu_pop_raw`, `heap::percpu_push_raw`) and test
  one bit of the section's raw status word (`rseq::pop_raw`/`push_raw`,
  `POP_*`/`PUSH_*`; `pop`/`push` decode); every miss is one cold tail
  call that finishes the operation (`percpu_*_miss`, `Heap::percpu_pop_miss`,
  `heap::percpu_push_miss`), so the wrappers are frameless with no call
  (aarch64: `percpu_malloc` 97 -> 66 instructions, `percpu_free` 120 ->
  106; `malloc`/`free` unchanged); the x86_64 pop compares `len` with
  `zeroed` in the asm; the `CLASSES` bounds check left the byte count
  (`class_size`). `bench/quick.sh` takes `NEW_CONF`/`OLD_CONF`. (b) (this
  commit): the run as a per-CPU header of its own, a second table
  `Percpu::runs` of `nr_cpu_ids` `[CacheHeader; NCLASSES]` (capacity
  `cap / 4`, `compact_mask == u32::MAX`) served by the existing sections:
  a scheduled pop in compact mode pops the id's run (`Heap::percpu_run_pop`),
  an exhausted run is rebuilt by one block claim from the lowest partial
  slab (`push_batch`, lowest on top, the zero prefix with it) instead of a
  locked one-slot claim per scheduled pop; the drain takes the run, the GC
  returns a run whose class stopped compacting; `stats.percpu.*` count
  both tables; test `percpu_scheduled_pops_come_from_a_run_claimed_a_block_at_a_time`
  (one automatic arena, id-change tolerant; its body verified with
  `--test-threads=1` on both VMs). Measured (`bench/results/2026-09-19-{aarch64,x86_64}-task8-percpu-fastpath.md`
  and the `-ab.md` files; `old` dfe3f60): per operation on the C4A / C4,
  the mode over thread caches on larson1 +73.7 / +55.8 -> +46.7 / +32.5
  instructions and +8.3 / +5.8 -> +4.1 / +3.8 cycles after (a); on the
  Valkey model +177 / +148 -> +78 / +58 instructions and +70 / +81 -> +28
  / +31 cycles after (a) + (b); `quick` (mode vs the base's default, median
  of 3): C4A larson1 +5.8%, larson10 +9.0%, xmalloc +19.8%, alloc-test1
  +4.9%, valkey1 +4.6%; C4 +5.9%, +22.4%, +16.5%, +2.6%, +39.3% (from
  +15.1/+18.7/+23.9/+10.2/+9.7 and +11.1/+18.9/+23.8/+8.2/+67.5); the
  churn probe compacts under the mode (97.0%); `ab` 5 rounds: C4A larson1
  +5.3%, larson10 +9.4%, xmalloc +18.9%, valkey1 +4.7%, alloc-testN +4.8%;
  C4 +3.7%, +16.6%, +16.1%, +37.8%, +3.9%. The shipped default (`auto`,
  thread caches) is `quick` GO on both VMs (C4A -0.7/+0.3/+1.2/-0.6/+1.1%,
  C4 -2.0/-0.2/+2.6/-0.3/-0.7%) and within noise on `ab` (C4A -0.7/-0.4/
  +0.5/-0.3/-0.7%, C4 -0.9/-0.9/-0.2/+0.3/+1.4%): the acceptance holds. The

- Phase 2.5 task 10 (lane B), floor fix: done, f78601e. Lane D's acceptance
  found the container churn (`purge 400 100` in 512 MiB) failing on the
  Debian cells (5.10 x86_64, 6.1 x86_64 and arm64; RSS 217..349 MiB at
  2 s). Reproduced on lane B's own lab cells at 9a9c635: 7 of 16 runs
  fail on 6.1 arm64 (c4a-standard-4), 5 of 16 on 5.10 x86_64
  (e2-standard-4), 0 of 16 on the 16-vCPU 7.0 lane VM. Mechanism, shown
  by the advice counters now on the churn's policy line (pre-fix library,
  new test: `lazy_purges 18 eager_purges 1` in a failing run, `22 / 3` in
  a passing one): the three classes' walks come due a few ms apart; when
  they straddle a periodic tick the first tick returns one class eagerly
  (the band was `Scaled` at 403 MiB), usage drops under 70%, the next
  sample puts the policy in `Low` and the remaining 200..300 MiB go out
  with `MADV_FREE`, resident and charged, the cap met in the allocator's
  accounting and not the cgroup's. Timing, not the kernel: `MADV_FREE`
  pages stay charged on every kernel. Fix: `Policy::eager_excess`, an
  excess over the retention cap first seen at 70% or above is returned
  eagerly until the retention is within the cap; `Policy::headroom` is
  what `purge::set_headroom` mirrors; `advice_reason` gains `returning
  the excess over the cap`. After: 24 of 24 on both lab cells, `make
  test-cgroup` (all limits, the lazy rows included) green on the 7.0 VM,
  the lazy test unchanged (an excess first seen under 70% stays lazy).
  Design 8 and 11. `bench/results/2026-09-19-lane-b-floor-fixes.md`
  sections 1, 2.

Both ways: `rseq=yes rseq_size=33 mm_cid=yes` on both lane VMs (7.0, glibc 2.44; `__rseq_offset` 320 aarch64 / -384 x86_64 in the test binary against the note's 192 / -192, so the symbol read is what makes it right), `rseq=yes rseq_size=20 mm_cid=no` on the Mac's 6.10 linuxkit VM. `make test` 376 + 115 + 23 on all three hosts. - Phase 2.5 task 15 (lane C): done, commit 2cd06e2. The rseq time slice extension for lock holders: `src/slice.rs` (new; the probe through lane A's `rseq::probe` plus bit 4 of this thread's area `flags` when `__rseq_size >= 32`, the per-thread `prctl(79, 2, 1)` confirmed by bit 5, `configure` from `config::load` on the init thread, `acquired`/`releasing`/`released` for the lock, the lazy per-thread enable on a thread's first hold, `rseq_slice_yield` (471, unified table, probed with `ENOSYS`) with `sched_yield` where a seccomp filter hides it, the counters and the report words), `src/lock.rs` (`RawLock::lock`/`try_lock` request after the CAS, `unlock` withdraws before the release swap and yields after it when granted; every `RawLock`, none on the fast path), `src/config.rs` (`slice_ext:auto|on|off`, default `auto`, `KEYS` 27, `Config::apply_slice_ext`), `src/api/mallctl.rs` (`cstr_of` knows `on`), `src/api/print.rs` (`Slice extension: on (N threads enabled, M grants yielded via <syscall>)` / `off (<why>)` with the refused-`on` note), `tests/c/slice_ext.c` and its driver `api::ctests::c_slice_ext_both_ways`, `slice::tests::{probe_both_ways_and_off_never_requests, grants_are_yielded_under_oversubscription, layout_constants}`, `config::tests::slice_ext_values`; design 12 (the key row), design 17 (the table row and the "Time slice extension" subsection), README's feature table, the research row corrected. Findings and numbers in `bench/results/2026-09-19-x86_64-lane-c-task15-slice-ext.md` and `2026-09-19-aarch64-lane-c-task15-slice-ext.md` (with the `ab` files `...-task15-ab.md`): Ubuntu's `7.0.0-1011-gcp` on both reference shapes has the option compiled out (`kernel-disabled`), arm64 cannot have it on any kernel through master (no `GENERIC_ENTRY`/`HAVE_GENERIC_TIF_BITS`), so the `on` way was measured on the lane C4 under a 7.0.14 built with `CONFIG_RSEQ_SLICE_EXTENSION=y`: the lock-wait path is 0.01..0.12% of samples on larson16/larson10/alloc-testN, holder preemptions are 17 per 10^9 larson16 operations and 0 on the other two (34 and 58 at 2x and 4x oversubscription), instructions per operation identical, cycles within 0.3%, `quick` go on both VMs, `ab` 5 rounds within noise on both (x86_64 +1.8% / -1.8% / +0.8%, aarch64 +0.7% / -0.3% / +0.0%, every sorted pair overlapping), `auto` against `off` on one binary within noise oversubscribed. `make test` green on both lane VMs (393 + 117 + 23 each; the C4 with the extension on, the C4A on the fallback) and 306 on the host.
- Phase 2.5 task 8 (lane C, the model): done, commit 01adade. `src/model.rs` models the per-CPU claim protocol of design 15 so that lane A's implementation has verification before it lands: `Scenario::percpu` turns on two caches owned by a concurrency id (cache `c` bound to arena `c`), each an array plus `len` and the `zeroed` prefix of design 6.3; `Op::Pop` and `Op::Push(i)` are rseq critical sections (`mm_cid` read and the cache picked, the speculative reads of `len` and the top entry, one commit store) composed with the existing `Refill` (an empty cache refills from the cid's arena, the batch pushed back through a second section on whatever cid the thread is on then, the overflow released) and `Release` (a full cache flushes half through a section, then `release_routed`: each slab through its owner word's arena, a reopen per owner run) machines. `Variant::section` is `Rseq` (the protocol), `None` (plain loads and stores under the cid last read) or `CidOutside` (`mm_cid` read before the section); `Variant::strict_cid` checks that a cache is only mutated by a section whose thread kept its cid to the commit. New invariants: no slot in two caches or in a cache and owned by a thread (the holders table now covers caches, owned and staged slots), every set bit has exactly one holder once a zero pick's prefix bits reach their thread (a lost push is a bit with none), the zero prefix reads zero, the stale-commit flag. Scenarios: `percpu_share` (three threads on two cids: 163125 states, 3.6 s), `percpu_migrate` (a thread pops twice and pushes later, 4030 cross-cid pushes counted: 66746 states, 1.1 s), `percpu_purge` (direct free, purge, the `Purged` slab's zero pick reaching a cache's zero prefix, checked by a coverage probe: 241740 states, 4.5 s); `None` hands slab 0 slot 0 to threads 1 and 2 in 39 states (18 steps printed: T1's speculative read, T2's entry on the same cid marks T1 descheduled, both commit), `CidOutside` in 35 (T2's entry aborts T1, which retries at the section's start with the stale index and commits). 13 model tests in 8.6 s on the host (`cargo test --features std model::`), 9.2 s on Linux aarch64 in the dev container; the whole host suite 303 passed. Design 15 gained the paragraph listing the properties an implementation must satisfy, design 17's model row and plan task 13's Done paragraph point at the scenarios.

  of "Acceptance for the phase" with the evidence, the verdict and what
  remains, all at 675f357 on the lane's own VMs (`challoc-lane-d-*`, nine

- Phase 2.5 task 8 (lane A, 2026-09-20), the rseq probe test hardened:
  done, commit 8ea835a (97ad715 before the rebase onto main 2a0fffb). `rseq::tests::probe_reads_the_registered_area`
  spawned as many spinners as CPUs and asserted more than one `mm_cid`
  after 50 ms, which depended on the scheduler spreading them: lane E read
  `1 distinct mm_cid values over 8 busy threads` on the first `make test`
  of every Chainguard OS cell (8 vCPUs, podman default profile; 45/45
  alone, 8/9 in the whole binary, 540/0 under `--privileged`:
  `bench/results/2026-09-20-chainguard-os-cells.md` section 3). Now one
  thread per CPU of the allowed mask (`sched_getaffinity`, at most 64),
  each pinned with `sched_setaffinity`, all reading `cpu_id` and `mm_cid`
  until every pinned thread has shown its own id or two seconds pass;
  deterministic on both `mm_cid` implementations (6.18's recycled ids,
  6.19+'s per-task/per-CPU rewrite): a pinned thread's `cpu_id` reads its
  CPU on every load (the hard assertion: the probe reads the registered,
  kernel-written area) and two pinned threads running at once hold two
  ids. Where the check cannot be made (one CPU allowed, a pin refused, or
  no two spinners overlapped in the window) the test prints the reason
  and skips that half, never passes it. Reference VMs: `16 distinct
  mm_cid values over 16 threads pinned to CPUs [0..15]` on both (`make
  test` 408 + 120 + 23, `EXIT 0`, both VMs); six whole-binary runs on the
  x86_64 VM restricted to CPUs 0-7 under 16 busy loops on the same CPUs
  (the cell's shape) read `8 distinct` 6/6; the Mac's Docker VM takes the
  `no-mm_cid` branch. The Chainguard OS cells themselves are lane E's to
  re-run (no cgos host was reachable from this lane).

- Phase 2.5 task 7 (lane A, 2026-09-20), the T2A `make test` SIGILL in

  aarch64 branch prints the reason and asserts `fpac()` false. The

- Phase 2.5 task 8 (lane A, 2026-09-20), `pac_pointers:true` with
  `percpu_arena:percpu` SIGILL on FEAT_FPAC (lane D's campaign, section
  1.2; acceptance row 4a): done, commit 11155f3. The fault was in
  `rseq::push_batch`'s aarch64 section: the modifier register was
  advanced to the write position (`slots + len * 8`) before the `pacda`,
  so a batch pushed onto a non-empty stack (a free on the same CPU
  between a refill's empty pop and its push, or a flush's push-back onto
  a stack another thread had pushed to) was signed under another
  modifier than the stack base every pop and `take_all` authenticate
  with; the section now keeps the base in its own register and stores
  through a second one. Tests: `heap::tests::percpu_batch_onto_a_non_
  empty_stack_authenticates` (the sections driven directly on a fresh
  `Shared` of the mode; skipped without `mm_cid`), `c_tls_threads_with_
  pac_pointers` now runs `tls_threads` under the key with and without
  `percpu_arena:percpu`, `c_percpu_both_ways` adds the mode with
  `pac_pointers:true`; `run_bin_env` clears `CHALLOC_CONF` for the C
  programs (section 1.1 of lane D's file: a suite run under the
  environment key turned every both-ways test one way). Verified on the
  C4A: `make test` green and `tests/c/percpu percpu` / `tls_threads`
  under both keys five and six times each (the results file, section 7);
  the thread path did not change (the section's other instructions are
  the same; the x86_64 section has no PAC). Follow-up 0823a00: the unit
  test builds its own `Shared` with `pac_pointers:true` (`shared_percpu`
  leaves the key off, so its first run drove the sections plain).

- Phase 2.5 task 15 (lane C, test fix): the `slice::tests` on a kernel
  with `CONFIG_RSEQ_SLICE_EXTENSION` on, `5c421f4`. Lane E's cgos72-amd64
  cell (Chainguard OS kernel 7.2.3, the first host in the matrix with the
  option; `bench/results/2026-09-20-chainguard-os-cells.md`, "At a999097
  (third run)") found two faults in the tests, invisible on the reference
  VMs (Ubuntu 7.0 has the option compiled out): the teardown
  `configure(before.0 != Key, false)` turned the module on when a test ran
  alone (module `Unprobed`, off, a state `configure` cannot reproduce), so
  `assert_eq!(on(), before.1)` failed 7 runs of 10 and only the whole
  binary, where `config::load` had already turned it on, hid it; and
  `grants_are_yielded_under_oversubscription` counted coincidences (32
  threads on one lock, 20 us holds, a 3 s window: 0..6 yields in ~10k
  holds, zero on 3 runs of 13) because an unlock that wakes a sleeper ends
  the grant with its own `FUTEX_WAKE` before `released` reads `granted`.
  Now the tests snapshot and restore the switches themselves (`STATE`,
  `VERDICT`, `FORCED`; `PROBED`, `OFFSET`, `YIELD_SCHED` stay by design)
  and are order-independent, and the grants test makes the scheduler want
  to preempt every holder (two hogs per CPU, a hold run past the slice on
  the thread's own lock, no syscall inside) while the holder watches its
  own `granted` byte and releases inside the grant; bounded in holds and
  work, not seconds; a kernel with the option that grants nothing prints a
  skip line, never a pass, and the request path is asserted on regardless.
  Verified on a `matrix lab cgos72-amd64` VM under the lane's prefix
  (`challoc-lane-c-matrix-cgos72-amd64`, c4-standard-8, 7.2.3-r0-desktop-7.2,
  `slice_ext_nsec=5000`, `preempt=(full) lazy`), inside podman under the
  default profile: the `slice::` tests alone 10 of 10 passed, each run 23
  holds, 22..23 granted during the hold (after 436..1920 rounds of 100
  multiply-adds, i.e. inside the first slice), 22..23 yielded via
  `sched_yield`, 0.06..0.47 s; the whole `make test` once with the same
  code 410 + 120 + 23 passed, 0 failed, every churn/lazy `verdict=ok`
  (the test inside the whole binary: 23 granted, 23 yielded); one
  `--privileged` run alone: 23 granted, 23 yielded via `rseq_slice_yield`.
  Mac container (6.10, no extension): `slice::` alone 3 passed and the
  whole `make test` 410 + 120 + 23 passed, 0 failed, the test reading
  `320 holds ... 0 with the request set, 0 granted ... state
  no-slice_ctrl`. Logs (not committed): `.tmp-matrix-lab/2026-09-20-cgos72-amd64-{slice10,maketest-summary}.log`
  in the lane worktree. VM deleted (`matrix clean`, `delete all`);
