- Phase 2.5 task 8: the section-abort test proves the retry or skips with
  the host's shape (2e5bec6). Lane D's k68-amd64 flake
  (`bench/results/2026-09-20-phase-2.5-acceptance.md` 1b.2 (c), "no
  section was aborted under 23636 signals") was a probabilistic bound: the
  test inferred a signal inside a section from the process-wide
  `rseq::aborts()` delta after a fixed 400 ms. Now the `SIGUSR1` handler
  (`SA_SIGINFO`) reads the interrupted program counter from its ucontext,
  filled after the kernel's rseq fixup, and counts a signal as an aborted
  section exactly when the four bytes before that counter are `rseq::SIG`
  (the word before every `abort_ip`): the worker's own witness, no
  contribution from other tests or plain preemptions. The signaller keeps
  the 400 ms stress window, extends it only while no abort was witnessed
  (cap 4 s), then prints `percpu: skip: no abort in N signals (D
  delivered) over M ms on K allowed CPUs (U usable, P possible), the
  worker ran W ms for O operations: cannot prove the retry path here` and
  returns; slot conservation is asserted on every run, and where aborts
  are witnessed the abort handlers' counter (94fa9aa) must have moved.
  Same asm on both `mm_cid` implementations; the fixup at signal delivery
  predates both. Verified on the C4 (7.0-1011-gcp): `make test` green
  (410 + 120 unit/api, cgroup, churn, lazy, pagemap), the test alone ten
  times in a container held to 2 CPUs (2064..2281 signal-aborted
  sections each, equal to the process-wide count), and once on 1 CPU
  showing the flake's shape (68 of 39138 signals delivered, the worker
  ran 200 of 400 ms, 2 sections caught). Also in 2e5bec6, from lane C's
  7.2 Chainguard OS run:
  `api::mallctl::tests::arena_keys_create_lookup_purge_decay_and_destroy`
  raced another api test's `arenas.create` between the `arenas.narenas`
  read and `arena.{narenas+1}.purge` (the moved count's new all-arenas
  alias answers 0); the pair is retried until the count did not move
  across it and asserts ENOENT then, the far-range probes unchanged.
- Phase 2.5 task 11 (lane B, test fix): `purge::tests::prefault_populate_and_wakes_are_counted`
  follows the kernel's page size and the `MADV_POPULATE_WRITE` probe (51f2cae).
  Lane D's reading (bench/results/2026-09-20-phase-2.5-acceptance.md, "At a999097",
  row 1) confirmed in the code: the test hard-coded `page = 4096` and
  `reset_for_test(true, true)` forced the populate word on whatever the probe
  said, so on k510-amd64 (5.10, no `MADV_POPULATE_WRITE`, which is 5.14) and on
  64k-arm64 (a 4 KiB-aligned address is not page-aligned) the `madvise` failed
  with EINVAL and `fallbacks` read 1 against the asserted 0. The library is
  right (`api::init` hands the probe's `features.populate_write` to
  `prefault::configure`; the pass populates page-aligned slabs; the touch
  fallback counts and completes), so purge.rs changes only in its tests: a
  cfg'd `prefault_env()` reads `sys::features::page_size()` and runs the real
  `probe_populate_write(page)` on Linux (4 KiB and "no" off Linux, where
  `os::populate_write` always fails), the pass is configured in the probe's
  direction and asserts no fallback there (the advice works, or the touch is
  the populate), then a third leg forces the populate word and asserts one
  fallback with the bytes still counted exactly where the probe said no (the
  touch fallback contract), a zero buffer zero throughout; the wake
  assertions are unchanged; the test prints the probed direction and the
  forced leg's count. The advice test's misplaced doc comment moved back
  onto `advice_follows_headroom_band_key_and_explicit_purges`.
  Verified: host `cargo test --features std` 318/0 (`page_size=4096
  populate_write=no (probed)`, forced `fallbacks=1 (expected 1)`); Mac
  container `make test` 553/0 (410 + 120 + 23; 6.10, `page_size=4096
  populate_write=yes (probed)`, forced `fallbacks=0 (expected 0)`);
  `challoc-bench matrix k510-amd64 64k-arm64` under `challoc-lane-b`:
  k510-amd64 (Debian 11, 5.10.0-46-cloud-amd64, 4 KiB) `tests=553/0 fuzz=PASS
  cgroup=PASS outcome=PASS`, the test printing `page_size=4096
  populate_write=no (probed)` and forced `fallbacks=1 (expected 1)`;
  64k-arm64 (Ubuntu 24.04, 6.8.0-139-generic-64k, 64 KiB) `tests=553/0
  fuzz=PASS cgroup=PASS outcome=PASS`, printing `page_size=65536
  populate_write=yes (probed)` and forced `fallbacks=0 (expected 0)`
  (bench/results/matrix/2026-09-20-lane-b-{k510-amd64,64k-arm64}.log; the
  printed lines were read off the cells' `~/logs/test.log` before deletion,
  the fetched log keeps only the tail). Cells deleted, no lane-b VM remains.

  four workers at 1.0 together under both allocators, so throughput is clock

  write phase (761,992 vs 731,845 req/s, 912,526 vs 916,554; p99 -2.5% /

  vs 187 per memtier SET, 153 vs 86 per DEL, the bitmap-word load of the

- (e) 6d1f618: `scripts/check-needed.sh <binary> <soname>...` (readelf, exit 1
  when missing, prints NEEDED and RUNPATH), wired into contrib/valkey/build.sh
  (libchalloc.so.0) and contrib/ruby/build.sh (libjemalloc.so.2 on ruby and
  libruby.so); `make check-needed` = tests/as-needed.sh (tests/c/as_needed.c,
  as_needed_lib.c) on both lane VMs, green; the truth in design 13.6 and
  bench/results/2026-09-19-as-needed.md: Wolfi's gcc is --as-needed by
  default; an executable referencing malloc keeps DT_NEEDED (malloc resolves in
  libchalloc before libc); one referencing nothing drops it; the two-level
  shape (executable allocates only through a library that needs libchalloc)
  drops the executable's entry and the library's malloc binds to glibc for the
  whole process, silently (BFS search order puts libc.so.6 first); Ruby escapes
  it with -Wl,--no-as-needed in LDFLAGS (ruby/ruby#4627). Package rule: link
  with --no-as-needed (or reference the allocator) and run the checker in the
  package test on every executable and the allocating library.

## In progress

## Design decisions

- Phase 2.5 task 8 (lane A, 2026-09-19), the shape: the per-CPU caches are
  a table of `nr_cpu_ids` `[CacheHeader; NCLASSES]` arrays (`CpuCache`,
  3.3 KiB per id) reserved zeroed from the metadata region at init and
  installed header by header on first use; every header mutation (pop,
  push, batch push, take-all, install) is an rseq critical section in
  `asm!` on both architectures that reads `mm_cid` itself and commits with
  one store of `len` (or `cap` for the install). Reason: a zeroed header
  (`len == cap == 0`) is the slow path by construction, so the table costs
  no init work; sharing `CacheHeader` keeps one stack layout for the thread
  and per-CPU paths (PAC signing included); and a section per mutation
  means no lock and no atomic read-modify-write joins the fast paths, and
  two threads never touch one header at once (an id has one running
  holder; a preempted holder is aborted and retries).
- Phase 2.5 task 8, the thread heap stays under the mode: it is the init
  witness the fast paths already load, it keeps the byte counters (every
  per-CPU pop and push is counted there per operation, `thread.allocated`
  exact), its home caches serve `MALLOCX_ARENA` calls and huge runs, and
  a heap off the mode (explicit caches, `thread.tcache.enabled:false`, a
  thread `thread.arena`d onto an explicit arena) takes the thread paths.
  Reason: `tls_threads.c` and `jemalloc_api.c` check the counters to the
  byte, and a cache shared by a CPU's threads cannot be folded into one
  thread's figure; the alternative (approximate counters) breaks the
  jemalloc contract for two loads on a line the mode touches anyway.
- Phase 2.5 task 8, arenas per id: the header of `(id, class)` binds to
  arena `id % narenas` at install (with the default `narenas`, `4 *
  ncpus` capped at 64, one arena per CPU) and refills claim there; a
  plain `free` pushes a verified slot of any arena onto the running CPU's
  cache and skips the owner word, the flush routing it back through
  `release_routed`. Reason: design 6.3's sized path already caches slots
  of any arena in one stack and routes at the flush, so the rule and the
  code exist; the owner load leaves the `free` fast path.
- Phase 2.5 task 8, compaction keeps its gate and loses its run: the
  per-header probe/compact modes of design 6.4 are kept (volatile hint
  stores, a stale mode changes only which pops are scheduled), and a
  scheduled pop in compact mode claims one slot straight from the arena's
  lowest partial slab instead of taking the run's lowest entry. Reason:
  the run pop moves the top entry into the run's slot and shrinks two
  words, which no single commit store can make consistent under an abort
  (a partial move duplicates one entry and loses another); the direct
  claim lands the allocation where the run would have, at one lock hold
  per scheduled pop in fragmented classes only.
- Phase 2.5 task 8, the flush takes the whole stack out (`take_all`),
  selects the highest-address half on the thread's stack buffer exactly as
  `Heap::flush` does (sorted split or `select_nth_unstable`, the straddle
  compare, `group_by_slab`), releases it and pushes the kept half back
  (`push_batch`); a refill claims onto the stack and pushes in one
  section. Capacities are the thread ladder clamped to `MAX_CAP` (1024),
  so the buffers are 8 KiB and 4 KiB stack arrays. Reason: the selection
  cannot run in place on a header another thread may own by the time it
  finishes, and the two sections cost a copy each; `tcache_nslots_small_max`
  above 1024 applies to thread caches only (documented).
- Phase 2.5 task 8, `double_free_scan:true` keeps thread caches
  (`PercpuState::OffScan`, the report says so). Reason: the scan of the
  top four entries is not written into the critical sections; the
  hardening a deployment asked for wins over the mode.
- Phase 2.5 task 8, `thread.arena` under the mode: an explicit arena takes
  the thread onto its own caches there (`Heap::percpu` false), an
  automatic one puts it back on the CPU caches; jemalloc returns `EPERM`
  for the automatic range under `percpu_arena`. Reason: nothing here needs
  refusing (the home arena of a per-CPU thread serves huge runs and
  explicit calls only), and `jemalloc_api.c` restores `thread.arena` to
  its automatic home and expects 0.
- Phase 2.5 task 8, the cache GC, `thread.tcache.flush`, `malloc_trim` and
  the purges' caller flush act on the caches of the CPU the thread runs
  on; an idle CPU's caches wait for the next thread to run there and tick;
  thread exit leaves the CPU caches alone. Reason: owning another id's
  header from outside a section would need `membarrier(RSEQ)` per drain;
  the residue is at most `cap` slots per (id, class) and is exactly what
  the mode bounds by cores rather than threads.
- Phase 2.5 task 8, the decision is made in `Shared::init` behind an
  `allowed` parameter (the API passes true, the unit tests' `Shared`
  builders false, `shared_percpu()` true), and the mode is never on where
  `rseq::probe` says `mm_cid` is unusable whatever the key. Reason: the
  heap tests reason about thread caches and must not flip on the VMs; a
  forced request on a kernel that does not write `mm_cid` would read 0 for
  every thread and let two CPUs mutate one header.
- Phase 2.5 task 8, the table has `nr_cpu_ids` entries
  (`/sys/devices/system/cpu/possible`), not the affinity mask's width, and
  an `mm_cid` at or above it aborts (`cid_out_of_range`). Reason: the
  kernel bounds `mm_cid` by `nr_cpu_ids`, not by the mask (`taskset` and
  cpusets shrink the mask, not the id space); an id past the table means
  the sizing rule is wrong for this kernel, which is a heap inconsistency,
  not a fallback case. An unreadable file keeps thread caches
  (`PercpuState::OffCpus`).
- Phase 2.5 task 8, `Percpu` sits after `regions` in `Shared`. Reason: the
  thread paths' first line (`multi_arena`, `push_flags`, the `Regions`
  head) is byte for byte what it was; the per-CPU paths read `base` and
  `n` from the line after the class bases.
- Phase 2.5 task 8 (lane A, 2026-09-19), the default: `auto` keeps thread
  caches (`heap::PercpuState::OffAuto`, the report says "off (auto: opt in
  with percpu_arena:percpu)") and `percpu` is the opt-in; the code of the
  mode is byte for byte what `auto` ran in the measurements. Numbers
  (`quick`, `new` = the mode on, `old` = 9351eeb, positive is worse; C4A /
  C4): larson1 +15.1% / +11.1%, larson10 +18.7% / +18.9%, xmalloc +23.9%
  / +23.8%, alloc-test1 +10.2% / +8.2%, valkey1 +9.7% / +67.5%; RSS up on
  larson (16 -> 19 MiB) and xmalloc (53 -> 75 MiB on the C4A); the churn
  probe compacts (97.5%). NO-GO on every row, so the plan's own fallback
  rule ("default-on when present is allowed only if quick and ab are
  within noise") decides, and the human decides the final default with
  the `ab` tables (`bench/results/2026-09-19-*-task8-percpu.md`). Why in
  `heap.rs` and not in the key's default: `src/config.rs` is lane C's file
  (the one-line change is under Notes for the human).
- Phase 2.5 task 8 follow-up (lane A, 2026-09-20), the fast-path form:
  the per-CPU wrapper the entry point tail-calls inlines the section and
  returns on a hit; every miss is one cold tail call that completes the
  operation, and the sections hand back a raw status word rather than an
  enum. Reason: the frame and the decode were the cost, not the section
  (a wrapper that called the section's function and matched its enum
  opened a frame, decoded an `Option`, and bounds-checked `CLASSES`: +65..83
  instructions per larson operation; the same section inlined with cold
  tail-call misses is +33..47, the floor of the design as written: the
  descriptor store, the `mm_cid` load and range check, the table index,
  three loads, the status bit and the 7-instruction byte count). A tail
  call is what keeps `malloc`/`free` frameless in thread mode, so the mode
  cannot be inlined into the entry point itself without the frame the
  first form had; the wrapper is where the section belongs.
- Phase 2.5 task 8 follow-up (lane A, 2026-09-20), the run under the mode
  is a second per-CPU table, not the bottom of the stack. Reason: the
  thread run's pop moves the top entry into the hole, two words no single
  commit store can make consistent under an abort, which is why the landed
  form claimed one slot under the lock per scheduled pop (most of the
  Valkey model's cost); a run that is a `CacheHeader` of its own needs no
  new section (`pop`, `push_batch`, `take_all`, `install` on the second
  table), keeps the thread rebuild's shape (a block of `cap / 4` from the
  lowest partial slab, one lock hold per block, the class re-checked), and
  carries the zero prefix so `calloc` stays honest. The cost is `cap / 4`
  more slots of residue per (id, class) in compact mode and 3.3 KiB per
  id of zeroed table; the run of a class that stops compacting is returned
  by the GC.
- Phase 2.5 task 8 follow-up (lane A, 2026-09-20), the default after the
  follow-up: `auto` still keeps thread caches. Reason: the plan's rule
  (default-on only if `quick` and `ab` are within noise on both reference
  VMs) still fails: after both mechanisms the mode is at larson1 +5.3% /
  +3.7%, larson10 +9.4% / +16.6%, xmalloc +18.9% / +16.1%, valkey1 +4.7% /
  +37.8%, alloc-testN +4.8% / +3.9% on `ab` (C4A / C4), half or less of
  what it was but not noise; the brief says record how far and stop. The
  one-line flip in `src/config.rs` (lane C's file) is not made; Notes for
  the human say what is left.
- Phase 2.5 task 8 (lane A, 2026-09-19), the eight properties lane C's
  model states (design 15 at merge), checked against the code: (1)
  `mm_cid` is read inside the armed section and the header address is
  derived from it alone (`src/rseq.rs`: the `off_cid` load right after
  label `2:` and the `imul`/`madd` with `STRIDE` in every section, e.g.
  `pop` x86_64 lines 365-368, aarch64 438-442); (2) one commit store, the
  `len` write, ends every section (`pop` 386 / 463, `push` 552 / 619,
  `push_batch` 727 / 807, `take_all` the `len = 0` store; `install`
  commits `cap` instead, its one published word); (3) the abort handler
  is `jmp 8b` / `b 8b` back to the `rseq_cs` store before the section
  start, so a retry re-arms and re-reads `mm_cid` (393 / 469 and the same
  in each section); (4) the only store into the rseq area is `rseq_cs`
  (`off_cs`, 363 / 436; no other `[{rs} + ...]` store exists, `grep
  '\[{rs}' src/rseq.rs`); (5) the refill claims outside any section and
  pushes the batch through `push_batch` on the current id, releasing
  what did not fit (`src/heap.rs` `percpu_alloc_slow`, lines 3012-3028);
  the flush takes the stack out with `take_all`, selects on the thread's
  buffer, releases the high half and pushes the kept half back through
  `push_batch`, releasing the overflow (`percpu_flush`, 3143-3182);
  nothing is dropped; (6) every release goes through
  `Shared::release_routed` (3028, 3165, 3182), which routes each slab
  run by the owner word; (7) the zero prefix: `push_batch` sets `zeroed`
  to the batch when the stack was empty and the claim was zero
  (`claim.zeroed` at 3021), `pop` reads `zeroed` inside the section and
  reports `len < zeroed`, `push` clips it inside the section; the flush's
  push-back passes `zeroed = false` (3175), which is design 6.3's "dropped
  by a flush", so the prefix survives refill, pop and push and is given up
  at a flush, as with thread caches; (8) the fallback is the thread-cache
  protocol untouched: `percpu_free_fast` and `pop_percpu_mode` take the
  thread path for a heap off the mode (3442, 2843) and the entry points
  take the old inline path when `PUSH_PERCPU` is clear (`src/api/libc.rs`
  `malloc`, `free`, `free_sized_hint`), whose disassembly is unchanged.
  One property the model may want stated: the sections' pre-commit stores
  (`allocs`, `low_water`, `zeroed`, the slot above `len`) are harmless
  under an abort, which is what makes (2) sufficient.
- Phase 2.5 task 8, `tests/c/tunables.c` runs with `percpu_arena:off` in
  every mode. Reason: its `dirty` and `random`/`ordered` modes assert one
  thread's LIFO reuse and one refill batch from at most two slabs, which
  the per-CPU mode does not promise to a thread that migrates between two
  calls (it holds by construction with thread caches).

- Phase 2.5 task 4d (2026-09-19): the post-delete RSS criterion fails at 64 KiB pages (224.9 vs 146.1 MiB) by the mechanism design 5 already named, `Empty` 4..16 KiB slabs in mixed 64 KiB page groups, now 79.5 MiB at Valkey's scale against ~2 MiB in the oscillation test; so the sub-page slab geometry is not documented as fine on large pages, 4d closes as a measurement and the allocator-side fix is task 4e (page-sized slabs when the page is larger than the slab, jemalloc's geometry at LG_PAGE 16; refill steering the fallback), with this soak as its acceptance. The delete phase's +1.7..2.4% is read as at the Phase 2 acceptance (the usable-size query residue), not as a page-size finding: both allocators run 7% fewer cycles per command at 64 KiB. No `src/` change in 4d.
- Phase 2.5 task 4c (2026-09-19), a configured `guard_slab_interval` that is not a power of two rounds *up* to the next one and is reported as a configuration issue (`Issue::Rounded`, its own message naming the rounded value), which `abort_conf` turns into an abort like the 1 -> 2 clamp; `opt.guard_slab_interval` returns the rounded value. Reason: the sized fast path's guard test must be a mask (the runtime `%` was the largest single term of the path's instruction window on small-L3 hosts), rounding up keeps at least the guard density the operator asked for, and reporting it keeps `abort_conf` honest about a value the allocator did not take literally.
- Phase 2.5 task 4c (2026-09-19), the sized fast path no longer reads the class table's watermark (`is_handed_out`): a sized free of a slot start in a slab no arena has been handed is cached and aborts by name at the flush (`SlabTable::owner` on a slab past the watermark, the check `release_owned` already makes), the same way a slot in a handed-out but uncommitted (`Fresh`) slab already did (design 6.3). Until the flush such a slot can be popped by `malloc` and a program that writes to it faults on the reserved `PROT_NONE` page instead of aborting by name. Reason: the watermark was the path's only use of the 384-byte class table line, and dropping it let the whole guard test move to a dense 8-byte per-class pair; the exact `free`, `malloc_usable_size` and the flush keep every abort the negative tests expect. The task's "under 330 instructions per SET" target is not reached (344.6 on the C4 from 384.6; jemalloc 268.2): the terms left are named in the results file (the frame the `double_free_scan` path's `cached_twice` call keeps, 14 per SET), and the acceptance the task is judged on (the C3 lab's write phase within 10% of jemalloc, `valkey1-free` unchanged, `quick` go, scorecards) is met, so the task is marked done with the count recorded rather than left open on the target.

- Phase 2.5 task 4b (2026-09-19), attribution before any fast-path change, and the rule the attribution adds: a fast-path change to `src/heap.rs`/`src/class.rs`/`src/api/*.rs` aimed at the Valkey rows is judged on instructions per SET as much as on misses. The write phase of the model has one dependent DRAM miss per SET and overlaps consecutive SETs' misses only while both fit the core's window; challoc's 391 instructions per SET against jemalloc's 266 sit at the knee (350..430 on Golden Cove, lower on Zen 3/4 and Cascade Lake), so on hosts whose L3 does not hold the 32 MiB dict table the extra instructions cost a DRAM latency, not 20 cycles (`bench/results/2026-09-19-8481c-valkey1-attribution.md`). Reason for recording it as a rule: the reference C4 (5 MiB L3 per core) hides it, so a change that is neutral on the reference can be +30% on a C3/C3D/N2D/C2 VM; `quick-perf`'s instr/op column and the `valkey_model -x K` cliff are the instruments, and the two `divl` of `is_guard_slab` on the sized path are the first thing to remove (task 4c). PMU access for these CPUs on GCE is bare metal only (`c3-standard-192-metal`, about $10/hour, 11 minutes to ssh, deleted within the hour).

- Phase 2.5 task 4 (2026-09-19), the per-CPU scorecard file is named by machine family (`bench/results/scorecard-<family>.md`: c4a, t2a, c4, c3, c3d, n2d, c2, n4), the CPU model, zone and vCPU count in the note column. Reason: a family's CPU can vary by zone (N2D gives Milan or Rome), so the family is the stable key and the model is data; the two reference files keep their architecture names. CPU cells skip `make fuzz` and `make test-cgroup` (the kernel matrix of task 3 covers them; the plan's per-CPU list is the probe line, `make test` and one scorecard).
- Phase 2.5 task 4 (2026-09-19), a matrix cell names a zone list tried in order, at most two entries: families live in different zones (T2A only in us-central1) and a zone can be out of capacity or quota for a family; a cell with no zone left is recorded `outcome=PENDING reason=create:<error>` rather than FAIL.
- Phase 2.5 task 4 (2026-09-19), the container test's five-level-paging check applies only when the CPU reports the LA57 bit: `/proc/cpuinfo`'s `address sizes: ... 57 bits virtual` is cpuid 0x80000008's address width, which a GCE Genoa guest prints while the hypervisor hides LA57, so its kernel runs four-level paging and the hinted `mmap` lands below 2^47; the library's `la57_enabled=no` was right and the test's reading wrong.
- Phase 2.5 task 3 (2026-09-19), the oscillation's target on large pages (`tests/c/cgroup_oscillate.c`, design 5 and 11): when the page is larger than 4 KiB and HIGH is not given, the default target is 480 - 8 = 472 MiB (`LARGE_PAGE_ENVELOPE_MIB`), printed at start; 4 KiB unchanged. Reason: measured, the 64 KiB top of the same object sequence is 5 MiB of anon and 2 MiB of page tables higher with the allocator's `active` identical and nothing purgeable at the top (`bench/results/2026-09-19-matrix.md` F1 resolved), so the failure was the test's 32 MiB envelope absorbing page rounding that no policy setting can discharge; the only allocator-side remedies are page-sized slabs for the sub-page classes or refill steering by page-group density, both fast-path work not undertaken here. The allocator's own share of the 64 KiB excess is 3 MiB of 480 (metadata tables +1, stuck sub-page `Empty` +2), now recorded in design 5 as the trade-off of sub-page slabs on a large-page kernel. Also decided: `challoc-bench matrix lab|ssh|sync` keeps one cell VM for such measurements (log under `.tmp-matrix-lab/`), since the reference VMs cannot boot a 64 KiB kernel.
- Phase 2.5 task 1 (2026-09-18), Python rows are fixed stdlib scripts, not pyperformance: `pip install pyperformance` (1.14.0) works in the container on both VMs (recorded in `bench/python-bench.sh`'s header), but its standard set is 100 benchmarks driven by pyperf's own worker-process and repetition protocol, about an hour per interpreter configuration, and this task has ten configurations (five allocators, two `PYTHONMALLOC` modes) times five rounds; it also cannot give one process per row under `bench/rt` (wall time and peak RSS) with the allocators interleaved per round, which is the harness's protocol. The plan's fallback is used: `bench/probes/python/{json,dict_list,regex,object_graph}_bench.py`, each 2..4 s, deterministic, one process per row. The scorecard's `python1`/`python1-malloc` rows are the `json` workload (encode/decode of a 7 MB structure: the serialiser buffers and the text always go through malloc, the decoded objects only with `PYTHONMALLOC=malloc`). Wolfi has no free-threaded 3.13 package (`*freethread*`, `*3.13t*`, `*nogil*` searched), so there is no third variant. The scorecard header grew by two columns after `redis`; `bench/scorecard.sh` migrates an existing file (header, separator, older lines padded with `-`) on its next run and pads in memory when rendering.
- Phase 2 task 2 (2026-09-18), what `mseal` seals: only the never-committed parts of the three reservations (`Regions::never_committed`: the gap on either side of every class region as one contiguous range per gap, `NCLASSES + 1` of them, and the alignment-plus-slide slack before and after the huge and metadata regions; 57 ranges at quantum 16, `MAX_SEALED = NCLASSES + 5`), one `mseal` call per range, right after `Regions::reserve` in `api::init`, gated by the probe's `Features.mseal`. Not sealed, and why: the class regions, the huge region and the metadata region are `mprotect`ed on commit and `MADV_DONTNEED`ed on purge for the life of the process, and `mseal` forbids exactly those (the design's "reservations" is read as "what never changes protection"); the huge-run guard pages in `PROT_NONE` fallback mode are re-committed on reuse; the region table is `Shared.regions`, a field of a static in the library's own data segment that shares its pages with locks and counters written at run time, in a mapping `ld.so` owns, and `mseal` does not stop writes anyway. Design 2, 10 (step 5, steps renumbered) and 14 and the README row now say exactly this.
- Phase 2 task 2 (2026-09-18), a refusal after a successful probe stops the sweep and does not abort: sealing is a hardening extra, not a heap invariant (`ENOMEM` at `vm.max_map_count` in a process with tens of thousands of VMAs would otherwise kill it at init). The count is observable: `malloc_stats_print` prints `Sealed ranges: N of M`, `api::sealed_ranges()` for tests.
- Phase 2 task 2 (2026-09-18), tests find sealed VMAs through `/proc/self/smaps` (`sl` in `VmFlags`, kernel 6.10+), not through allocator internals, and skip with a message where the program's own `mseal` probe fails: Docker's default seccomp profile filters the syscall on the Mac (EPERM) and on the GCE VMs' Docker 29.1 (ENOSYS: libseccomp does not know it), so on both hosts `make test DOCKER_FLAGS='--security-opt seccomp=unconfined'` is the run that exercises sealing; `make test` alone exercises the skip path and asserts nothing is sealed. The Rust layout test forks: a sealed layout can never be released and a second full layout must not stay alive in the test process.
- Phase 1.5 task 14 (2026-09-18), `check_live` takes the located slab and slot and loads the watermark `Relaxed`: `Regions::classify` already computed slab and slot from the dense arrays for every query, and `locate` recomputed them from the table line (a second dependent chain in front of the bitmap word, `bench/results/2026-09-18-aarch64-gce-usable-size-chain.md` 1.2). The relaxed load uses `desc_if_committed`'s argument unchanged (a live slot's group words are visible through the program's own ordering; any other pointer aborts on every observable value); `free`'s slow path (`classify_free` -> `check_located`) shares the function, so its watermark load is relaxed too, which changes no outcome (the abort names may differ only for an invalid pointer racing a hand-out). The guard-slab test runs in the cold abort path only: guard slabs never have a bit set (`take_group` leaves them zero), so the bit test catches them and `not_live` picks the `guard slab` message as before; this removes a 32-bit `udiv` from every query.
- Phase 1.5 task 14 (2026-09-18), `valkey_model -u` / row `valkey1-usable`: the attribution rule needs a per-operation number on a workload that calls the query, and neither `valkey1` nor `valkey1-free` does (the model freed through `sdallocx`/`free` alone). `-u` calls `malloc_usable_size(p)` before every free, which is Valkey's `zfree(ptr)` (`zmalloc_size`, then `je_sdallocx`); the row is for `quick-perf`/`ab` attribution and is not in the `quick` gate's default set or the scorecard, so no existing number moves.
- Phase 1.5 task 13 (2026-09-18), SONAME: the plain build carries `libchalloc.so.0` (`build.rs` always emits `-Wl,-soname`; the `jemalloc-soname` feature selects `libjemalloc.so.2` instead). Reason: Valkey links `-lchalloc` and a distro package needs a versioned `DT_NEEDED`; `.0` is the ABI major of a 0.x crate. Cargo still writes the file as `libchalloc.so`, so `contrib/valkey/build.sh` makes the `libchalloc.so.0` link beside it (the binary's `RUNPATH` is that directory) and checks `DT_NEEDED [libchalloc.so.0]`; `ctests::jemalloc_compat_stage` asserts the plain soname; `scripts/jemalloc-compat.sh` and its `libjemalloc.so.2` check are unchanged; every `LD_PRELOAD` path is a file path and unaffected. Design 13.6.
- Phase 1.5 task 13 (2026-09-18), one library or two for quantum8/quantum16: one quantum per build stays (the class table is compile time, as jemalloc's `lg_quantum` is; a run-time quantum would put a table select on every class lookup and change the tested builds). The Phase 3 rule (design 13.6, plan Phase 3 task 2): the system `libchalloc.so.0` and the compat `libjemalloc.so.2` are `quantum16`, because Wolfi's `jemalloc` 5.3.1 is configured without `--with-lg-quantum` (checked in `wolfi-dev/os` `jemalloc.yaml`: `--enable-xmalloc --prefix=/usr --with-lg-page=$_pgs --with-lg-hugepage=21`), i.e. jemalloc's default quantum 16 on both architectures, which is the class table Ruby and every other consumer of the system jemalloc already runs on; Valkey, the one `quantum8` consumer (defrag asserts `arenas.quantum == 8`, from its bundled jemalloc's `--with-lg-quantum=3`), gets a private `quantum8` build in its own package under `/usr/lib/valkey/` (named in `RUNPATH`, which is what `contrib/valkey/build.sh` does today with `-Wl,-rpath`) or statically from `libchalloc.a`, the way Wolfi's Valkey builds its bundled jemalloc today. Two libraries with one soname cannot share `/usr/lib`, so the system library is never `quantum8`. Nothing in this repository's tested builds changes.
- Phase 1.5 task 13 (2026-09-18), `challoc_tls_get`/`challoc_tls_set` removed, `challoc_version()` added (`src/api/mod.rs`): returns the crate version (`CARGO_PKG_VERSION`, `0.1.0`) as a NUL-terminated static string, not `mallctl.rs`'s `VERSION_STR`. Reason: `VERSION_STR` is the jemalloc-shaped `5.3.0-0-gchalloc` that exists for parsers of `mallctl("version")` and is not challoc's version; the probe's job is to say that challoc is loaded and which challoc, and the soname major follows the crate major. It reads and writes no allocator state and does not initialise. `tls.rs` asserts neither TLS accessor is a dynamic symbol and that `challoc_version` is; `require_challoc` in `tests/c/common.h`, `cxx_new.cpp`, `libc_api.c` and `tests/glibc/test-skeleton.c` `dlsym` it (the fuzz driver and the mimalloc stress use `common.h`). `tests/c/tls_threads.c` no longer drives the slot: each of 64 threads reads `thread.allocatedp` (the counter's address inside its heap), checks `thread.allocated` moves by its own block's usable size (the pointer's value is stale until that read, as the Rust test documents), and the main thread checks the 64 addresses are distinct and not its own, under a barrier so no heap is reused before it is observed. `tests/c/fork_signal.c` used `challoc_tls_get() == NULL` to prove challoc had not initialised before `main`; it now proves the property that check guarded: the SIGALRM handler records whether it ran inside one of the program's own atfork handlers (`in_atfork`/`ran_inside`), which happens only when the raise was not blocked, i.e. challoc's prepare had not run yet (handlers registered in the wrong order) or did not block signals; `CHECK(ran_inside == 0)` per round.
- Phase 1.5 task 13 (2026-09-18), sized `operator delete` routed through the sized path (`src/api/cxx.rs`): `_ZdlPvm`/`_ZdaPvm` are `free_sized(p, size)`, `_ZdlPvmSt11align_val_t`/`_ZdaPvmSt11align_val_t` are `free_aligned_sized(p, align, size)`, so the hint is `class_for(size, align)`, the same class the matching `new` (`malloc`/`aligned_alloc`) took, and a mismatch aborts as `free_sized` does; the unsized aligned forms stay plain `free`. Reasons: consistency with `free_sized`/`sdallocx` (review item 38); jemalloc's own sized `delete` is `sdallocx` with the same contract, so a program passing a wrong size is already outside jemalloc's contract; the mechanism is task 4e's (`bench/results/2026-09-18-aarch64-gce-sized-free.md`): the slot is pushed without loading the slab descriptor's bitmap line. Attribution rule: no benchmark row calls a sized `delete`: the built larson imports `_Znam` and `_ZdaPv` only (unsized `delete[]`; its `SIZED` variant is not compiled), so there is no before/after row to record beyond the `quick` regression check on both VMs (`bench/results/2026-09-18-cxx-sized-delete.md`); `tests/c/cxx_new.cpp` exercises every sized form under the preload.
- Phase 1.5 task 13 (2026-09-18), `je_pvalloc` added (`src/api/libc.rs`), with `je_memalign`/`je_valloc`/`je_pvalloc` now referenced and exercised by `tests/c/jemalloc_api.c` (review item 43 listed them as presence-only).
Format: `Phase N task M: <decision>. Reason: <one line>.`

- Phase 0 task 1: Dockerfile.dev swaps wolfi-base's pinned `glibc`, `glibc-locale-posix`, `ld-linux`, `libcrypt1` (2.43) for the `glibc-2.44` stream before `apk add rust`. Reason: every Wolfi rust stream links libLLVM-22 built against glibc 2.44; plain `apk add rust` gives a rustc that fails to start.
- Phase 0 task 1: `target/` in the container is a named Docker volume per architecture; the cargo registry is a shared named volume. Reason: keeps Linux artifacts out of the host `target/`, avoids the slow macOS bind mount, and lets arm64/amd64 builds coexist.
- Phase 0 task 1: `quantum16`/`quantum8` are Cargo features with a `compile_error!` if both or neither is set; `lib.rs` exposes `QUANTUM`. Reason: design.md section 3 says build-time feature but does not say how to guard misuse.
- Phase 0 task 1: the `no_std` panic handler is a bare trap (`ud2` / `brk #1`) until `sys::abort` exists in task 2. Reason: no syscall layer yet; `panic = "abort"` means it is only reached by explicit panics.
- Phase 0 task 2: syscall numbers are hand-declared per arch in `src/sys/nr.rs`; a Linux-only unit test compiles `tests/c/sysnr.c` and checks every constant against the container's kernel headers. Reason: explicit and independent of the `libc` crate version; the header check keeps them honest on both arches.
- Phase 0 task 2: `sys::abort` writes `challoc: <msg>\n` with one buffered `write(2)`, unblocks SIGABRT, `tgkill`s the calling thread, resets the disposition to SIG_DFL and re-raises, then `exit_group(134)`. Reason: mirrors glibc's `abort` so a handler or blocked mask cannot let the process continue past an inconsistency.
- Phase 0 task 2: Linux-side coverage for `sys` is Rust unit tests under `cargo test --features std` run inside the container (mmap/mprotect/madvise, mseal, process_madvise, prctl, getrandom, futex, clocks, files, abort in a child process). Reason: no exported entry points exist yet to drive from C via `LD_PRELOAD`.
- Phase 0 task 2: `sys::open` always adds `O_CLOEXEC`; `PIDFD_SELF` is defined as -10000 but only task 3's probe decides whether the kernel honours it. Reason: the allocator never wants its fds inherited across exec; the sentinel's presence varies by kernel.

- Phase 0 task 3: page size comes from libc's `getauxval(AT_PAGESZ)` (design section 10 step 1), falling back to parsing `/proc/self/auxv` with our own syscalls, then abort. Reason: guessing a page size would corrupt every mapping; `getauxval` never allocates and is how glibc's malloc learns it.
- Phase 0 task 3: every probe treats any error as "unavailable", and `process_madvise` is probed with `MADV_DONTNEED` and checked for effect (whole page reported, page reads zero). Reason: failure modes vary (ENOSYS, EINVAL, EPERM, EBADF); DONTNEED is what purge issues and older kernels accept other advice but not it.
- Phase 0 task 3: `process_madvise` falls back from `PIDFD_SELF` to `pidfd_open(getpid())` and keeps that fd (`ProcessMadvise::Pidfd`); `Features::release()` closes it for the fork child, which must re-probe. Reason: the pidfd names the parent after fork.
- Phase 0 task 3: the `mseal` probe seals a `PROT_NONE` scratch page and leaves it mapped. Reason: a sealed page cannot be unmapped; PROT_NONE costs one VMA and no RSS, and it proves sealing rather than just the syscall's existence.
- Phase 0 task 3: `MADV_COLLAPSE` is not probed eagerly. Reason: a reliable probe needs a populated 2 MiB-aligned region and THP `always`/`madvise`; design section 10 already defers THP to the `thp` tunable, so its probe belongs with that work (Phase 2 task 5).
- Phase 0 task 4: the TLS slot symbol is `.globl` + `.hidden`. Reason: hidden keeps it out of the dynamic symbol table so the only dynamic relocation is the `TPOFF64`/`TLS_TPREL64` GOT fixup and nothing can interpose it; it still has to be global because debug/test builds place the `global_asm!` in a different codegen unit from the accessors.
- Phase 0 task 4: `challoc_tls_get`/`challoc_tls_set` are permanent diagnostic exports, and `tests/c/tls_threads.c` finds them with `dlsym(RTLD_DEFAULT)`. Reason: no heap exists yet so a C program under `LD_PRELOAD` needs some way to drive the slot, and a separate test-only `.so` build would double the fat-LTO build; dlsym keeps the test binary free of a link-time dependency and lets it prove it fails without the preload.
- Phase 0 task 4: `make test` runs `cargo build --release` before `cargo test`, and tests that need the `.so` fail with a message if it is missing rather than building it themselves. Reason: a test spawning `cargo build` would contend with the running `cargo test` for the target-dir lock.
- Phase 0 task 5: class stride is 4x the region (gap 3x) so `c = (p - superblock) >> lg_stride` is a shift; the region halves from 2^38 to 2^32 on `mmap` failure, and as a last resort the stride drops to 2x the region (gap 1x) before aborting. Reason: design 2 wants a power-of-two stride, and 3x is not one; the 1x gap is below the 1.25x minimum but keeps a 39-bit-VA kernel working rather than failing outright.
- Phase 0 task 5: the superblock and every class base are aligned to `MAX_SLAB` (4 MiB, the 256 KiB class's slab), and per-class random offsets are multiples of it. Reason: `offset = p & (slab_size - 1)` in design 3 is only correct when the class base is aligned to its slab size; one alignment for all classes is simplest and is THP-friendly.
- Phase 0 task 5: region bases come from the kernel's mmap placement plus a random reservation order for the three mappings, a random `MAX_SLAB`-granular offset inside each class gap, and a random 1 GiB slide of the huge and metadata bases inside an over-reserved window. Reason: design 2 asks for getrandom-randomised bases; a `MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE` search for arbitrary bases can fail unpredictably, whereas sliding inside a reservation cannot.
- Phase 0 task 5: the `>> 32` reciprocal is kept as designed even though it is only exact for offsets that are multiples of the slot size. Reason: `free` only needs exactness on slot starts, and a misaligned offset can never satisfy `slot * size == offset` for any `slot`; the exhaustive host test over every in-slab offset of every class confirms it.
- Phase 0 task 5: `class_of(0)` is class 0 (one quantum). Reason: `malloc(0)` must return a unique pointer and the smallest slot is the cheapest way.
- Phase 0 task 5: `Regions::release` (munmap of all reservations) exists as a public unsafe method. Reason: the Linux tests reserve twice in one process to check randomisation; the allocator itself never calls it.
- Phase 0 task 6: `AtomicBitmap` keeps the padding bits above `nslots` in the last word permanently set (`init`). Reason: a set bit already means "not claimable", so refills can never hand out a non-existent slot and "full" is simply every word equal to `!0`.
- Phase 0 task 6: `AtomicBitmap::release` returns the previous word and leaves the abort on `prev & mask != mask` to the caller in `slab.rs`. Reason: keeps the module pure logic that `cargo test` can drive on the host while still detecting a slot released twice from two caches.
- Phase 0 task 6: claims use `Acquire` and releases `Release`. Reason: POSIX requires `malloc`/`free` to synchronise memory, so a slot's new owner must happen-after its previous owner's writes; jemalloc orders its bitmaps the same way.
- Phase 0 task 6: `TwoLevelBitmap` is exactly two levels as designed, but tracks `l1_used`, the number of leading level-1 words that can be non-zero, and `lowest_set` scans only that prefix. Reason: real universes (2^22 slabs, 2^28 huge pages) need 1024 to 65536 level-1 words, and an unbounded scan would touch and commit every summary page; with the bound a heap of a few thousand slabs does one word plus two `tzcnt`s, matching the design's intent.
- Phase 0 task 6: `TwoLevelBitmap` storage is caller-provided zeroed `u64`s sized by `words_needed`, level 1 first then level 0, accessed through a raw pointer with no lifetime. Reason: task 7 carves it out of the metadata region, which lives for the process; tests back it with a `Vec`.
- Phase 0 task 7: `src/lock.rs` is a new module holding one futex mutex type (`RawLock`, `Lock<T>` with a guard), Drepper's "mutex, take 3": CAS to lock, short spin, then `FUTEX_WAIT`; `FUTEX_WAKE` only when the word says a waiter may sleep; off Linux the wait is a yield so host tests run. Reason: design 9 names class, huge, metadata and background-thread locks but section 17 gives them no home, and `meta.rs` is the first user.
- Phase 0 task 7: the metadata region is carved from both ends. Objects (`Heap`, `ClassCache`, ...) come from a bump pointer at the base, committed ahead in 64 KiB chunks, with freed objects on per-bin free lists; reservations (descriptor arrays, bitmaps, stacks) come downward from the top, page aligned and uncommitted, and the owner calls `Meta::commit` on the pages it starts using. Reason: design 4 wants descriptor arrays committed on demand as the region grows, and committing whole tables up front would charge tens of GiB of overcommit under `vm.overcommit_memory=2`; two watermarks growing towards each other need no free-space tracking.
- Phase 0 task 7: object bins are 16, 32, 48, 64, 128, then 1.5x and 2x steps up to 1 MiB (31 bins); bins below 64 are 16-aligned, all others cache-line aligned, and there is deliberately no 96 bin. Reason: a heap or cache of one thread must never share a cache line with another thread's, so every bin from 64 up is a multiple of 64; consecutive bins differ by at most 2x so internal waste is bounded.
- Phase 0 task 7: `Meta::alloc` returns null on region exhaustion or a failed `mprotect` but aborts on a layout it cannot serve (size above 1 MiB, alignment above 64) and on `free` of a pointer outside `[base, bump)` or misaligned for its bin. Reason: exhaustion is a resource failure that `malloc` must turn into `ENOMEM`; the others are allocator bugs, and design 14 says abort on inconsistency.
- Phase 0 task 7: `Meta::commit` trusts the caller to commit each page once and only sums lengths into `stats.committed`; `mprotect` itself is idempotent. Reason: per-page commit tracking would cost a bitmap over 2^36 bytes of metadata for a counter that is only reported, and every owner already keeps a high-water mark.
- Phase 0 task 7: memory fresh from the bump pointer is guaranteed zero (never touched, kernel zero pages), reused objects are not; `alloc_zeroed` scrubs only when the block came from a free list (address below the old bump). Reason: avoids touching pages for large fresh arrays while keeping a cheap zeroed path for `Heap` creation.
- Phase 0 task 8: a refill reserves its wanted count in the descriptor's `live` under the class lock *before* claiming bits, and gives back the unused part afterwards (`unreserve`). Reason: design 4 and 5 claim outside the lock but purge needs `live == 0` under the lock; without the reservation a purge could `MADV_DONTNEED` a slab whose slots a thread had just claimed, and the calloc `zeroed` flag would be unsound.
- Phase 0 task 8: `ClassState` keeps a third `TwoLevelBitmap`, `purged`, next to `partial` and `empty`. Reason: design 5 selects "the lowest Purged slab" before committing a new one, but the `ClassState` sketch lists only two sets.
- Phase 0 task 8: the `zeroed` flag of a claim is true only for the thread that moved the slab from `Fresh`/`Purged` to `Active` under the lock; a second thread refilling from the same still-untouched slab gets `false`. Reason: state alone cannot tell a concurrent claimer whether a slot was dirtied and released in the window before the transition; the conservative answer only costs a `memset` in `calloc`.
- Phase 0 task 8: a partial bit may be stale (slab actually full). Only a refill that observed the slab full clears it, after re-checking `is_full` under the lock; a releaser sets it when the word it cleared was `!0`. Reason: with lock ordering this guarantees every slab with a clear bit is (or will be) in the partial set, which is all first-fit needs; the cost is one extra lock and scan per newly filled slab.
- Phase 0 task 8: guard slabs are committed read-write like their neighbours and get `MADV_GUARD_INSTALL` on their first page; if the install fails the slab is re-protected `PROT_NONE`. Callers pass `guard_interval = 0` when the kernel lacks guard regions. Reason: a `PROT_NONE` slab every N slabs splits the region into one VMA per guard (plan Phase 2 task 1 forbids that), while a guard region inside a read-write VMA does not.
- Phase 0 task 8: `ClassState` owns the class's descriptor array and commit watermark as well as the per-arena sets. Reason: Phase 0 has one arena; Phase 1 task 3 must split the shared per-class table (descriptors, `next_slab`) from the per-arena `ClassState` when arenas arrive.
- Phase 0 task 8: descriptor arrays and the level-0 words of the three slab sets are committed page by page as `next_slab` grows; level 1 of each set is committed at creation. Reason: with 2^22 slabs per class the descriptor reservations alone are about 13 GiB (`quantum16`), which must stay uncommitted under `vm.overcommit_memory=2`.
- Phase 0 task 8: `ClassState::desc` aborts for a slab number at or beyond an atomic `committed_slabs` mirror of the watermark. Reason: a `free` of a pointer into a never-committed slab would otherwise fault on the `PROT_NONE` descriptor page instead of aborting with a message.
- Phase 0 task 8: `dirty_at` is `max(now_coarse_ns, 1)` so 0 always means "not Empty"; `purge_empty(cutoff)` purges under the class lock with one `madvise` per slab. Reason: the batched `process_madvise` driver and the decay deadlines are Phase 1 task 2; this gives the state machine a reachable `Purged` state now.
- Phase 0 task 9: `heap.rs` defines `Shared` (the `Meta`, the `Regions`, `[ClassState; NCLASSES]` for arena 0, the GC interval and the thread-exit key), initialised in place by `Shared::init`; every `Heap` keeps a back pointer to it. Reason: design 17 gives the process-wide state no home, the class states borrow `meta` and `regions`, and the key destructor receives only the heap pointer. Task 11 puts one `Shared` in a static.
- Phase 0 task 9: a heap's unused cache pointers all point at one shared read-only sentinel `ClassCache` with `len == cap == 0`, so the fast paths test only `len` (alloc) or `len == cap` (free) and never a null. Reason: one branch fewer on both fast paths; every write to a cache is guarded by `len > 0` or `len < cap`, and `gc` skips `cap == 0` explicitly (the host test caught a write to it as `SIGBUS`).
- Phase 0 task 9: refill writes the claimed batch reversed so the lowest address is popped first; flush sorts the oldest `cap / 2` entries with `sort_unstable` before `ClassState::release`. Reason: address-ordered first fit (design 5) and one `fetch_and` per bitmap word instead of one per pointer; the sort is amortised over 128 frees and is the first thing to revisit if `larson` shows it.
- Phase 0 task 9: calloc's zero tracking is a per-cache prefix count `zeroed`: a zero refill sets it to the batch size, every push cuts it to the push index, a flush subtracts what it removed, and a pop is zero iff its index is below it. Reason: design 6.3 asks for a per-batch flag; the prefix keeps the flag exact under pushes above the batch at the cost of one `min` on the free path and none on malloc.
- Phase 0 task 9: cache capacity is the fixed class default (256 up to 128 bytes, halving per doubling, minimum 8) in Phase 0; GC every 64 slow paths on a 10 s interval releases three quarters of each cache's low water (jemalloc's rule) and restarts tracking without shrinking `cap`. Reason: the tunables that would drive adaptive capacities are Phase 1 task 1; low-water tracking costs one `min` on pop.
- Phase 0 task 9: no top-4 cache compare on free. Reason: design 6.3 calls it optional, and Phase 2 task 3 owns the hardening tunables together with the benchmark rows the plan demands for them.
- Phase 0 task 9: after the key destructor the TLS slot holds `TLS_EXITED` and the thread is served straight from the slab bitmaps, as it is while the slot holds `TLS_CREATING`; `pthread_setspecific` failure aborts; `pthread_key_create` failure is remembered (`Shared::has_thread_key`) and leaves exiting threads' heaps unreclaimed. Reason: glibc re-runs destructors for up to four rounds if a later destructor allocates, and creating a heap per round would leak cached slots; jemalloc also aborts when it cannot set its TSD.
- Phase 0 task 9: `free` of a pointer in the huge region aborts with a placeholder message. Reason: the huge allocator is task 10; aborting is the only honest answer until then.
- Phase 0 task 10: the huge region is an extent allocator: every page below the high-water mark `next_page` belongs to exactly one extent (an `Active` run including its guard page, or a free `Empty`/`Purged` extent), adjacent free extents are always coalesced whatever their states (the merge is `Empty` unless both were `Purged`), and pages never handed out count as clean. Reason: first fit over the free-page bitmap then always lands inside one extent, so a split touches at most two descriptors and the calloc zero flag is exact; the cost is that a later purge may `MADV_DONTNEED` clean pages that were merged into a dirty extent.
- Phase 0 task 10: the descriptor table is open addressing with linear probing and backward-shift deletion over 16-byte `(key, desc)` entries at a load of one half or less; a run is keyed by its start page, a free extent by its first and last page. Tables up to 1 MiB come from `Meta::alloc`, bigger ones from `Meta::reserve_committed` and are leaked when outgrown (at most the size of the final table in total). Reason: design 7 names a hash table keyed by start page; the last-page key is what lets `free` find both neighbours in O(1) without a page map, and storing the key per slot is what makes backward-shift deletion possible with two keys per descriptor.
- Phase 0 task 10: `Empty` extents live in a FIFO list ordered by `dirty_at` rather than an address-ordered set; when a free coalesces, the `Empty` participant with the oldest `dirty_at` survives and keeps its list position, so `purge_empty(cutoff)` is a pop from the head. Reason: decay needs age order and the free bitmap already supplies address order; the design's "empty set ordered by address" would make every purge a full scan.
- Phase 0 task 10: with `MADV_GUARD_INSTALL` the guard page is committed read-write like its run and the guard is removed when the run is freed, so free extents never contain guards and runs stay in one VMA; without it the guard page is `PROT_NONE`, and every allocation re-commits its whole usable range with one `mprotect` so stale `PROT_NONE` guards inside a reused extent become writable. Reason: design 7 wants both modes; one idempotent `mprotect` per huge allocation is cheaper than tracking per-page protection.
- Phase 0 task 10: alignment above a page picks the first aligned start inside each free extent (or at the mark); the pages before and after the run stay free extents in the extent's state, and a hole at the mark becomes a `Purged` extent. Reason: design 7 "over-allocate and release the head and tail" without a separate release step.
- Phase 0 task 10: every fallible step of `Huge::alloc` (two descriptor allocations, table growth, bitmap commit, `mprotect`, guard install) runs before any state changes, so a failure returns null and leaves the allocator untouched; `Huge::free` cannot fail, so its table growth is best-effort and a fuller table just probes longer. Reason: `malloc` must turn resource failures into `ENOMEM`, and a half-applied split would be a heap inconsistency.
- Phase 0 task 10: `Shared` gains `huge: Huge` and `Shared::init` a `guard_install` flag; `Heap::free` classifies with `Shared::classify_free` and routes huge pointers to a cold `free_huge`; `Heap::alloc_huge`/`Shared::alloc_huge` bypass the caches. The slab fast path is instruction-for-instruction the same as before (the huge arm was already a cold call), and no benchmark row exists because nothing is exported yet; the first Phase 0 acceptance run is the baseline. Reason: the huge allocator is unusable without a free route, and the plan's benchmark rule cannot be met before task 11 exposes `malloc`.
- Phase 0 task 10: `huge_retain_max` is an `AtomicUsize` on `Huge` (`set_retain_max`) defaulting to 64 MiB, and `Huge::free` returns the usable bytes released. Reason: Phase 1's `malloc_conf` needs to set it without a lock, and the thread counters need the size without a second lookup.
- Phase 0 task 10: `bitmap::TwoLevelBitmap` gained `set_range`/`clear_range`, and `class::Lazy`/`class::Set` became `pub(crate)`. Reason: a 64 MiB run is 16384 bits, and the huge free-page set is exactly the lazily committed set `class.rs` already had.
- Phase 0 task 11: the C-ABI exports live in a `cfg(not(test))` submodule as one-line wrappers over `pub fn` Rust implementations. Reason: `cargo test` links the crate into the test binary, where a `#[no_mangle] malloc` would interpose the binary's own allocator; the Rust functions are unit-tested in-process against the real global state and the exported symbols are tested through the release `.so` under `LD_PRELOAD`.
- Phase 0 task 11: the fork prepare handler takes the class locks in index order, then the huge lock, then the metadata lock. Reason: class and huge code take the metadata lock while holding their own and nothing nests the other way, so this order lets any in-flight operation finish; taking metadata first could deadlock against a thread holding a class lock and waiting for metadata.
- Phase 0 task 11: `pthread_atfork` is registered before `Ready` is published, and an allocation from the initialising thread during init aborts with a message rather than being served. Reason: design 10 says init must not allocate; glibc's first 48 fork handlers come from a static array, so the abort path is a bug detector, not a code path.
- Phase 0 task 11: `guard_slab_interval` is 8 when `MADV_GUARD_INSTALL` probed and 0 otherwise. Reason: `class.rs` requires 0 without guard regions because the `PROT_NONE` fallback would split each class region into one VMA per guard; Phase 2 task 1 owns the tunable.
- Phase 0 task 11: `free`, `realloc` and `malloc_usable_size` of a non-null pointer before init abort without initialising the allocator. Reason: design 10 says such a pointer is invalid, and initialising just to classify it would only delay the same abort.
- Phase 0 task 11: huge `realloc` keeps the pointer only when the new size needs the same number of pages; otherwise it allocates, copies and frees, with no in-place shrink. Reason: design 6.3 describes exactly alloc/copy/free; in-place huge shrink and `xallocx` growth arrive with the jemalloc API in Phase 1.
- Phase 0 task 12: the C drivers live in `src/api/ctests.rs` (`cfg(test)`), one `#[test]` per program, not in `tests/*.rs`. Reason: the crate is only `cdylib`/`staticlib`, so Cargo integration tests cannot link it; and under `api::` they run in the second `make test` process together with the other `LD_PRELOAD` tests.
- Phase 0 task 12: every program calls `require_challoc()` (`malloc_usable_size(malloc(1)) == QUANTUM` and `dlsym("challoc_tls_get")`) and the driver first runs it without the preload and asserts failure. Reason: a broken preload would otherwise let glibc pass the whole suite.
- Phase 0 task 12: the "thread exits with a full cache" criterion is checked as a leak bound: 200 threads each fill and free 300 slots in seven classes, and the number of distinct slot addresses per class must stay at or below 4x300. Reason: a cache that was not reclaimed by the exit destructor would consume fresh slots every round and grow linearly; the bound is what makes the test fail rather than merely run.
- Phase 0 task 12: pointer identity across `realloc` is compared as `uintptr_t` and a block kept after a failed `realloc` is reached through a `volatile` copy. Reason: gcc 15's `-Wuse-after-free` (on under `-Wall`, and `-Werror` is set) rejects even comparing a pointer that was passed to `realloc`.
- Phase 0 task 11: `free_sized` and `free_aligned_sized` ignore their size and alignment arguments. Reason: C23 makes a mismatch undefined and glibc's implementation is plain `free`; checking the size against the class is a Phase 2 hardening option.
- Phase 0 task 11: `TwoLevelBitmap::lowest_set_from` only reads a level-0 word whose level-1 bit is set. Reason: the lazily committed sets in `class.rs` and `huge.rs` leave level 0 `PROT_NONE` beyond the high-water mark; a set level-1 bit means the word was written, so it is committed. The old code read `l0(from / 64)` unconditionally, which the small test regions hid through page rounding and the real 1 TiB huge region (level 1 exactly 512 KiB) exposed as a segfault on the first huge allocation.
- Phase 0 task 11: `Regions::reserve` over-reserves the huge and metadata mappings by `MAX_SLAB` and aligns their bases up before applying the random slide. Reason: `mmap` guarantees page alignment only; on native kernels the top-down placement happened to be aligned, but under Rosetta with concurrent tests it was not, which failed the layout and huge alignment tests and would have left the real allocator with a base the design says is `MAX_SLAB`-aligned.
- Phase 0 task 11: `make test` runs the `api::` tests in a second `cargo test` process. Reason: they initialise the real allocator (53 TiB superblock, 1 TiB huge, 64 GiB metadata reservations) for the life of the process, and the layout tests then reserve a second full layout; native kernels take both, Rosetta traps with "could not find free space" for its own bookkeeping.

- Phase 0 task 13: the fuzz driver includes `tests/c/common.h` (via `-Itests/c`) and is driven by `src/api/ctests.rs` like the task 12 programs, at 400k ops x 4 threads and 200k ops x 1 thread x 64-slot table, so every `make test` runs it; `make fuzz` is the full 10M x 4 run. Reason: one set of CHECK/fill/RNG helpers, and a fuzzer nobody runs finds nothing.
- Phase 0 task 13: blocks are filled and verified over their whole `malloc_usable_size`, not just the requested size, and `realloc` is checked to preserve `min(old usable, new size)`. Reason: overlap between neighbouring slots or a wrong class size shows up only in the tail bytes; the copy length is what `api/libc.rs` documents.
- Phase 0 task 13: sizes include 0 and an 8..16 MiB tier, alignments go up to 64 KiB through `aligned_alloc`/`posix_memalign`/`memalign`, and `realloc(p, 0)` is checked to free and return null. Reason: the task 12 programs cover each of these in isolation; the fuzzer interleaves them with everything else.
- Phase 0 task 13: the seed defaults to a fixed value and `random` draws one from `getrandom`; the seed in use is printed first and a failure prints the exact replay command. Reason: an unattended loop needs reproducible runs, and a failing fuzz run is worthless without its seed.
- Phase 0 task 14: the `no_std` panic handler prints `challoc: <message> [<file>:<line>]` instead of `challoc: panic: panicked at <file>:<line>:\n<message>`. Reason: every heap-inconsistency abort is a `panic!`, and the plan asks for "the challoc message"; the reason should come first and on one line.
- Phase 0 task 14: the double-free negative test has two modes because Phase 0 detects a double free at two different points: `double_free_stale` flushes the thread cache between the two frees (the slot bit is clear, caught by the one-load check on `free`), `double_free_cached` frees back to back (both copies sit in the cache and the batched release catches the duplicate). Reason: a back-to-back double free with the slot still cached is only caught at flush until Phase 2's `double_free_scan`; the test documents that boundary instead of pretending otherwise.
- Phase 0 task 14: `negative.c` routes every bad pointer through a `volatile` and allocates its churn blocks before the victim. Reason: gcc 15 rejects `free` of a stack address, a freed pointer, or an out-of-bounds store as errors under `-Werror`; and a `malloc` after the first `free` would pop the victim straight back out of the thread cache.
- Phase 0 task 15: the five glibc sources (tag `glibc-2.44`) plus `tst-malloc-aux.h` are copied unmodified into `tests/glibc/malloc/`, mirroring glibc's tree so their trailing `#include "../test-skeleton.c"` resolves to challoc's `tests/glibc/test-skeleton.c`, and `tests/glibc/include/` shims the three internal headers they use (`libc-diag.h`, `support/check.h`, `support/test-pointer.h`). Reason: unmodified sources keep the provenance simple (README lists tag, commit, licence) and the skeleton is where challoc adds `require_challoc` and the `ok <name>` line the driver expects.
- Phase 0 task 15: glibc tests compile with the common `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` plus `-D_GNU_SOURCE -Wno-unused-parameter`, and the skeleton has no alarm-based timeout. Reason: glibc builds its tests without `-Wextra` (`tst-mallocfork.c` has an unused signal parameter) and installs its own SIGALRM handler in that test; the other C drivers here have no timeout either.

- Phase 0 task 16: mimalloc `test/test-stress.c` (tag `v3.5.2`) is copied unmodified into `tests/mimalloc/` with its MIT `LICENSE`; `tests/mimalloc/stress.c` is the program actually built: it defines `USE_STD_MALLOC`, `#include`s the source with `#define main test_stress_main`, and wraps it with `require_challoc()` and the `ok stress` line; `include/mimalloc.h` and `include/mimalloc-stats.h` stand in for the headers the source includes unconditionally and declare only `mi_subproc_id_t`. Reason: same provenance rule as task 15 (README lists tag, commit, licence), and the wrapper is where challoc's bare-run refusal and driver protocol live without touching upstream code.
- Phase 0 task 16: the stress program is compiled without `NDEBUG` and with `-Wno-unused-parameter`. Reason: `alloc_items()` asserts every `calloc` word is zero, which is exactly the calloc zero-tracking check we want; the debug-only progress line is harmless, and POSIX `run_os_threads()` ignores its `subproc` argument.

- Phase 0 acceptance (step 1): `ClassState` is `repr(C, align(128))`: read-mostly fields first, then the class lock and `Inner` in `lock::Padded` (a `repr(align(128))` wrapper; `lock::PAD_ALIGN`), then a 128-byte `_tail` so `size_of::<ClassState>()` is 640, with const asserts on the offsets and on `size % 512 != 0`. Reason: with the natural 512-byte stride and 4 KiB pages every class's hot line lands on the same few L1 sets on Apple silicon; single-threaded alloc-test1 went 2.13 -> 2.52 s and the stall sat on `free`'s epilogue loads. Reproduced with a 64-byte-aligned 512-byte stride too; 384- and 640-byte strides are as fast as the unpadded 344.
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 1): 128 bytes, not 64, is the padding unit. Reason: Apple silicon has 128-byte lines and Intel's adjacent-line prefetcher pairs 64-byte lines; 128 covers both. `meta::CACHE_LINE` stays 64 (bin geometry) and is a separate question (remediation step 3).
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 1): `Shared` is `repr(C)` with `regions` first, then the class array, then `meta` and `huge`. Reason: `regions` is read by every `malloc`/`free`; the `meta` and `huge` locks must not share its line.
- Phase 0 acceptance: each remediation step gets its own results file `bench/results/<date>-<arch>-<step>.md` run with `ALLOCS="challoc jemalloc"`, not a rerun of the full four-allocator protocol. Reason: two full runs on the same day differed by up to 40% on every allocator (host load), so a step is only judged against jemalloc measured in the same run; the before-row is the previous file.
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 2): a refill takes the class lock once and reserves from up to 8 slabs in that one hold (`ClassState::plan`), reserving at most `nslots - live` from each: `live` is incremented under the lock before any bit is set and decremented only after bits are cleared, so it is an upper bound on set bits and the reservation is always claimable. Reason: the old `select`/`on_full` pair took the lock once per slab visited plus once more per slab it found full, and a producer/consumer pattern (xmalloc-test) visits several nearly full slabs per refill.
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 2): a claim that scans every word of its slab without finding a bit rescans instead of giving back the reservation; only after 2^22 empty passes does it abort. Reason: `claim_from` inspects each word once, so when a concurrent claimer wins the bits it was about to take the scan comes up short although the slots exist (seen in `fork_alloc` and mimalloc stress on the first attempt, which aborted); unbounded contention on one slab that lasts millions of passes is an inconsistency, not a schedule.
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 2): a partial bit is cleared eagerly only when a plan reserves every slot of a slab with `live == 0` (fresh, purged, or empty); a slab with live slots that gets filled keeps a stale bit until the next plan finds it full under the lock. Reason: with nothing allocated in the slab no release can race the clear, while for a live slab a release between the reservation and the claim could leave a free slot in no set.
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 2): `release` collects the slabs whose words went from full to not full and sets their partial bits under one lock hold at the end of the batch (`Reopen`, 8 entries, flushed early on overflow), skipping slabs that are no longer `Active`. Reason: a sorted flush of 128 slots from a consumer thread hit several full words of one slab and took the lock for each; a slab that went `Empty` in the meantime is in the empty set and must not be put back in the partial set.
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 2): `SlabDesc::live` is loaded with `Acquire`. Reason: a releaser clears bits and then subtracts, so a planner that sees the smaller count must also see the cleared bits for `nslots - live` to be a sound lower bound; the load sits under the class lock, so the cost is nil.
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 3): `meta::CACHE_LINE` is 128 (const-asserted equal to `lock::PAD_ALIGN`): bins are 16, 32, 48, 64 at 16-byte alignment, then 128, 256 and the 1.5x/2x ladder from 384 up, all 128-aligned and 128-multiples (30 bins; the 192 bin is gone, `bin_for` promotes an over-aligned small layout to the 128 bin). Reason: a 64-byte-aligned `Heap` (512-byte bin) or `ClassCache` (3072) placed at 64 mod 128 shared a 128-byte line at each end with a neighbouring thread's object; making every object from 128 up a whole number of 128-byte lines removes that without a per-object pad. Cost: a 16-slot cache (144 bytes) now takes 256 instead of 192; nothing else moves bin.
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 4): the exported `malloc` and `free` contain no calls except one tail call each to a cold `malloc_slow`/`free_slow` that redoes the whole operation from scratch (init, heap creation, refill or flush, huge runs, and every abort). Reason: a `bl` into any diverging cold function (`invalid_free`, `never_committed`, `panic_bounds_check`) made LLVM keep a five-register frame with a 12-instruction prologue/epilogue on every call; with only tail calls the entry points are frameless (`free` 78 -> 73 instructions, `malloc` 56 -> 51 including the cold blocks). The slow path repeats the checks, so a fast-path `false` for a bad pointer ends in the same abort message as before.
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 4): the fast paths use the thread's heap pointer as the proof that init completed and read `SHARED` through `api::shared_unchecked()` without loading `STATE`. Reason: a heap is only created by `heap_for_thread` under `shared()`, so a `TLS > TLS_EXITED` thread has observed `READY` with acquire already; on aarch64 the `ldar` on `STATE` headed every dependent-load chain and blocked all later loads.
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 4): `Regions::slab_slot`, `ClassState::desc_if_committed` and `SlabDesc::is_allocated_unchecked` are the fast path's non-aborting forms of `classify`, `desc` and `check`, with the descriptor stride cached in `ClassState::desc_stride`; none of them bounds-check `CLASSES`, `DESC_STRIDE` or the bitmap word. Reason: the old path checked `class < NCLASSES` three times (once real, twice through `cs.class()` on a `u16`), recomputed `bitmap_words` to bounds-check a slot already known to be below `nslots`, and loaded `class` then the stride table as two dependent loads.
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 4): `heap::MAX_CAP` is 1024 (design 6.2 amended: 1024 slots up to 128 bytes, halving per doubling, minimum 8; `tcache_nslots_small_max` default 1024). Reason: larson scales 1 -> 10 threads at +10.8% -> +33% behind jemalloc, so most of the gap is contention, and the profile's hottest instruction is the consumer of the `free` check's bitmap-word load. Every flush or refill writes the descriptor lines of the slabs it touches and invalidates them in every other core, and with one arena the "lowest partial slab" policy makes all threads share the same slabs; halving the flush rate halves those misses. Measured on the trimmed build: cap 256/512/1024 gave larson 225/243/252 M ops/s and xmalloc-test 201/390/590 M frees/s with larson RSS 54/58/62 MiB (jemalloc 167). Redis asks for `tcache_nslots_small_max:1000` anyway. A cache may now hold more than one slab's worth of slots; refills and flushes already spanned slabs.
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 4): not adopted, measured neutral on larson within noise (3 alternating rounds each): `DESC_ALIGN` 128 (descriptor false sharing on 128-byte lines) and a `Relaxed` load of `committed_slabs` on the fast path. Reason: the misses are true sharing of slab words between threads, not the line layout or the acquire.
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 4): `fork_prepare` blocks every signal on the forking thread before taking the locks and `fork_release` restores the mask after its last unlock; the saved mask lives in a static that is written and read only while every lock is held. Reason: glibc's `_Fork` restores the caller's mask before the parent handlers run, so a signal handler that forks between our prepare and our release (glibc's `tst-mallocfork`, where the child's SIGALRM overtook the parent's release under Rosetta) re-entered `fork_prepare` with the locks held by its own thread and waited forever; `tests/c/fork_signal.c` reproduces it (pre-fix build hangs, watchdog exit 2).
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 5): `libchalloc.so` exports the 20 C++ operator symbols (`src/api/cxx.rs`, design 13.1 amended); `operator new` runs the `new_handler` loop and throws `std::bad_alloc` through libstdc++'s `std::__throw_bad_alloc`, both reached as weak undefined GOT references from `asm!`, never a `DT_NEEDED` on libstdc++. Reason: jemalloc 5.3.1 exports them, so a C++ benchmark such as larson compared "within 10% of jemalloc" is otherwise measuring libstdc++'s `operator new` -> `malloc` PLT hops (13.5% of larson's samples); weak references keep C consumers loading under `BIND_NOW`, and the plan's hard rule of Rust-only code rules out a C++ thunk, so the throw is libstdc++'s own function unwinding through our frames (which have `.eh_frame` entries and no landing pads, like C code), checked by `tests/c/cxx_new.cpp` on both architectures.
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 6): `thread.allocated`/`thread.deallocated` are derived, not counted per operation. Each `ClassCache` header carries `allocs` (pops, bumped on the header line the pop writes anyway) and `moved` (slots refilled minus slots flushed, maintained on the slow paths); pushes are `len + allocs - moved`, and `Heap::stats` sums `size * count` over the caches plus the `uncached` counters for huge runs and cacheless slabs. The header grows from 16 to 32 bytes (`heap::CACHE_HEADER`). Reason: the old counters cost every `malloc` and `free` a load from the class table plus a read-modify-write of the heap line; `free` now touches no counter at all and `malloc` only its own header. Consequence for Phase 1: `thread.allocatedp` cannot hand out a pointer to a live counter; materialise the value into a per-heap `u64` on each read (and on the slow paths if a consumer polls it).
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 6): `low_water` and `zeroed` are written on the fast paths only when they actually change (`if len < low_water`, `if len < zeroed`), which is rare: `low_water` can only drop after a GC reset it to `len`, and `zeroed` is usually already 0. Reason: an unconditional `min` was a load plus a store per operation; the branch is a load plus a well-predicted compare.
- Phase 0 acceptance (step 6): the `free` fast path loads `committed_slabs` with `Relaxed` (`ClassState::desc_if_committed`); the slow path's `desc` keeps the acquire. Reason (argument in the doc comment): for a valid slot the program's own ordering of the free after the allocation, plus the class lock the allocation was claimed under, already carries the commit's happens-before to the freeing thread, so the acquire added nothing; for an invalid pointer the only stale observations are a smaller count (`None`, slow path repeats with acquire) or the descriptor's initial zero words (rejected), never a count above what is committed, so the descriptor page is always mapped. On aarch64 the `ldar` headed the descriptor and cache loads; measured +2-3% on larson in step 5.
- Phase 1 task 1: both `malloc_conf` and `je_malloc_conf` are read (in that order, both weak undefined GOT references), and a consumer must export the symbol from its executable for the library to see it. Reason: Redis and Valkey define `je_malloc_conf`; a program linked against the allocator exports the variable automatically because the linker sees the library's reference, while a bare `LD_PRELOAD` needs `-rdynamic` (exactly jemalloc's situation), which is why `tests/c/conf.c` is built with it.
- Phase 1 task 1: configuration is read after the feature probe and before the region reservation (design 10 lists it as step 4, after reserving). Reason: `abort_conf` may abort, and nothing should be mapped yet when it does; the probe's scratch page is the only mapping made before it.
- Phase 1 task 1: `abort_conf` is evaluated once after every source has been read, over the total number of problems from all sources. Reason: it must apply to keys that precede it in the same string or come from a lower-priority source; jemalloc achieves the same with a separate first pass for `abort_conf`, and a single count is simpler.
- Phase 1 task 1: out-of-range numbers are clamped into the key's range *and* reported as a problem (so they abort under `abort_conf`); unknown keys, malformed pairs and unparsable values are reported and skipped; the compatibility keys (`junk`, `zero`, `retain`, `oversize_threshold`, `percpu_arena`, `metadata_thp`, `stats_print`, `confirm_conf`) accept any value unchecked. Reason: this mirrors jemalloc's `CONF_HANDLE_*` behaviour that Redis and Valkey configurations were written against; validating values we ignore would only create spurious aborts.
- Phase 1 task 1: integers parse like jemalloc's `malloc_strtoumax(..., 0)`: decimal, `0x` hex, leading-zero octal, optional sign; keys and values are case-sensitive and not trimmed; a value may be double-quoted and then contain commas. Reason: Redis's `je_malloc_conf` and existing `MALLOC_CONF` strings must parse identically under both allocators.
- Phase 1 task 1: `MALLOC_CONF`/`CHALLOC_CONF` are ignored when `getauxval(AT_SECURE)` is non-zero; `/etc/challoc.conf` is still read. Reason: a setuid or capability-elevated process must not take allocator tunables from an untrusted environment (glibc's `secure_getenv` rule, which jemalloc follows with `issetugid`); the file is root-controlled.
- Phase 1 task 1: cache capacity is `tcache_nslots_small_max` for classes up to 128 bytes, halving per doubling of the class size, with the whole ladder shifted by `lg_tcache_nslots_mul - 1` doublings and clamped to `[MIN_CAP, tcache_nslots_small_max]`; `tcache:false` gives capacity 0, which makes `ensure_cache` return null so every operation takes the existing direct slab path. Reason: design 6.2 says the two jemalloc keys override its ladder without saying how; jemalloc's `nregs << lg_mul` is meaningless with our 64 KiB+ slabs, and this mapping makes Redis's `lg_tcache_nslots_mul:3,tcache_nslots_small_max:1000` mean what Redis intends (1000-slot caches for everything up to 512 bytes).
- Phase 1 task 1: `Shared::init` takes the `Config` plus the `guard_install` probe flag and derives the guard slab interval (`guards`, `guard_slab_interval`, 0 without the kernel feature), the huge-run guards (`guards && guard_install`), the GC interval and the cache capacities; `background_thread`, `dirty_decay_ms`, `narenas`, `thp`, `lazy_free`, `zero_on_free`, `slot_randomize`, `double_free_scan` and `cgroup` are stored and inert until their tasks (`muzzy_decay_ms` stays inert by design).
