Reason: only knobs that exist get wired; the rest of the table is parsed now so later tasks read fields instead of touching the parser. No fast path changed (`ensure_cache` runs once per class per thread; `Shared` keeps `regions` first), so no benchmark row.
- Phase 1 task 2: each `ClassState` and the `Huge` allocator keep an `oldest_empty: AtomicU64` (the `dirty_at` of the oldest `Empty` slab or extent, 0 when none) in the read-mostly area, written under their lock (set on the none-to-one transition, recomputed by every purge or list change) and read without it by the decay driver, so a periodic tick over 52 classes takes no lock unless a purge is due. The class value may lag a claim that reused the oldest slab, which makes a deadline early (a no-op purge that recomputes), never late. Reason: design 8 asks for a per-class deadline driven from the slow path; scanning the empty sets under 52 locks every 64 slow paths would put lock traffic back on the hot path that Phase 0 acceptance removed.
- Phase 1 task 2: purges are batched per class (or per huge purge) into `purge::Batch` and discarded while the class lock is still held; a slab is flipped to `Purged` before the batch goes out. Reason: the lock is what stops a claim from handing out a slab between the state change and the discard (the existing `purge_never_races_a_claim` argument); releasing it around the syscall would need an extra "purging" state. Batches never span classes, which would need two locks at once.
- Phase 1 task 2: a short or failed `process_madvise` (a stale pidfd, a kernel that refuses the advice) redoes the whole batch with a loop of `madvise`, and only an `madvise` failure aborts. Reason: `MADV_DONTNEED` and `MADV_FREE` are idempotent on free pages, and the probe cannot rule out a kernel that accepts `MADV_DONTNEED` but not `MADV_FREE` through `process_madvise`.
- Phase 1 task 2: under `lazy_free` the `Purged` state no longer implies zero memory: `class::take` and `huge::take` consult `purge::purged_is_zero()` and report `zeroed: false`, so `calloc` clears. Reason: `MADV_FREE` leaves the old contents in place until the kernel reclaims the pages, which may be never.
- Phase 1 task 2: while a background thread exists the slow path does no purging (`purge::on_tick` returns after the presence check); the thread is created on the first periodic tick after `background_thread:true` (never from init) and the tick that fails to create it purges in the foreground. Reason: the point of the thread is to keep purge latency off application threads; jemalloc's foreground decay likewise defers to its background threads.
- Phase 1 task 2: the background thread sleeps on a futex sequence word; the none-to-one `Empty` transition of any class or the huge list bumps the word and issues one `futex_wake` (skipped when no thread exists). Reason: without it a thread that found nothing dirty would sleep without a timeout until the next unrelated wake-up; the transition is rare (once per decay window per class) so the syscall is negligible, and reading the word before the scan makes a bump that lands between scan and wait return at once.
- Phase 1 task 2: `pthread_create` allocates (glibc's `allocate_dtv` calls `calloc`), so the creating thread parks its TLS slot on `TLS_CREATING` for the call and that allocation is served straight from the slabs, the same path heap creation uses for `pthread_setspecific`. The thread is detached, named `challoc-bg`, created with every signal blocked (inherited as its mask), on a 256 KiB stack reserved from the metadata region with its lowest page left `PROT_NONE` as the guard; the stack is never freed. Reason: re-entering the heap that is mid-`tick` would alias `&mut Heap`; signals must never run application handlers on our thread; the metadata region is the only allocator the design allows for the stack.
- Phase 1 task 2: the fork child marks the thread absent *and* withdraws the request (design 8.1: "not recreated in the child until the child asks for it again"), closes an inherited `ProcessMadvise::Pidfd` (it names the parent) and downgrades the discard backend to the `madvise` loop, updating the `Features` record so `malloc_stats_print` will report the truth. `PIDFD_SELF` needs no fd and survives. Reason: a pidfd of the parent would discard the parent's pages; re-probing in the child would map a scratch page inside a fork handler for a feature the (usually short-lived) child rarely needs.
- Phase 1 task 2: `malloc_trim(pad)` ignores `pad`, flushes the calling thread's caches before purging everything, and returns 1 if anything was purged. Reason: glibc's callers (Ruby's GC calls `malloc_trim(0)`) expect RSS to move; a thread's own cache can pin up to 1024 slots per class in `Active` slabs, and returning them costs only the caller.
- Phase 1 task 2: the decay period lives in `Shared::decay_ns` as an `AtomicU64` (`purge::NEVER` for `-1`, 0 for "purge as soon as the tick sees it") with `set_decay_ms` for task 5's `arena.<i>.dirty_decay_ms`. Reason: one place to read on every tick, changeable at run time without a lock.
- Phase 1 task 2: a huge run over `huge_retain_max` is still discarded at free time, now through `purge::Batch` so it honours `lazy_free` and the probed backend. Reason: one discard primitive.
- Phase 1 task 3: `class.rs` splits a per-class `SlabTable` (descriptor array, commit watermark, guard slabs, its own commit lock) out of `ClassState`, and every arena's `ClassState` for class `c` points at the same table; slab numbers go in address order to whichever arena commits next and each descriptor records its owner. Reason: design 13.3 says arenas share the class regions and `free` classifies by region, so descriptors must be findable for any slab regardless of arena; partitioning the region per arena would cut each arena's address space by the arena count.
- Phase 1 task 3: `Shared.multi_arena` (set when any arena other than 0 is built) gates the descriptor `arena` load on the `free` fast path; with one arena the path is unchanged. Reason: the descriptor header is on a different cache line from most bitmap words of the small classes, so an unconditional load would cost a line per `free` for the common single-arena case.
- Phase 1 task 3: a heap keeps its home arena's caches inline and other arenas' caches on a linked list (`ArenaCaches`) allocated on first touch, rather than the design's `caches[MAX_ARENAS_LOCAL]`. Reason: almost every heap touches one arena; the list puts no limit on arena indices (`MAX_ARENAS` = 4096, the `MALLOCX_ARENA` field) and costs nothing until a second arena is used.
- Phase 1 task 3: an explicit cache (`tcache.create`) is a `Heap` owned by no thread (`Heap::create_detached`), registered in `Shared.tcaches` (1024 ids). Reason: it needs exactly a heap's caches, refill and flush and nothing else; design 13.3 already says explicit caches are not thread safe.
- Phase 1 task 3: `narenas > 1` reserves that many automatic indices at init, assigns them to new heaps round robin (`Arenas::assign`) and builds each arena on first touch; `arenas.create` appends explicit indices after them, which are never assigned automatically. Reason: jemalloc's semantics, and building an arena commits about 1 MiB of set tables (level 1 of three `TwoLevelBitmap`s per class), which `narenas:64` should not pay up front.
- Phase 1 task 3: `dirty_decay_ms` lives on each `Arena` (`arena.<i>.dirty_decay_ms`); `Shared::set_decay_ms` sets every existing arena and the huge allocator follows arena 0. Reason: the huge region is shared by all arenas (runs record their arena) and has one empty set.
- Phase 1 task 3: fork lock order is arena creation lock, every arena's class locks, every table's commit lock, huge, meta, background. Reason: matches the nesting (`Arenas::create` takes meta under the creation lock; a class lock nests its table's commit lock, which takes meta).
- Phase 1 task 3: the `MALLOCX` routing (`arena::alloc`/`alloc_huge`/`free`, `Flags::decode`) lives in `arena.rs` and is exercised from Rust tests against the real allocator; the `mallocx` family exports and `mallctl` keys are task 5. Reason: no C-visible entry point exists yet for arenas, and task 5 owns the exports.
- Phase 1 task 3 (fix): `Arena::set_decay_ms` (and so `Shared::set_decay_ms`) wakes the background thread through `purge::note_decay_changed`. Reason: the thread sleeps until the earliest deadline computed with the old period and is only woken by none-to-one `Empty` transitions, so a shorter `dirty_decay_ms` set while something was already `Empty` took effect only at the next unrelated wake-up; this made `api::tests::background_thread_starts_purges_and_is_absent_after_fork` flaky under the parallel `api::` run (a concurrent test's freed huge run gave the thread a 10 s deadline before the test set 1 ms; the two fork-handler tests then failed on the poisoned `FORK_TEST` mutex). Verified on aarch64: 204 + 53 pass, `make fuzz` clean.
- Phase 1 task 3 follow-up: `multi_arena` is the first field of `Shared` and `Regions` is `repr(C)`, so the flag shares a 128-byte line with `superblock`/`lg_stride` (compile-time asserts in `heap.rs`); the heap's home arena is read with `read_volatile` inside the `multi_arena` branch of `checked_slot`. Reason: a plain load was hoisted above the flag test and cost every single-arena `free` the heap header line; the flag after `Regions` sat on its own line.
- Phase 1 task 3 follow-up: `SlabTable` carries `geom: Class` and `region_len`, and the `free` fast path classifies through `SlabTable::slab_slot_fast` from the `Regions` header line plus that one table line, never `Regions::class_base` or `CLASSES`; `Regions::slab_slot` stays for the slow path and tests. Reason: two cache lines fewer per `free`; measured neutral on larson but it is the right shape and helps the multi-arena path equally.
- Phase 1 task 3 follow-up: the regression stays open and recorded rather than reverting the arena tree. Reason: four hypotheses are excluded with data, the remaining suspects are listed with a plan, and the tree is correct on both architectures; reverting would lose task 3.
- Phase 1 task 3 follow-up (2): `SlabTable` orders its fields so everything the `free` fast path reads (`desc_stride`, `base`, `region_len`, `desc_base`, `committed_slabs`, `geom`) sits in the first 64 bytes, `nslabs` after. Reason: the only fast-path variant that moved the single-thread larson number (+2%), consistent with a 64-byte L1 line; zero cost.
- Phase 1 task 3 follow-up (3): `SlabTable::new` places class `c`'s descriptor array `desc_stagger(c) = 3 * 128 * c` bytes into a reservation that is longer by the same amount; `commit_next` commits `stagger + (s + 1) * stride`. Reason: 52 back-to-back power-of-two reservations all start on a 1 MiB boundary, which put every class's hot descriptor line in one L1 and one L2 set; three lines per class with `gcd(3, 128) == 1` gives 52 distinct sets in both caches at the cost of 20 KiB of address space per process.
- Phase 1 task 3 follow-up (3): reverses the follow-up decision above: `SlabTable` no longer copies `Class`/`region_len`, `slab_slot_fast` is gone, and `checked_slot` classifies through `Regions::slab_slot` (`class_base[c]`, `lg_region`, `CLASSES[c]`) before touching `tables[c]`. Reason: the copies were measured neutral, and with them the whole critical path of `free` hung on `class -> madd -> one of 52 table lines`, whereas the dense arrays are about a dozen lines for all classes and `class_base[c]` is one indexed load; the table's three descriptor fields are then off the critical path as in the pre-arena tree.
- Phase 1 task 4: `curregs` is not merged from per-heap counters. Each `ClassState` keeps `claimed` (slots reserved by `take`, written under the class lock) and `released` (one Relaxed `fetch_add` per `release` batch) on its otherwise idle last cache line, and `curregs = claimed - released`, clamped to the capacity of the slabs that can hold live slots. Reason: `release` only takes the lock when a slab reopens, so a locked counter would add a lock per flush; summing `live` over descriptors is O(slabs) per epoch (Valkey calls `epoch` every 100 ms); merging per-heap `moved` counters would need a heap registry and an exit-time fold for slots still in use. The plan's "per-thread counters" stay what they are (`Heap::stats` for `thread.*`); the "epoch merge" is the recomputation and storage of every arena's `ArenaStats` and the process `Snapshot`.
- Phase 1 task 4: `stats.*` values are frozen at the last `epoch` (all zero before the first), as in jemalloc; `stats::collect` computes live figures without storing them, for `malloc_stats_print`. The huge allocator's `Empty`/`Purged` extents and the committed metadata belong to no arena and are attributed to arena 0 (as `arena.0.purge` already does), so the per-arena figures sum to the totals; each arena counts the huge runs allocated through it (`Arena::note_huge_alloc`/`note_huge_free`, driven by `Shared::alloc_huge`/`free_huge`; `Huge::free_run` returns the freed run's arena). A huge run's `large.allocated` and active bytes are both its usable bytes (pages minus the guard), matching jemalloc's extent-size accounting. Guard slabs and guard pages hold no memory and are counted nowhere.
- Phase 1 task 5: a MIB is the path of child indices through a static name tree (`api/mallctl.rs` `ROOT`), one `size_t` per component, with a numeric component (`arena.<i>`, `arenas.bin.<j>`, `stats.arenas.<i>`, `stats.arenas.<i>.bins.<j>`) stored as the number itself and `opt.<key>`'s second component the index in `config::KEYS`; `mallctlnametomib` resolves interior nodes (jemalloc does) and using one as a key is `ENOENT`. Reason: Valkey's defragmenter resolves `stats.arenas.0.bins.0.curregs` once and then writes `mib[2]` and `mib[4]` in place before every `mallctlbymib`, which only works with jemalloc's positional encoding.
- Phase 1 task 5: `experimental.utilization.query` fills jemalloc's `extent_util_stats_verbose_t` in its real order (`slabcur_addr, nfree, nregs, size, bin_nfree, bin_nregs`) and `batch_query` one `(nfree, nregs, size)` triple per pointer, not the order design 13.4 lists; `size` is the slab size, `slabcur_addr` the lowest partial slab of the class in the pointer's arena, a huge run is `(0, 1, usable)`, and a pointer that is not a live allocation gets zeros rather than an abort. Reason: Valkey reads `out[3*i..]` positionally; the query is a statistics read, not a `free`, so a stale pointer must not kill the server.
- Phase 1 task 5: the library does not define `malloc_conf`/`je_malloc_conf`; it keeps reading a program's definition through the weak GOT references of task 1. `malloc_message` and `je_malloc_message` are two separate exported Rust statics (the plain one is consulted first). Reason: rustc's cdylib export list carries only Rust items, so data symbols defined in `global_asm!` (the first attempt) stay local under its version script, and stable Rust has neither weak statics nor symbol aliases; a program defining the conf symbol is the only case consumers depend on and it already works (`tests/c/conf.c`).
- Phase 1 task 5: `thread.allocatedp`/`thread.deallocatedp` point into a per-heap `ThreadStats` copy that every read of any of the four `thread.*allocated*` keys refreshes (`Heap::materialise_stats`) and that is stale in between. Reason: the counters are derived from cache headers since Phase 0 step 6, so no live word exists to point at.
- Phase 1 task 5: `thread.tcache.enabled:false` folds the derived counters into `Heap::uncached`, flushes and frees every cache of every arena and sets a per-heap flag that makes `ensure_cache` return null (the existing direct slab path); `true` clears the flag. Only `ensure_cache` (refill slow path) changed and the two new `Heap` fields sit after `caches`, so the fast paths are instruction-for-instruction the same and no benchmark row was taken. Reason: jemalloc's key semantics with the cheapest slow-path-only implementation.
- Phase 1 task 5: `arena.<i>.purge` and `arena.<i>.destroy` flush the calling thread's caches before purging, as `malloc_trim` does; `arena.<i>.destroy` is that purge for an arena other than 0 (`EFAULT` for 0 and `MALLCTL_ARENAS_ALL`, as jemalloc) and leaves the arena usable. Reason: Valkey's `MEMORY PURGE` runs `arena.4096.purge` on the main thread, whose caches can pin up to 1024 slots per class in `Active` slabs; user memory cannot be freed on the caller's behalf and jemalloc requires a destroyed arena to be empty anyway.
- Phase 1 task 5: `version` is `"5.3.0-0-gchalloc"`. Reason: consumers split jemalloc's `<major>.<minor>.<bugfix>-<nrev>-g<gid>` on `-` and `.` (Redis's `mem_allocator`, tikv-jemalloc-ctl), and the gid field is where the truth fits.
- Phase 1 task 5: `stats.cgroup.*` is `ENOENT` until Phase 2 task 5 wires the cgroup sampler; `arenas.nlextents` reads 0 (there is no lextent table); `max_background_threads` reads 1 and accepts any non-zero write; writing `background_thread:true` starts the thread synchronously (`EAGAIN` if it cannot), `false` withdraws the request. Reason: keys that exist must answer honestly; jemalloc creates its background thread synchronously on this write and Valkey's `jemalloc-bg-thread yes` expects it to exist afterwards.
- Phase 1 task 5: the `*allocx` family leaves `errno` alone on failure (jemalloc's do), `rallocx(NULL, ..)` allocates like `mallocx`, `dallocx(NULL)` is a no-op, `sallocx`/`xallocx` of null or of a non-allocation abort; an in-place `rallocx` (same class, or same huge page count at a suitably aligned start) ignores `MALLOCX_ARENA` as jemalloc's `arena_ralloc` does; `xallocx` always reports the current usable size because challoc never grows a block in place; `MALLOCX_ZERO` zeroes the whole usable size unless the allocator knows the memory reads as zero. Reason: jemalloc's documented contracts where it has them, the cheapest honest answer where it does not.
- Phase 1 task 5: `mallinfo2`/`mallinfo` map challoc figures onto glibc's fields so `arena >= uordblks`, `fordblks = arena - uordblks` and `hblkhd` = mmapped bytes hold (table in `api/glibc.rs`), computed live; `malloc_info` requires `options == 0` and a stream (`-1`/`EINVAL` otherwise) and emits glibc's `<malloc version="1">` shape with one `<heap>` per arena; `malloc_stats` writes to stderr with `write(2)` and never through the hook; `malloc_stats_print` honours `J`, `g`, `a`, `b` and puts its JSON under a top-level `"jemalloc"` object. Reason: programs parse these shapes, not our names.
- Phase 1 task 5: `je_` aliases cover the libc entry points too (`je_malloc`, `je_free`, `je_calloc`, `je_realloc`, `je_aligned_alloc`, `je_memalign`, `je_posix_memalign`, `je_valloc`, `je_malloc_usable_size`). Reason: Valkey's bundled jemalloc is built with `--with-jemalloc-prefix=je_`, so its code calls `je_malloc`; the task 7 patch then needs no renames.

- Phase 1 task 6: the `jemalloc-soname` feature only sets `DT_SONAME` (`build.rs` emits `cargo:rustc-cdylib-link-arg=-Wl,-soname,libjemalloc.so.2` when `CARGO_FEATURE_JEMALLOC_SONAME` is set); the file is still `target/<dir>/release/libchalloc.so` and `scripts/jemalloc-compat.sh` copies it to `libjemalloc.so.2`. Reason: Cargo cannot rename a cdylib per feature, and the soname is the only thing a link-only consumer records (`DT_NEEDED`), so it is the only thing the library itself must carry. The default build stays without a soname, as before.
- Phase 1 task 6: the compat build goes to its own target dir (`target/jemalloc-compat`, ~2 s for a fresh fat-LTO build) and `make test` runs the staging script after `cargo build --release`. Reason: building both feature sets into `target/release` would flip the artifact `make test` loads under `LD_PRELOAD` on every alternation; the separate dir costs nothing noticeable and lets the test check both variants side by side (same export list, soname only on the compat one).
- Phase 1 task 6: the stage has `lib/libjemalloc.so.2`, a `lib/libjemalloc.so -> libjemalloc.so.2` link name and `lib/pkgconfig/jemalloc.pc`, no header and no `libjemalloc.a`. Reason: the plan asks for the `.so.2` and the pc file; the link name is what makes `-ljemalloc` resolve at all, while headers belong to `jemalloc-dev` (a program that includes `<jemalloc/jemalloc.h>` co-installs it, as the Phase 3 `challoc-jemalloc-compat` package will assume) and a static `libjemalloc.a` would let a `-static` link pick up an allocator whose fork and TLS behaviour Phase 1 has not tested that way.
- Phase 1 task 6: `jemalloc.pc` follows upstream's `jemalloc.pc.in` field for field (`prefix`, `exec_prefix`, `libdir`, `includedir`, `install_suffix`, `Cflags: -I${includedir}/jemalloc`, `Libs: -L${libdir} -ljemalloc`) with `Version: 5.3.0`, taken from `mallctl.rs`'s `VERSION_STR` minus the `-0-gchalloc` tail. Reason: consumers' `pkg-config --atleast-version` and any `${libdir}` substitution keep working unchanged; `prefix` defaults to the staging dir so the staged pc file is usable as is, and `PREFIX=/usr` (`make jemalloc-compat PREFIX=/usr`) writes the installed layout for packaging.
- Phase 1 task 7: the Valkey patch adds `MALLOC=challoc` (also `USE_CHALLOC=yes`) to `src/Makefile`: `-DUSE_JEMALLOC -DUSE_CHALLOC`, `-lchalloc` first in `FINAL_LIBS`, no `jemalloc` dependency target; `zmalloc.h` and `allocator_defrag.h` include `<challoc/jemalloc.h>` under `USE_CHALLOC` and `HAVE_DEFRAG` is defined for it. Reason: challoc exports the `je_` names itself (task 5), so unlike Debian's `USE_SYSTEM_JEMALLOC` patch no symbol renames are needed, and `HAVE_DEFRAG` otherwise requires `VALKEY_VENDORED_JEMALLOC`, which only the bundled build defines.
- Phase 1 task 7: the header Valkey compiles against is challoc's own `contrib/valkey/include/challoc/jemalloc.h` (the `je_` prototypes, jemalloc's `MALLOCX_*`/`MALLCTL_ARENAS_*` bit layouts, `JEMALLOC_VERSION*` 5.3.0 with `challoc` in the gid field), under `<challoc/...>`, not `<jemalloc/...>`. Reason: it documents exactly the API challoc guarantees, never collides with `jemalloc-dev`, and Phase 3's `challoc-dev` can install it as is; Valkey compiles with `-Wundef -Werror`, so it uses `__SIZEOF_POINTER__` rather than jemalloc's internal `LG_SIZEOF_PTR`.
- Phase 1 task 7: `ZMALLOC_LIB` under `USE_CHALLOC` is `"jemalloc-5.3.0-challoc"`. Reason: Valkey's tests and `INFO` consumers glob `*jemalloc*` on `mem_allocator` to decide whether defrag, `MEMORY PURGE` and `MEMORY MALLOC-STATS` apply, and the suffix makes `valkey-server --version` name the real library (Phase 3's package test).
- Phase 1 task 7: `malloc_stats_print`'s text banner is jemalloc's (`___ Begin jemalloc statistics ___` / `--- End jemalloc statistics ---`), with the `Version:` line saying challoc. Reason: Valkey's `MEMORY MALLOC-STATS` coverage test asserts `*jemalloc*` in the text, the same reason the JSON form already keeps its top-level `"jemalloc"` object.
- Phase 1 task 7: `layout::NBINS`/`BIN_MAX`: the `mallctl` bins (`arenas.nbins`, `arenas.bin.<j>`, `stats.arenas.<i>.bins.<j>`, `small.*`) are the classes whose slab is the minimum 64 KiB, i.e. up to 4 KiB (32 for `quantum8`, 28 for `quantum16`); larger classes stay slab-allocated but are reported like jemalloc's large extents (`large.{allocated,active,dirty,retained}` gain the medium slabs, `ArenaStats` has `large_active`/`large_dirty`/`large_retained`; the utilization queries answer `(0, 1, class size)`; Valkey's `allocatorShouldDefrag` then skips them as it skips jemalloc's large allocations). Reason: Valkey's `frag_smallbins` metric sums `(nregs * curslabs - curregs) * size` over the bins and its defrag tests need it under 2 MiB on an idle server and under 2.5 MiB after defrag; jemalloc's bins have slabs of a few pages, while a fresh Valkey under challoc showed 7.4 MB (a 4 MiB slab of the 160 KiB class with 4 live slots alone counts 3.4 MB) and, after defrag, 0.93 MB of the 2.79 MB residue sat in the 5120..14336 classes as a handful of live blocks each pinning a 128..256 KiB slab, which no defragmenter can recover. Design 13.4 amended accordingly.
- Phase 1 task 7: `experimental.utilization.{query,batch_query}` report `size` as `nregs * size` (the region bytes of the slab) rather than the power-of-two slab size. Reason: Valkey's `allocatorShouldDefrag` computes `size / nregs` and asserts it is a bin size; jemalloc's slabs are exact multiples of the region size, challoc's are not (`65536 / 204 = 321` for the 320 class), and the assertion killed the server in the big-list defrag test.
- Phase 1 task 7: `Dockerfile.dev` adds `tcl` (9.0.4, provides the `tclsh9.0` that `./runtest` looks for) and `openssl-dev` (Wolfi builds Valkey with `BUILD_TLS=yes`). `contrib/valkey/build.sh` clones tag 9.1.2 (Wolfi's `valkey-9.1.yaml`, expected commit checked), builds challoc with `quantum8` into `target/valkey/challoc`, links with `-L`/`-rpath` to it, and checks `--version` and the ELF `DT_NEEDED`/`RPATH` (no `ldd` in the image). Reason: the plan's script has to run in the dev container and be idempotent across iterations.
- Phase 1 task 7: `arena.<i>.purge` and `arena.<i>.decay` treat `i == arenas.narenas` as every arena, like `MALLCTL_ARENAS_ALL`. Reason: jemalloc's pre-5.0 convention, still honoured by 5.3, and what Valkey's `jemalloc_purge` (`MEMORY PURGE`, synchronous `FLUSHALL`) calls; the first full `./runtest` died on `ERR Error purging dirty pages` in `unit/other`.
- Phase 1 task 7: `Dockerfile.dev` also adds `procps`. Reason: Valkey's test harness shells out to `ps -p <pid> -f` (`process_is_alive`) and BusyBox `ps` has no `-p`, so `wait_for_cluster_state` skipped every node and the first slot-bound write of each cluster fixture raced Valkey's 2 s `CLUSTER_WRITABLE_DELAY` (`CLUSTERDOWN The cluster is down`, reproduced by hand: a fresh single node reports `cluster_state:fail` for about 1.5 s after slot assignment, then `ok`). Two full-suite runs died on it before the cause was found; not an allocator fault.
- Phase 1 task 7: `Dockerfile.dev` also adds `posix-libc-utils-2.44` (`getconf`, `ldd`). Reason: `tests/integration/rdb.tcl` runs `getconf PAGESIZE`, `replication.tcl` `getconf CLK_TCK` and `util.tcl` `ldd` on the server binary; a missing command is a test-client exception that aborts the whole `./runtest` run. The 2.44 stream matches the image's glibc swap.
- Phase 1 task 8: the jemalloc baseline is a second, unpatched clone of the Valkey tag built with `MALLOC=jemalloc` (bundled 5.3.0) in `target/valkey/src-<tag>-jemalloc`, cloned from the local checkout (the patch there is uncommitted, so the clone is pristine) and built by the soak script on first use. Reason: the acceptance criterion compares with bundled jemalloc 5.3.0 and one tree cannot hold both builds.
- Phase 1 task 8: p99 comes from the load generators (valkey-benchmark `--csv` `p99_latency_ms`, memtier's `Totals` p99), not from `INFO latencystats`. Reason: latencystats measures command execution time only (p50 reads 0.001 usec under pipelining), not the client-observed latency the criterion means.
- Phase 1 task 8: the workload is SET/DEL over a bounded 500k-key keyspace per generator (valkey-benchmark `key:<12 digits>`, memtier `key:<n>`), no list/hash pushes. Reason: valkey-benchmark's lpush/rpush tests grow one key without bound and would exceed the 7.8 GB Docker VM within a 10-minute write phase; SET with 16-1024 byte values gives the size mix that fragments slabs.
- Phase 1 task 8: the same `valkey-benchmark`/`valkey-cli` binaries from the challoc tree drive both servers, and memtier is Wolfi's package. Reason: benchmark protocol, same client binary for every allocator; only the server changes.
- Phase 1 acceptance (step 1): the p99 half of the soak criterion is read as inconclusive on the Docker VM and decided on the GCE VMs (Phase 1.5); the RSS halves are decided here. Reason: the task 8 smoke run measured +-7% memtier p99 and 0.6-1.6 ms valkey-benchmark p99 between identical runs on this shared 10-CPU VM, larger than the observed write-phase gap, while RSS figures are reproducible to 1%.
- Phase 0 regression fix: in guard-install mode `Huge::prepare` commits `[mark, end)`, not `[max(s, mark), end)`, so an alignment hole below an aligned run is committed the moment `take` records it as a free extent. Reason: the module's invariant is that every page below the mark is committed apart from fallback-mode guards, and `find`/`take` place later runs in the hole without a further commit; committing the hole here costs nothing extra (the same single `mprotect`, over a longer range) and keeps the fallback mode's per-allocation `mprotect` out of the install mode.
- Phase 0 regression fix: the non-Linux `os` stub in `huge.rs` keeps a `cfg(test)` log of committed ranges and the test `Env::alloc` checks every run against it (Linux: reads the run's first and last usable byte instead). Reason: host tests run the install mode over addresses they cannot touch, and the exhaustive host tests passed for months over an allocator whose install mode handed out `PROT_NONE` memory; a commit invariant the host can check is what would have caught it.
- Phase 0 regression fix: a run's pages are checked, not the guard page. Reason: in install mode the guard page is committed and guarded, in fallback mode `PROT_NONE`; touching it is a fault in both.
- Phase 1 acceptance (step 1): the soak result is analysed and recorded before any remediation, and the `unit/memefficiency` / full-suite status is cited from task 7's run because `src/` has not changed since. Reason: same procedure as Phase 0 acceptance (record, then remediate, one results file per step); a 486 s rerun of an unchanged library adds nothing.

- Phase 1 acceptance (step 2): the minimum slab stays 64 KiB; a 16 KiB minimum (for every class, and for the classes from 256 bytes up only) was built, measured and rejected. Reason: on the arm64 reference VM 16 KiB for every class halved `xmalloc-test` (64-byte cross-thread frees, 374 -> 185 M frees/s: four times the slabs per refill and flush) and 8 KiB cost 70%; the two-regime form (64 KiB below 256 bytes, `max(16 KiB, 16 * size)` above) kept xmalloc-test inside noise but cost larson 2.2% (arm64) and 3.8% (x86_64), and the full 20-minute soak on the VM left the post-delete RSS at +22.7% vs jemalloc with the surviving slabs at 43..48% occupancy in every class, the same occupancy the 64 KiB slabs left on the Mac; jemalloc's own 16..32-region bins end at 47..55% and only its 4-region bin at 86%. `bench/results/2026-09-16-aarch64-gce-min-slab.md` has the rows and the reading. The code keeps the rule expressible (`layout::SMALL_SLAB_BELOW`, `LG_SMALL_SLAB`, `lg_slab_for` public) so a 4..8-region geometry for the 512..1024-byte classes can be tried in step 3 without re-plumbing.
- Phase 1 acceptance (step 2): `layout::BIN_MAX` is the fixed size 4096, no longer `MIN_SLAB / 16`. Reason: the bins Valkey's defragmenter and `frag_smallbins` see must not move when the minimum slab does; the boundary was chosen for the reported-fragmentation floor of the medium classes (task 7).
- Phase 1 acceptance (step 2): `SlabTable` carries `slabs_per_page = max(1, page / slab_size)`; when it is above 1 (a slab smaller than the kernel page, which design section 1 permits up to 64 KiB) `commit_next` commits the whole page group with the group's first slab, a guard group is `slabs_per_page` consecutive guard slabs with one `MADV_GUARD_INSTALL` (or one `PROT_NONE` page) at its start, and `purge_empty` discards a group only when every slab in it is committed, owned by the purging arena and `Fresh`, `Empty` or `Purged` (every `Empty` one flips to `Purged`, a younger one included). Reason: the geometry is a compile-time table read by the `free` fast path and `mprotect`/`madvise` are page-granular, so any minimum slab below 64 KiB needs this; committing a page at a time is free (`next_slab` is monotonic) and a purge must not discard a neighbour another arena may be claiming under a lock this thread does not hold. Dormant at 64 KiB (`slabs_per_page` is 1 on every supported page size); tested on the host and on Linux with a faked page four times the slab (`class::tests::page_groups_when_the_page_is_larger_than_the_slab`). Known limitation: a page group holding slabs of two arenas is never purged.
- Phase 1 acceptance (step 2): tests that assumed one refill fits in one slab (`heap::tests::caches_are_keyed_by_arena`, `set_arena_swaps_cache_arrays`, `stats::tests::identities_hold_through_the_lifecycle`, `api::mallctl::tests::arena_keys_create_lookup_purge_decay_and_destroy`) derive the slab count from `default_cap / 2` and `nslots`; `free_of_a_never_allocated_slot_aborts` uses the 16-byte class. Reason: they failed under the 16 KiB trial and would again under any geometry change.
- Phase 1 acceptance (step 2): `api::tests::background_thread_starts_purges_and_is_absent_after_fork` no longer asserts the freed huge run is still `Empty` before polling for the purge. Reason: with the test's 1 ms decay the background thread can purge it before the freeing thread runs again; seen once on the loaded Mac Docker VM, passed on both GCE VMs, the poll loop is the real assertion.
- Phase 1 acceptance: the consumer's configuration is never changed to satisfy an acceptance criterion (the contrib Valkey patch keeps stock `active-defrag-ignore-bytes` and all other defrag settings) and the plan's criteria are not relaxed; challoc must meet them. Reason: the human's call on 2026-09-16, "we want better overall performance in our allocator".

- Phase 1 acceptance step 3 (b), the compacting cache (`docs/design.md` 6.4, review P1): implemented as scheduled address-ordered pops with three departures from the review's sketch, each forced by a VM measurement recorded in `bench/results/2026-09-17-aarch64-gce-compacting-cache.md`. (1) The run of low addresses is *descending* in the slot array (the pre-change reversed refill batch, lowest on top) and the scheduled pop serves `slots[run_end - 1]`, not an ascending run with a cursor: an ascending batch makes ordinary pops descend and xmalloc-test's consumer then pushes descending over an ascending kept half, which takes the flush's `sort_unstable` off its O(n) sorted-input path (-76% on the arm64 VM, the mirror of it11's -41% reverse pathology). The flush is therefore unchanged and drops the run; a rebuild claims a `cap / 4` block from the lowest partial slab and swaps it into the bottom of the stack, no sort. (2) The schedule is gated per cache: probe mode asks the class once per 4096 pops and compacts only while the class has at least 4 slabs' worth and 1/32 of holes (`ClassState::holes`) *and* its claim counter advanced by less than a slab's worth since the previous probe (`ClassState::claimed`; ordinary refills already take the lowest partial slab, and xmalloc-test's producers run 0..8 slabs of transient holes through a 150..455-slab class that way, measured with `bench/probes/binsample.c`). Unconditional at `k = 3` the schedule cost larson -15..-22% and alloc-test +8..+15%; gated, `new` is indistinguishable from `new0` (schedule off) on larson and alloc-test. A rebuild re-checks and drops back to probing. (3) `run_end <= low_water <= len` keeps the run intact without a second fast-path compare (the `low_water` branch clips both), which means a refill now starts `low_water` at the batch length instead of 0: a GC that finds a refilled batch untouched releases three quarters of it (the design's GC semantics; the old zero was a special case, two tests updated). The probe pop also runs `tick`, so a balanced churn on a dense class still gets GC and decay on a thread without a background thread (the churn probe's emptied slabs were otherwise never purged). Default `tcache_compact_shift:3` (`k` is the plan's; the gate, not `k`, is what keeps the dense benchmarks whole); 0 turns the schedule off. Cache header 32 -> 48 bytes.
- Phase 1 acceptance step 3 (b): the refill-rate half of the gate reads `ClassState::refilled` (cache refills only), not `claimed`. The first 20-minute soak with the compacting cache (97e0cf8) came back with the residue frozen at 34..46% and RSS after settle 157.3 vs 131.2 MiB: Valkey's active defragmenter allocates its copies with `MALLOCX_TCACHE_NONE`, i.e. `alloc_direct` claims from the lowest partial slab, hundreds per second, and the gate took that for ordinary refills in every probe window (and the random-size churn's 64-slot refills sat exactly at the one-slab threshold). Rebuild and direct claims no longer count and the threshold is two slabs' worth per window; the churn probe, larson, xmalloc-test and alloc-test are unaffected (xmalloc-test refills thousands of slots per window). Lesson for the gate: measure the *cache* traffic, not the class's.
- Phase 1 acceptance step 3 (b): the compaction gate constants (`COMPACT_MIN_SLABS = 4`, `COMPACT_MIN_RATIO = 32`, `COMPACT_MAX_REFILL_SLABS = 2`, `PROBE_MASK = 4095`) are `heap.rs` constants, not `malloc_conf` keys: nothing in Valkey or Ruby would set them and each one has a measured reason; promote to tunables only if a consumer needs it. The floor they leave uncompacted is under 4 slabs' worth of holes per class (256 KiB for the 640..1024 classes).
- Phase 1 acceptance step 3 (b): `api::print::tests::text_report_has_every_section_and_live_figures`, the GCE watch item, failed on both the Mac and the x86_64 VM in this iteration with the message captured: the text report began with the JSON test's output. The tests' `CAPTURE` buffer is module-wide and `report()` cleared and read it while another test's `malloc_stats_print` appended chunks; `report()` now holds a test mutex for the whole call. Not an allocator bug; the watch item is closed.

- Phase 1 acceptance (2026-09-17): the no-consumer-changes rule is narrowed by the human. The soak post-delete RSS criterion stays measured under stock Valkey configuration. After step 3 lands and the four `unit/memefficiency` defrag failures are rerun (plan step 4), `#ifdef USE_CHALLOC` values in the contrib patch are allowed where the Valkey constant encodes jemalloc's slab geometry (the test suite's 2 MB / 5% / frag < 1.05 defrag thresholds; `active-defrag-ignore-bytes` only if the residue is provably pinned objects), each with a design decision naming the jemalloc constant it replaces and the measurement behind it. Reason: Valkey's defragmenter is a jemalloc feature calibrated to 4..32-region slabs (`docs/review-2026-09-17.md`); holding challoc to those constants measures the wrong thing, holding it to stock-config RSS measures the right one.
- Phase 1.5 (2026-09-17): the reference VMs move to `us-east4-a`; the `us-central1-b` pair was deleted the same day, after the east pair produced iteration 14's numbers. Reason: `ZONE_RESOURCE_POOL_EXHAUSTED` on starts in us-central1 (2026-09-17 04:34 UTC and, per the human, for weeks); both machine types, the same CPU models (Neoverse V2, Xeon Platinum 8581C) and kernel 7.0.0-1011-gcp verified there with `pmu-check`; `challoc-bench start` now retries. Numbers from the two zones are comparable in principle but no A/B row mixes them.

- Phase 1 acceptance step 3 (c): the compaction diagnostics stay in the product. Five per-class counters (`ClassState::compacted`, `compact_on`, `compact_off`, `gate_refill`, with `refilled`) are written only on the cold paths (rebuild, mode changes, declined probes) and printed as the last columns of the bins table of `malloc_stats_print`, followed by a per-class occupancy histogram of the non-empty slabs (one lock hold per non-full slab, only when the report is asked for). Reason: `MEMORY MALLOC-STATS` in a soak is the only window into the schedule, and without it two iterations argued about five candidate causes that the counters excluded in one run; the JSON report and the mallctl namespace are unchanged so nothing jemalloc-shaped moves.
- Phase 1 acceptance step 3 (c): the post-delete residue is recorded as pinned live keys (values idle for minutes in a churn that turns every other key over in under a second), so its remedy is allocator-side slab geometry for the 512..1024-byte classes, not cache policy and not a consumer change. Reason: the tracer and the keyspace observer in `bench/results/2026-09-17-aarch64-gce-compacting-cache-soak3.md`; the plan's step 4 allows `active-defrag-ignore-bytes` only for provably pinned objects and never for the soak criterion, and the soak's memtier flags are part of the protocol both allocators run under.
- Phase 1 acceptance step 3 (c), slab geometry (f27d736, e6b89c3): the 512..1024-byte classes use the smallest power-of-two slab from 4 KiB up whose internal waste (`slab - nslots * size`) is at most a thirty-second of the slab (`layout::LG_MIN_SLAB` 12, `SMALL_SLAB_BELOW` 512, `SLAB_WASTE_DIV` 32, `lg_slab_for`): 4 KiB for 512 and 1024 (8 and 4 slots), 8 KiB for 896 (9), 16 KiB for 640 and 768 (25 and 21); classes below 512 bytes keep 64 KiB (`LG_SMALL_SLAB`), the medium tier keeps `max(64 KiB, 16 * size)` (`LG_MEDIUM_MIN_SLAB`). Reason: the residue is pinned live values in exactly these classes, each holding its whole slab (`bench/results/2026-09-17-aarch64-gce-compacting-cache-soak3.md`); jemalloc's 1 KiB bin has 4 regions. The waste bound was 1/16 first (4 KiB for 640 and 768 too, commit f27d736): it took the soak's peak RSS from 568 to 616 MiB (VmHWM), 21 MiB of it the 640 class's 256 wasted bytes per 4 KiB slab under 549k live values (valkey-benchmark's 512-byte values land there), against a 5% peak criterion; at 1/32 the two classes fall back to 16 KiB, whose waste is 2.3% and 1.6%. Power-of-two slabs are what make `p & (slab - 1)` the in-slab offset, so jemalloc's 20 KiB / 12 KiB slabs for these two classes are not available. Numbers in `bench/results/2026-09-17-{aarch64,x86_64}-gce-slab-geometry.md`.
- Phase 1 acceptance step 3 (c): the compaction thresholds (`COMPACT_MIN_SLABS`, `COMPACT_MAX_REFILL_SLABS`) and the guard slab interval (`guard_slab_interval`) count 64 KiB slabs' worth of slots and bytes whatever the class's slab size (`Class::compact_slots`, `layout::LG_COMPACT_UNIT`, `heap::guard_interval_for`, `LG_GUARD_UNIT`). Reason: both were calibrated in bytes with 64 KiB slabs in mind (four slabs of holes are worth compacting; one guard per eight slabs); with 4-slot slabs "two slabs' worth of refills" would be eight slots and every refill would close the gate, and "one guard per eight slabs" would put a `PROT_NONE` or guard page every 32 KiB (35k VMAs for the soak's 640 class on a kernel without `MADV_GUARD_INSTALL`). The `churn_model` tests fill the same 2560 slots as before (640 slabs of 4) and bound in the same bytes; the floor they measure (`COMPACT_MIN_SLABS` x 64 KiB of holes) is where compaction stops, as designed.
- Phase 1 acceptance step 3 (c): `MAX_PICKS` 8 -> 16 (one lock hold covers a 128-slot refill of the 512 class and a 64-slot refill or a 64-slot sorted flush of the 1024 class, 16 slabs each), and `purge::Batch::push` merges adjacent ranges (a run of empty 4 KiB slabs is one `madvise` / one iovec). Reason: the plan's P6 note; a hold that reserves from 16 slabs is longer, but one hold per refill is what the pre-change library had for these classes and the A/B rows are within 3% with it.

- Phase 1 acceptance step 4: the contrib patch's `tests/unit/memefficiency.tcl` hunk gives `perform_defrag_test` an `ignore_bytes` option (default the stock `2500kb`) and `test_eval_scripts` passes `5000kb` when `mem_allocator` matches `*challoc*`; nothing else in the suite and no production default changes. The constant it replaces is the test's `active-defrag-ignore-bytes 2500kb`, and what that constant encodes is not slab geometry but the bundled jemalloc's `je_` prefix: Lua's `l_alloc` is plain `realloc`/`free`, so under stock Valkey the Lua heap sits in glibc malloc outside `allocator_frag_bytes`, whereas challoc replaces libc malloc and serves it. Measurement (`bench/results/2026-09-17-valkey-suite.md`): `used_memory_vm_eval` 12,126,208 under both allocators for the test's 50k scripts, jemalloc's `allocator_allocated` 24.87 MB vs challoc's 36.68 MB at the same `used_memory`; the residue after defrag is 3.16 MB (standalone) / 3.58 MB (cluster) on both architectures, 2,391,200 B of it in the 80-byte class where 13,312 not-yet-swept Lua strings and 1,024 permanent Lua objects pin 38 of 54 slabs (`collectgarbage` empties the 38 and the frag falls to 1.75 MB), and the defragmenter re-moves 626..833 unrelated objects per cycle with no effect because it never visits Lua objects. The plan's three allocator-side answers were tried: page-group `batch_query` and fullest-slab `TCACHE_NONE` placement do not touch `curslabs` of the pinned class; 4 KiB slabs for the 80-byte class do make the test pass (residue 407,600 B, one cycle) but are a `size == 80` special case without a rule (the 1/32 rule would also shrink the 64-byte class, whose 16 KiB slabs halved `xmalloc-test`), so they are recorded and not adopted. Reason: the narrowed rule allows a `USE_CHALLOC` test constant only where the residue is provably pinned objects; it is (Lua garbage and permanent Lua objects), and a stock-configured challoc Valkey is unaffected (100 MB / 10% defaults). Flagged under Notes for the human because the constant encodes the prefix/glibc split rather than slab geometry.
- Phase 1 acceptance, Ruby: the library defines `malloc_conf` (a plain exported Rust static in `api/jemalloc.rs`, null) and keeps reading it through the GOT (`config::malloc_conf_addr`, no longer `.weak`); `je_malloc_conf` stays a weak undefined reference. Reason: Ruby's `--with-jemalloc` configure probe links a program that reads `malloc_conf` against `-ljemalloc` (`configure.ac`, `rb_cv_jemalloc_library`), so a library that only references the symbol fails it with "jemalloc requested but not found". jemalloc's own definition is weak; stable Rust has no weak statics, and the `global_asm!` weak definition plus `--export-dynamic-symbol` route noted under task 5 was tested (`.tmp-exp`, GNU ld) and rejected: rustc's version script keeps the symbol local, and a local definition binds the library's own GOT read to itself, hiding a program's string. The strong definition changes nothing for a dynamically linked program that defines the symbol, since the executable comes first in the loader's lookup (`c_conf_plain_symbol_interposes_the_library_definition`); on x86_64 a PIE that merely reads it gets a COPY relocation, so `jemalloc_link.c` checks the value, not the address. `je_malloc_conf` is left undefined because Valkey defines it and a strong definition would clash under a static link. Design 13.2 amended.
- Phase 1.5 task 4 (form a, rejected): the affinity was a per-cache hint slab plus a per-slab stamp of the heap whose hint it is (the descriptor's `dirty_at` word while `Active`), `plan` taking the hint first, then the lowest partial slab not another heap's hint, then empty/purged/fresh slabs, then another heap's hint slab; rebuild claims lowest-first, direct claims pure first fit. Reason it was tried this way: the plan's hint alone cannot separate threads (every heap's first refill and every hint miss goes to the same lowest partial slab). Reason it was rejected: measured on both VMs it does not separate them either (L1D misses/op unchanged; every hint miss, every multi-slab batch and every flush leaks slots between threads, and the per-thread cache then keeps the interleaving LIFO), while the descriptor walk under the class lock costs 5..20%. Only set-level partitioning (arenas) moves the counters; see plan 4c.
- Phase 1.5 task 4c (form b): `narenas` defaults to 0 = auto, resolved once at `Shared::init` to `min(4 * ncpus, 64)` (`Config::effective_narenas`, `MAX_AUTO_ARENAS`) and stored so `opt.narenas` reports the number in use; `ncpus` is the width of `sched_getaffinity(0)`'s mask (new header-checked syscall wrapper `sys::ncpus`; cpusets and `taskset` count, a failed call reports 1, i.e. 4 arenas). Reason: the plan's formula is jemalloc's, and the cap bounds the per-heap-creation table walk and the partial slab sets a thread-heavy program can hold. The probe (3 rounds, both VMs) put `narenas:16` and the default within 2% of each other on 10-thread larson; the plan's formula was kept.
- Phase 1.5 task 4c: `Arenas::assign` is least-loaded under the creation lock with a per-arena count of bound thread heaps (`Arenas::bound`, `bind`, `unbind`; released in `Heap::destroy`, moved by `set_arena`, never counted for explicit caches). Ties go to an already built arena, then the lowest index (jemalloc's `arena_choose_hard`). Consequences relied on: the first heap always lands on arena 0 and a lone heap never builds a second arena, so `multi_arena` stays clear and single-threaded programs pay nothing on `free` (`new` at `narenas:1` equals `old` in the probe; no fast-path change was needed); a thread that comes and goes takes the arena the previous one left; larson's spawn-before-exit pattern therefore keeps about two arenas per lane in rotation instead of all of them.
- Phase 1.5 task 4c: direct (`MALLOCX_TCACHE_NONE`) allocations on a thread that has no heap yet still come from arena 0 (`arena::alloc`, unchanged); the `utilization_queries` test compares owners with each other instead of assuming arena 0.
- Phase 1.5 task 4c, the quick gate: on amd64 the first `quick` said NO-GO on two rows, larson1 +5.1% (one round; a 3-round probe on a quiet VM put it at -3%, better than round-robin `narenas:16`'s -5.4%) and xmalloc-test +57% against `old` at parity with jemalloc. The xmalloc-test cost of arenas on x86_64 is the one plan 4c names and asks to be measured against jemalloc, and no `narenas > 1` variant can pass a 5%-against-old gate on that row there, so the variant was taken to the protocol tier on the plan's text; the override is recorded here rather than by loosening `QUICK_TOLERANCE`. (A second `quick` run after the commit compared HEAD with itself, `OLD` not given: not evidence.) The driver's `perf-stat` appended `-d` after the container's `sh -c` arguments and never detached; it now inserts it after `docker run`.
- Phase 1.5 task 4b: mimalloc runs on the `--without-jemalloc` build, not on the `--with-jemalloc` one. Reason: the jemalloc-linked ruby carries `DT_NEEDED libjemalloc.so.2` and uses `malloc_usable_size` from that header's ABI; preloading mimalloc over it would still map a jemalloc library and mix two allocators' accounting. Wolfi's jemalloc is preloaded on the `--with-jemalloc` binary because the sonames match, so the loader binds `DT_NEEDED` to the preloaded copy (verified from `/proc/self/maps` before every run: only `/usr/lib/libjemalloc.so.2`).
- Phase 1.5 task 4b: both `gem list` and `erb_render.yml` are rows (the plan asked for one real script); `array_small_and` (15 ms) was passed over for `array_sort_block` (5 s of 8 KiB sort temporaries) next to `array_sample_100k__1k`; the ruby/benchmark rows run `--disable-gems` as Ruby's own `make benchmark` does, the startup and gem_list rows keep RubyGems. Wall time and RSS come from `rt` around the measured ruby (benchmark-driver's `-e` executable is a wrapper that execs `rt`), so benchmark-driver's host process is never in the numbers. benchmark-driver is a shallow clone of its default branch into `target/ruby/benchmark-driver`; the results header records its version and commit.
- Phase 1.5 task 4b: the two Ruby builds are installed into their configured prefixes (`target/ruby/install-{challoc,glibc}`) by `ruby-bench.sh` on first use because the build directory's `ruby` has no stdlib path (`rbconfig` LoadError); `contrib/ruby/build.sh` is unchanged. The x86_64 run started after 00:00 UTC; its file is named with its aarch64 partner's date, and says so.

- Phase 1.5 task 4d: `malloc_usable_size` keeps the exact bit check (a size query of a freed slot aborts). A variant that validated only the region and slot start (`SlabTable::locate`) was measured on the arm64 VM in the soak's write phase: 105 fewer instructions per SET and no change in cycles per command (3720 against 3704; the descriptor line the query loads is in L1 because the slot `zmalloc` queries is the one the cache just handed back), so the safety net stays; details in `bench/results/2026-09-18-aarch64-gce-valkey-throughput.md`.
- Phase 1.5 task 5: the per-class slot stacks stay separate 8 KiB-bin metadata objects instead of the plan's one contiguous slot block per heap. Reason: the block build (inline headers plus a 99 KiB block in a 128 KiB bin, per-class offsets from the ladder) failed `quick` on aarch64 (xmalloc -6.8%, median of three) and was worse on every x86_64 row; `perf stat`/`perf record` put the loss in L1D fills on xmalloc-test's freeing threads (3..4x, 49% of them on the `others` walk, the allocating threads unchanged), and four one-line variants bounded the cause to "a stack inside a 128 KiB-strided object" (a lone 8 KiB stack per 128 KiB object reproduces it; page-aligning the stacks or sliding them randomly per heap does not help; 8 KiB objects are at parity). The mechanism is not attributed (lines and 4 KiB geometry are identical, the L2 is physically indexed); the block would have bought another 10% of aarch64 dTLB refills (2.8 against 3.17 per operation). Details in `bench/results/2026-09-18-aarch64-gce-dense-headers.md`.
- Phase 1.5 task 5: `Heap` is `repr(C)` with the header array at offset 128 and `CacheHeader` is `repr(C, align(64))`, 56 bytes of fields padded to one line, hot fields first (`len`, `cap`, `low_water`, `zeroed`, `slots`, `allocs`, `compact_mask`). Reason: the fast paths address a header as `heap + 128 + 64 * c` and must never share a header line between classes; `Heap`'s small fields are 84 bytes, so the array starts at the second line boundary.
- Phase 1.5 task 4e: the sized check is class equality, `class_for(size, align) == class of p` (jemalloc's `nallocx(size, flags) == sallocx(p)`), strict on both sides, and a huge run passes it (the huge allocator's own check follows). Reason: arithmetic on two dense lines and no tolerance for a hint that would have put the slot in another class, which is what the deferred bit test relies on; a huge size hint has no class to compare and the huge free is exact anyway.
- Phase 1.5 task 4e: C23 `free_sized` checks `class_of(size)` and `free_aligned_sized` checks `class_for(size, align)`; `free_sized` of `aligned_alloc`/`memalign` memory whose alignment moved it to a larger class aborts, and `tests/c/libc_api.c` now frees its `memalign` block with `free_aligned_sized`. Reason: C23 7.24.3.4 defines `free_sized` only for `malloc`/`calloc`/`realloc` memory, and a lenient upper-bound-only check would blunt the guard the deferred path depends on.
- Phase 1.5 task 4e: the sized fast path reads neither the bitmap word nor the arena (both on the descriptor line), so the home cache holds slots of any arena on that path; `Heap::flush` releases the sorted batch through `Shared::release_routed`, which calls `ClassState::release_owned` (the old `release` loop returning the owned prefix instead of aborting) per run of another arena's slabs, one descriptor load per switch and nothing extra when every slab is the cache's own. `release` keeps the wrong-arena abort for direct callers. `MALLOCX_ARENA(a)` with the default cache pops whatever the cache holds, as jemalloc's `arena_malloc` does with a tcache. Reason: a second load from the descriptor line to read the arena would be the same miss the task removes.
- Phase 1.5 task 4e: `dallocx` (no size) stays the exact `free` with flags; `MALLOCX_TCACHE_NONE` and explicit caches run the same hint check, the former releasing straight to the slab where the bit is tested at once. Reason: the plan names the size hint as the substitute for the bit test; without one there is nothing to substitute.
- Phase 1.5 task 4f: a slab's bits in the class bitmap are `nslots` padded to a power of two below a line (512 bits) or a multiple of a line from there (`Class::slab_bits`), not `nslots` exactly. Reason: the plan allowed bits straddling words; padding makes a slab's bits whole words or one sub-word range, so claim/release stay one `fetch_or`/`fetch_and` per word with a per-word mask (`AtomicBitmap` keeps its shape, plus a shift and mask), and a 64-byte line holds a whole number of slabs, which is what makes line-sized groups possible. The padding costs at most a factor of two below a line (the 1024-byte class: 4 bits per slab, no padding; 80-byte class: 819 -> 1024 bits, 128 bytes per 64 KiB slab).
- Phase 1.5 task 4f: slabs go to arenas in groups of `slabs_per_line` consecutive slabs (the plan's first option; 128 for the 1024-byte class, 1 for classes below 256 bytes), committed one at a time by the owning arena; a group spans at most 2 MiB of the region (`LG_GROUP_SPAN`), and a class whose slabs would exceed that pads each slab's bits to a whole line instead (medium classes from 512 KiB slabs up: 64 bytes of bitmap per slab). Reason: the group is address space, not memory (a group's slabs are committed as used), but at the 4 GiB minimum region a 4 MiB-slab class has 1024 slabs, and 64 arenas each holding a 32-slab group would exhaust it; capped at 2 MiB, 64 arenas hold 128 MiB of any class's region. Descriptor stride `max(16, 64 / slabs_per_line)` so a descriptor line is also one arena's.
- Phase 1.5 task 4f: the arena on the `free` fast path comes from a dense owner array (one `u16` per group, `SlabTable::owner`), not from the descriptor, which keeps its `arena` field for the slow paths and stats. Reason: with several arenas the fast path's `desc.arena()` load would have been the same descriptor miss the task removes; the owner array is 2 bytes per group (2 KB for a 550 MiB heap of 1 KiB values). The alternative, pushing plain frees onto the home cache without the arena check and routing at the flush as the sized path already does (design 6.3), would drop the load entirely and is left as a proposal (Notes for the human).
- Phase 1.5 task 4f: `SlabTable::committed_slabs` is now the hand-out watermark (descriptors, bits and owner words exist below it; a slab below it may be uncommitted memory in its arena's group, `Fresh` with clear bits). A `free` into such a slab aborts on the clear bit ("invalid or double free", where it used to say "never allocated"); a pointer above the watermark still aborts with "never allocated". Reason: the check's abort point is unchanged (the call), only the message for one sub-case differs; page groups never straddle two arenas' groups (the group is a multiple of `slabs_per_page`), so the page-group purge rule lost its "slab of another arena in the group" case, which the page-group test now documents.
- Phase 1.5 acceptance remediation: `tcache_max` (new key, jemalloc's name and default 32768) makes `Config::cache_cap` zero for classes above it, so the slow paths serve them straight from the bitmaps (the existing `tcache:false` machinery); range 0..=`MEDIUM_MAX`. Reason: caching 40 KiB..256 KiB slots at 8 per (thread, arena, class) held 70 MiB of xmalloc-test's and most of mstress's excess RSS at 16 arenas; jemalloc caches nothing above 32 KiB and the objects cost more to touch than the class lock.
- Phase 1.5 acceptance remediation: a heap's cache for another arena (`Heap::others`) gets `Config::other_cache_cap`: the home ladder up to `BIN_MAX` (4 KiB) and zero above, decided in `ensure_cache` (slow path). Reason: a thread caches slots of every arena it frees into, so a foreign cache's residue is multiplied by the arena count (16 threads x 16 arenas x 12 classes x 8 slots of 4..32 KiB = 190 MiB in mstress) where jemalloc's single per-thread cache holds one set; cross-arena frees of >4 KiB objects are rare in every workload measured (mstress: 0.1% of objects) and go to the owner's class lock once each.
- Phase 1.5 acceptance remediation: an arena's slab sets (3 per class) are chunked, interleaved views (`TwoLevelBitmap::from_raw_chunked`: rows of `stride` words, chunks of one level-1 word plus its 64 level-0 words) of one lazily committed table per slab size (`class::SetTable`, `SET_GROUPS` = 11), reserved at arena creation and committed as a prefix by `take_group`; the arena's table lock nests inside the class locks and the fork handlers take it after them. Reason: one page-aligned table per set touched 2..4 pages per (arena, class) and was alloc-testN's whole excess (2.7 MiB VmRSS at 17 arenas), and a uniform table over all classes would either commit a 20 MiB level-1 block per arena (the 4 KiB-slab classes have 2^26 slabs) or reserve 1.3 GiB per arena; grouping by slab size keeps the reservation at the old per-arena footprint and the touched pages at one per (arena, slab size). The dense `from_raw` form and `words_needed` are unchanged for `huge.rs`. The table is a separate metadata object behind a pointer in `Arena` (so `Arena` keeps its size and the objects after it their offsets); measured neutral against the inline form on xmalloc-test -w 16 and larson10 (`bench/results/2026-09-18-{aarch64,x86_64}-gce-xmalloc-boxed.md`), kept for the smaller `Arena`.
- Phase 1.5 acceptance remediation: a heap's `n`-th `others` node is placed `n % 16` cache lines into a block padded by 15 lines (`heap::NODE_STAGGER_LINES`, `node_block_layout`; the node records its `pad`, `destroy` frees the block). Reason: with the 40 KiB class's foreign stack no longer allocated between nodes, successive nodes and their 8 KiB stacks sat exactly 12 KiB apart, a multiple of the L1D way size on both reference CPUs, and the cross-arena `free` walk of xmalloc-test's consumers missed on every node head (58% of the L1D misses in `Heap::other_caches`, +1.65 misses per free, -15% / -25% throughput on aarch64 / x86_64 at equal instructions); the old layout was spread by accident, this spreads it by construction.
- Phase 1.5 acceptance remediation: `Heap::alloc_slow` (the default path, not `alloc_in`) allocates slab classes above `tcache_max` from arena 0 whatever the heap's arena; `free` routes by owner as before. Reason: those classes are uncached and served under the class lock anyway, their slabs are 1..4 MiB, and each thread arena's partial slab per class multiplied by 16 arenas was mstress's remaining +18..32% RSS after the cache changes; jemalloc's large allocations are per-arena extents with no slab to keep partially filled.

- Phase 1.5 acceptance (2026-09-18, driver's decision on iteration 27's question): (a), implement jemalloc's progressive decay. Reason: design section 8 already says the decay policy is "jemalloc's in shape" and the RSS criterion compares against jemalloc at the same `dirty_decay_ms`, so purging `Empty` slabs along jemalloc's smoothstep curve over the window (the setting keeps jemalloc's meaning, "window of the curve") is fidelity to the design, not a new trade; (b) would read a design gap as a price and (c) invents a knob jemalloc consumers cannot set. Keep the flat behaviour reachable as `decay_curve:flat` only if it costs nothing; otherwise drop it. The soak and `unit/memefficiency` must be rerun after this and after iteration 27's cache changes before Phase 1.5 is called done.
- Phase 1.5 acceptance remediation (2026-09-18, iteration 28), progressive decay, the smallest form: no per-epoch cohort counters; a class's decay walk builds the age histogram of its `Empty` slabs from their `dirty_at` (200 steps, jemalloc's `SMOOTHSTEP_NSTEPS`), takes the allowance as the weighted sum with jemalloc's `h_steps` (smootherstep, 2^24 fixed point, `purge::WEIGHTS` built by a `const fn`) and purges the oldest steps down to it, the highest addresses first within the cut-off step so a refill still finds the lowest. Reason: the descriptors already hold every slab's exact age, so a persistent backlog would be a second copy of the same information that could drift from it; the walk is a slow-path/background-thread cost of one pass over the empty set per step. One new atomic per class (`next_decay`, the end of the current step) keeps the visit lock-free between walks.
- Phase 1.5 acceptance remediation (iteration 28): the allowance rounds up (jemalloc rounds its page count down), so a lone `Empty` slab is not purged before its cohort's weight is zero (step 199 of 200); the huge allocator purges whole extents, oldest first, and never below the allowance (an extent larger than the remaining excess is skipped for younger ones that fit), so a lone extent lasts the whole window as before. Reason: jemalloc's unit is a page and it splits extents to hit the limit exactly; challoc's units are a 16-page slab and an unsplit extent, and purging a lone 64 KiB slab or a lone 64 MiB hash table after 1% (or, for the extent, 11%) of the window to satisfy a rounding would trade a refault for nothing. Cohorts of many slabs or extents follow the curve to within one unit.
- Phase 1.5 acceptance remediation (iteration 28): no `decay_curve:flat` key. Reason: the driver allowed it only if free; it would be a config key, a branch in two walks and their tests for a shape jemalloc consumers cannot select.

- Step-back review 2026-09-18 (`docs/review-2026-09-18.md`, commit 17b5f6e; the human's "Go for it" on its next step, 2026-09-18): the review's plan text is in `docs/plan.md` as Phase 1.5 tasks 7-16, a reordered Phase 2 and Phase 3 prerequisites. Order: the release-readiness work (tasks 7-13: the calloc race seen once in `make test`, release assertions and `overflow-checks`, the flush-boundary and sized-path detection gaps, the fork handler's three missing locks, truthful docs and inert keys, seams, packaging decisions) comes before the usable-size chain (14) and the mstress row (15). Reason: the review found correctness and claim gaps that outrank a 10% RSS row on a 0.63 s synthetic, and the mstress fix ((d) in whole-group form) is the largest ownership change since task 4f and must not land before the calloc failure is understood. The mstress choice recorded in task 15 is the review's recommendation as accepted by the human; overrule it there if you disagree.

- Phase 1.5 task 7 (b): `check_zero` is a `MALLOC_CONF` key (design section 12 row added), not `cfg(debug_assertions)`. Reason: the campaign needs it on the release build the tests and benchmarks use, and a key can be turned on in a deployment to diagnose a report; it is gated by one relaxed atomic load on lock-held paths only.
- Phase 1.5 task 7 (b): `SlabDesc`'s padding byte is now a shadow of the state (`state + 1`), written by `init` and `set_state` unconditionally, checked only under `check_zero`. Reason: it costs one byte store next to an existing one under the class lock and lets the debug mode prove or exclude descriptor corruption, which it did (excluded).
- Phase 1.5 task 7 (b): the campaign driver lives in `bench/probes/calloc-zero-campaign.sh` (committed), not a `.tmp-*` script. Reason: `challoc-bench sync` excludes `.tmp-*`, and the protocol must be rerunnable on the VMs by the next iteration; its knobs go inside `make run CMD=...` because `make run` forwards no environment.
- Phase 1.5 task 7 (c): a zero pick's slots are the lowest `n` bits of the slab, claimed in `take` under the class lock, rather than keeping a `Fresh`/`Purged` slab out of the partial set until its reserver has claimed. Reason: it is the smallest form that keeps one lock hold per refill and the `Active` batch claim outside the lock; the alternative hides a fresh slab from every other refill for the whole unlocked window and needs a second lock hold to publish it. `Empty` picks (dirty, never reported zero) keep the unlocked bit loop, so nothing changes for a warm heap.
- Phase 1.5 task 7 (c): a `Fresh`/`Purged` slab with a bit already set when `take` claims its prefix aborts in every build (`zero_slab_had_bits_set`), not only under `check_zero`. Reason: `live == 0` with a set bit means `live` no longer bounds the set bits, the heap inconsistency the rules say to abort on; the check is one compare per word on a path that does the same RMWs anyway.
- Phase 1.5 task 8: `overflow-checks` stays off in `[profile.release]`. Reason: measured on both reference VMs against 1aa14da (`bench/results/2026-09-18-{aarch64,x86_64}-gce-release-asserts.md`), the flag costs xmalloc 15..18%, larson1 6..8% and larson10 4..5%, an instruction-count mechanism (+9.2% instructions per larson1 op at unchanged misses: a checked add or subtract with a cold branch on every cache push, pop and batch counter), which fails the task's own 5% gate; the wrap-prone subtractions the review named are checked explicitly on their slow paths, and the dev profile (every unit test) keeps the checks on. Revisit only with a fast-path design that does the cache index arithmetic without a checked op per operation.
- Phase 1.5 task 8: the `bin_stats` clamp is counted, not aborted on. Reason: `released` is added to at the end of `release_owned`, after `dec_live` may have moved the slab to `Empty` under the lock, so a reader in that window legitimately sees `claimed - released` above the capacity of the non-`Empty` slabs; the review's "abort when the clamp fires with the lock held" has no such state (both counters are read in `bin_stats`, and `released` is never written under the class lock). The count is in the bins table so a permanently growing value is visible.
- Phase 1.5 task 8: `RunState::from_u8` (huge runs) aborts on bytes no state has, like `State::from_u8`. Reason: the review named only the slab enum, but the huge decode had the same default arm to `Purged`, whose pages `take` reports as zero to `calloc`.
- Phase 1.5 task 8: the `others`-node assertion is on the node-creation branch of `Heap::other_caches`, not at its entry. Reason: `free_other` (every remote free, xmalloc's row) calls it once per free to find the node; the invariant (no node for the home arena, which would split the home caches) can only be broken where a node is created, and a lookup cannot find a node that was never created.

- Phase 1.5 task 9 (a): the flush's double-free check is the plan's one compare at the split point, not a pass over the kept half. The pass (every neighbouring pair of the sorted kept half, `rest_len` compares per flush) was tried first and cost xmalloc-test +4.9% on aarch64 and +7.3% on x86_64 (NO-GO; `bench/results/2026-09-18-*-gce-detection-gaps.md` section 1). A pair the split keeps together is consistent (both copies cached, bit set) and is caught by the release that flushes it; only the straddling pair released one copy while keeping the other.
- Phase 1.5 task 9 (b): the "at or above the arena's committed watermark" check is at the flush, not on the sized fast path. The arena's commit cursor (`Inner::group_next`) is under the class lock and the sized path reads neither the arena nor the owner word, so it is not one load; `release_owned` reads the descriptor at every slab switch anyway and aborts on a `Fresh` slab by name. Publishing the cursor as a per-group word next to the owner array would be a second dense load per sized free, the bitmap-word question of task 4e in another form, and is left to the human. The guard test is on the fast path (arithmetic on the table line; attribution in the results file).
- Phase 1.5 task 9 (c): `double_free_scan` means "compare against the top four cache entries on every push" (plain `free`, sized path, `others` caches, slow-path push), not the "scan whole cache" of the old section 12 row: the review's 5.1 costs it as a few instructions and a whole-cache scan is a `cap`-length loop per free. Design 6.3 step 3 and the row rewritten.
- Phase 1.5 task 10 (a): `Tcaches.lock` is the outermost lock, ahead of the arena creation lock, because `tcache.create`/`destroy` hold it across `Heap::create_detached` (metadata lock, possibly arena materialisation) and `Heap::destroy` (class locks, metadata lock); the statistics locks are leaves (`stats.rs` takes each with nothing else held), placed after `meta` and before the background state lock so the design's "background lock last" stays true.
- Phase 1.5 task 10 (a): the lock inventory is derived from the source text at test time (`lock_inventory` in `src/api/mod.rs` tests), not from a registry in the code: a registry would be one more thing to forget when adding a lock; the text scan cannot be forgotten. Its parse is deliberately simple (field or static declarations whose type mentions `Lock<` or `RawLock`, owner = last `struct` seen, `lock.rs` and `mod tests` skipped), and `assert_locks` panics on an unknown key so the list and the handler cannot drift apart.
- Phase 1.5 task 10 (c): `errno` is saved and restored around the whole `create` (the review's first option) rather than starting the thread only from allocation-side ticks: it also covers the new `pthread_join`, and a decay-only free path would have moved the start onto a tick that may never come on a thread that only frees.
- Phase 1.5 task 10 (d): the background thread is now joinable and a restart joins the exited thread before reusing the stack. `present = false` is written by the thread on its way out, but glibc's exit path still runs on the stack until the kernel reaps it; only `pthread_join` (which waits on the kernel's tid clear) proves the stack idle. The alternative, a thread that parks instead of exiting, would have broken the in-process `fork_child` unit tests (a parked parent thread would share the stack with the next start) and left a `challoc-bg` task behind after `background_thread:false`. A fork child never joins: the copied `pthread_t` names a thread the kernel never clears in the child, and glibc's fork handling already removes the copied descriptor from its user-stack list, so the child reuses the stack directly.
- Phase 1.5 task 10 (c) test: the `libc_api background` mode depends on the cache geometry (`MAX_CAP` 1024, refill `cap / 2`, `PERIODIC_EVERY` 64, a probe per 4096 allocations) to make a free the 64th slow path of a fresh thread; it asserts the thread is absent before the frees and present after them so a geometry change fails the test instead of silently uncovering the path. There is no API that requests the thread without starting it (`mallctl("background_thread", true)` creates it synchronously), so `MALLOC_CONF` is the only way to arm a start from a tick.
- Phase 1.5 task 11: an inert key is reported through the same channel as a bad key (`Issue::Inert`, counted by `load`) rather than by a separate flag, so `abort_conf` rejects it with no second pass and a program running without `abort_conf` sees one stderr line per inert pair at start-up, like jemalloc's warnings for unknown keys. The value is still validated and stored so `opt.<key>` reads back what was set (a bad value stays a bad value). `stats_print` has no field: its `opt.` leaf stays `ENOENT` and the report prints it without a value.
- Phase 1.5 task 11: `huge_retain_max` is clamped to 2^40 (`layout::LG_HUGE_PREFERRED`), the largest run the preferred huge region can hold, so "retain everything" is expressible and the value fits the `c_uint` the `opt.` leaf writes only up to 4 GiB (larger settings read back truncated through `mallctl`, as `narenas`-style `c_uint` leaves do for every `Uint` key; jemalloc's `opt.` size keys are `size_t`, a seam for task 12).
- Phase 1.5 task 11: `PIDFD_SELF`'s kernel version was 6.16 in `sys/mod.rs` and `sys/features.rs`, 6.14 in `purge.rs`, design 8 and `docs/research.md`; unified on the research document's 6.14 without independent confirmation (two web lookups did not settle it; see Notes). The runtime probe decides, so no behaviour depends on the number.

- Phase 1.5 task 12 (2026-09-18): `guard_slab_interval:1` is out of range and clamps to 2 (`Issue::OutOfRange`, so `abort_conf` rejects it and the default run reports it once). Reason: 1 would make every slab a guard slab and leave nothing to allocate (`SlabTable::new` refuses it); the smallest interval with any slabs is 2, and a reported clamp is how every other numeric key treats a value outside its range.
- Phase 1.5 task 12 (2026-09-18): `MALLOCX_TCACHE(t)` without `MALLOCX_ARENA` follows the default path (`alloc_zeroed`, so a class above `tcache_max` comes from arena 0), while `MALLOCX_ARENA(a)` keeps `a` whether or not `a` is the heap's home. Reason: the review's item 24 is about an explicit arena being overridden; an explicit cache with no arena flag named no arena, and keeping it on the default path leaves the behaviour of `tcache.create` users unchanged.
- Phase 1.5 task 12 (2026-09-18): the placement rule is consulted on the slow path only (`ensure_cache`); `free_other` and `alloc_other` carry no predicate and look the `others` node up without creating it. Reason: `quick-perf` priced a per-free predicate at +9 instructions per xmalloc operation on Neoverse-V2 (the `cap_for` ladder) and a tabulated one (`Shared::cap`, 1751d16) still at +1.8..2.3% on that row, while the lookup-only form is 5..8% ahead of dd26516 on xmalloc on both microarchitectures and creates no node for an uncached class either (a miss goes through `ensure_cache`, which refuses when the capacity is zero). The table was removed again in 2a8c6b6.
- Phase 1.5 task 12 (2026-09-18): `take_group`'s cross-check compares the owner word with the descriptors' arena field *before* the group is initialised (both must read zero for never-touched memory) rather than after. Reason: after `init` the two are trivially equal; the check as written catches a wild write into either array between reservation and hand-out, and it is the invariant plan task 15's group handoff will rely on.
- Phase 1.5 task 15 (2026-09-18): the handoff's lock order. The taker holds its class lock throughout `plan` and only ever `try_lock`s a donor's class lock; a busy donor is skipped and the fresh group is the fallback. Reason: no thread then blocks while holding a class lock, so there is no order to argue and the fork handlers' nesting (class locks by arena index, commit locks, set tables) is untouched. The first form locked higher-index donors blocking (consistent with that nesting, `try_lock` downward) and was correct, but on `xmalloc`, where every donor is a producer whose lock also serves its consumers' flushes, the takers joined the convoy (Mac fast tier: +6.1% after the tracking rework, against +9.0% before it); releasing the taker's lock to acquire in order was rejected because another thread of the taker's arena could interleave a refill in the middle of `plan`.
- Phase 1.5 task 15 (2026-09-18): idle-group tracking is the arenas' own `empty` and `purged` sets, nothing more. A group is idle when every committed non-guard slab of it is in its owner's `empty` or `purged` set (the rest are uncommitted `Fresh`), and the taker finds one by locking each other arena's state in turn and scanning its two sets group by group (`ClassState::next_idle_group`, at most `MAX_ADOPT_TRIES` = 8 groups per donor) before it takes a fresh group. Reason: the first form (a per-group `Active` count in the table plus a class-wide idle set under the commit lock, maintained in `take` and `on_live_zero`) failed the Mac fast tier on `xmalloc` (+9.0%, median of 3; RSS 120 -> 61 MiB): for the small classes a group is one slab, so every Active/Empty transition took the shared commit lock and did an RMW on a `busy` line that every arena's interleaved groups share. The landed form adds nothing to the transition paths and moves the whole cost to the fresh-commit path, which pays an `mprotect` anyway.
- Phase 1.5 task 15 (2026-09-18): the transfer moves the donor's commit cursor with the group when the group is the donor's current one (`Inner::group_next..group_end`), so the uncommitted `Fresh` slabs become the taker's current group and the donor's window is closed; `stats.committed` moves for the slabs below the cursor, `empty`/`purged` counts with the set bits, `claimed`/`released` not at all (`live == 0`, so no slot is out).
