Rule: reservations carved back to back whose sizes are powers of two all start on the same boundary; stagger them (descriptor arrays are now staggered; the set bitmaps are not, being slow-path only).
- Phase 1 task 3 follow-up (3): `make test PLATFORM=linux/amd64` and the numbers cannot overlap; run correctness after the timed rounds. The single-thread larson row (`larson 4 8 1000 5000 100 4141 1`) resolves 2% with five interleaved rounds even at host load 5; the 10-thread row needs load under 3 to resolve 4%.
- Phase 1 task 5: rustc's cdylib version script exports only Rust items. A data symbol defined in `global_asm!` (tried for a weak `malloc_conf` and an aliased `je_malloc_message`) links but never reaches `.dynsym`. If a consumer ever needs the library itself to *define* `malloc_conf` (a program that references it without defining it), the options are a build script passing `cargo:rustc-cdylib-link-arg=-Wl,--export-dynamic-symbol=malloc_conf` (linker dependent) or `#[linkage = "weak"]` (unstable). Nothing in Valkey or Ruby needs it.
- Phase 1 task 5: `mallctl` reads under `cargo test` run in parallel with tests that create arenas and allocate; the `api::` tests therefore assert on their own allocations and on identities, and the JSON test counts arena sections in the text it received. The only key whose semantics depend on process-wide quiet is `epoch` (`stats.*` frozen between epochs), and only `api::mallctl::tests::stats_are_frozen_between_epochs_and_arenas_all_sums` calls it in the `api::` process (the `stats.rs` tests run in the other process).
- Phase 1 task 5: `tests/c/jemalloc_api.c` reaches every non-standard symbol through `__attribute__((weak))` declarations, so it links against libc alone and the bare run fails in `require_challoc`; under the preload the weak references bind to `libchalloc.so`. This is a simpler pattern than `dlsym` for future C tests of new exports.
- Phase 1 task 6: `make run CMD=...` cannot carry `$(...)` or `$?`: make expands them (empty) before the shell sees them, so `cc ... $(pkg-config --libs jemalloc)` linked without `-ljemalloc` and looked like a broken export list. Put multi-step container commands in a `.tmp-*.sh` in the repo (gitignored) and run `make run CMD='sh .tmp-x.sh'`.
- Phase 1 task 6: the dev container has Wolfi's real `jemalloc` installed, so a program linked with `-ljemalloc` and run without `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` finds `/usr/lib/libjemalloc.so.2` (jemalloc 5.3, built without the `je_` prefix) and dies at load time with `undefined symbol: je_malloc_usable_size` (Wolfi links with `-z now`); a program that used only the unprefixed API would start under real jemalloc instead, which is why `tests/c/jemalloc_link.c` also checks `require_challoc` and the directory `dladdr` reports for `malloc`. Ruby's `--with-jemalloc` (Phase 1 acceptance) has not been checked against the stage yet: if its configure probes a symbol the library does not define (`malloc_conf` is read, not defined; see the task 5 decision) the check will need the `--export-dynamic-symbol` route noted under task 5.
- Phase 1.5 task 4b (2026-09-17), Ruby rows, not a regression but the one place challoc keeps more than jemalloc: on `gc_churn` (long-lived medium buffers plus bursts of short-lived ones freed by sweep) challoc's peak RSS is +6.6% (aarch64) / +4.2% (x86_64) against jemalloc and +21% against glibc (glibc's Ruby calls `malloc_trim` after every full GC). After 10 forced full GCs challoc returns 2% (Ruby's own heap pages), as do glibc, mimalloc and jemalloc on x86_64; Wolfi's aarch64 jemalloc, built with 64 KiB pages (`Page size: 65536`), returns 48% (359 -> 186 MiB). challoc's `dirty_decay_ms` (10 s) deadline never fires in a 1.6 s process and the `--with-jemalloc` Ruby never calls `malloc_trim`, so a long-lived Ruby (Rails, Sidekiq) is where the post-burst residency would show; a soak-length Ruby row or a `background_thread` default for Ruby-shaped consumers is the follow-up if that matters. Also visible: 1..2 ms of init on a 40 ms `ruby -e 1` (+5.1% / +2.4%) and about 1 MiB of fixed footprint above glibc on tiny processes.
- Phase 1.5 task 5 (2026-09-18): gcloud's auth token expired during the iteration ("Reauthentication failed. cannot prompt during non-interactive execution"); `challoc-bench sync/ssh/fetch` stopped working and the rest of the iteration went through direct `ssh -i ~/.ssh/google_compute_engine dlorenc_chainguard_dev@<ip>` to the two RUNNING VMs (IPs from the last `status`), which the README documents. Run `gcloud auth login` before the next iteration; the VMs were never touched by the driver from here (still running, per the warm-session rule). The one thing left undone by it: `challoc-bench fetch` overwrote nothing; both results files were copied with `scp`.
- Phase 1.5 task 5 (2026-09-18): the review's "one slot block per heap" is a measured loss on both microarchitectures for a reason the counters bound but do not name (see the design decision): stack pointers living in a 128 KiB-strided metadata object cost xmalloc-test's freeing threads 3..4x the L1D fills. If someone wants the last 10% of aarch64 dTLB refills that layout offered, the experiment to run is the block with `perf mem`-style data-address sampling on bare metal (the GCE PMU level has no `mem-loads`), or a block built from 8 KiB pieces.
- Phase 1.5 task 4e (2026-09-18), the soak's write phase is at parity and the delete phase is 3.6..3.8% behind; the residue is attributed and is again a design decision (`bench/results/2026-09-18-aarch64-gce-sized-free.md` 4.3). The sized free no longer touches the slab descriptor (`je_sdallocx` 79 cycles per SET against jemalloc's 66), but Valkey's `zfree(ptr)` calls `zmalloc_size(ptr)` (= `malloc_usable_size`) right before `je_sdallocx(ptr, size, 0)` (`src/zmalloc.c` line 473 of 9.1.2), and `malloc_usable_size` on a slab pointer runs the exact bit test on the same cold descriptor line, so the miss moved there: the usable-size query is 492 cycles per SET against jemalloc's 327 (task 4d: 288 against 353, when `free` had just pulled the line into L1). Frees through `zfree_with_size` (sds strings, objects with a known size) are miss-free now, which is the write-phase gain. Two ways forward, yours to pick: (a) `malloc_usable_size` returns the class size for a slab-slot pointer without the bit test (the variant task 4d built, `quick`-gated and reverted for lack of gain; it would gain now), at the price that `malloc_usable_size` of a freed slot returns a size instead of aborting, the same trade you took for `sdallocx`; (b) leave the query exact and take task 4f (the dense per-class bitmap makes that one bit an L2-resident load instead of a descriptor-line miss, for `usable_size` and plain `free` alike), which keeps every check and is next in plan order anyway. I have not done (a). Also for the record: the README's old footnote claimed a "most recent entries" double-free check at the call under `double_free_scan`; the config key exists, the check does not (no code in `heap.rs` reads it), and the footnote now says so.
- Phase 1.5 task 4f (2026-09-18), what the dense bitmap did and did not do (`bench/results/2026-09-18-aarch64-gce-dense-bitmap.md`). The write phase of the soak is now ahead of bundled jemalloc on both generators (throughput and p99, 1.8..4.9%) and the tie-breaker agrees (3313 against 3407 cycles per command under `perf record`); the usable-size query on `zfree`'s path is at jemalloc's cost (327 cycles per SET, from 492) and plain `free` (`valkey1-free`, Ruby) is at or ahead of jemalloc. The delete phase did not move: 3.2..4.6% behind on throughput and p99, +2.9% cycles per command (from +4.2%), and the usable-size query there costs 206 cycles per command against jemalloc's 96 (task 4e's figure). The annotate says the delete-phase query is no longer one long miss but a chain of short ones (the bitmap word 23%, the `CLASSES`/class-table loads 8..11% each), i.e. the `usable_size` slow-path form of the check (`Regions::classify`, then the table, then the word) has more dependent loads than the `free` fast path, which reads its geometry from the dense arrays. That is the next fast-path item on the Valkey delete phase and needs its own attribution first; I have not started it. Three things you may want to decide: (a) the plain `free` fast path still reads the owner word once a second arena exists (one `u16` per group, dense, but a load and a page per class); pushing plain frees onto the home cache and routing at the flush, as the sized path already does since task 4e, would remove it and the `others` cache walk on cross-arena frees, at the cost of the freeing thread's cache holding another arena's slots (jemalloc's tcache behaviour); (b) on small heaps the per-class metadata is now two pages (bitmap and descriptors) instead of one, which shows as +0.17..1.1 L1D TLB refills per larson operation on aarch64 with no cycle change; a chunked layout (a group's descriptors next to its bits) would undo that for small heaps and spread a large heap's bits over one page per few hundred slabs again, so I kept the dense layout for Valkey and Ruby; (c) `LG_GROUP_SPAN` = 2 MiB caps the address space one arena reserves per class per group; with the default 64 arenas that is at most 128 MiB of any class region, but a program that creates thousands of arenas with `arenas.create` and allocates one object of every class in each reserves a group per arena per class (address space only), which at the 4 GiB minimum region is the case to watch.
- Phase 1.5 acceptance run 1 (2026-09-18, iteration 26): the phase fails on RSS and I did not relax it; the attribution and the candidate list are in `bench/results/2026-09-18-phase-1.5-acceptance.md` and the in-progress item. Three things for you. (a) The RSS rows are the price of task 4c's per-thread arenas (`narenas:1` restores every row; `narenas:4` restores two of three), so the remediation is a trade against the larson16 gain 4c bought (+11.9% -> +3.2% on aarch64); the A/B named in the in-progress item prices it, and you may prefer to decide the default arena count yourself once it exists. (b) Criterion 4's literal reading ("no lock or atomic RMW in the top 10 symbols") is met on x86_64 and, on aarch64, three outlined LSE helpers (`__aarch64_ldadd4_acq_rel` etc.) are in `perf report`'s top 10 by rank at 0.16 / 0.08 / 0.04%: Rust outlines aarch64 atomics into helper symbols while x86_64's `lock` instructions are inline, so the rank reading measures the outlining, not the atomics (0.3% of challoc's cycles, all slab claim/release, none in `je_malloc`/`je_free`). I recorded PASS on substance with the numbers; if you want the rank reading, say so and it becomes a criterion decision, not allocator work. (c) gcloud's token refresh failed for the whole iteration (DNS from gcloud's Python), so everything after `sync` ran over direct ssh; the recipe is in `bench/gce/README.md`. Unrelated but found: `quick-perf`'s larson per-op counters were off by a constant since 5c (fixed, 28ca9e3); the dense-bitmap and dense-headers files' larson cycles/op columns from `quick-perf` are scaled by the same constant (ratios within a row are unaffected).
- Phase 1.5 acceptance run 2 (2026-09-18, iteration 27): the RSS remediation landed (d10fb6d) and two of the three failing rows are inside the band on both VMs; mstress is +8.9% on aarch64 and +18.5% on x86_64. What remains is not the arena count or the caches: at 17 arenas each arena's small-class Empty slabs wait out the full 10 s `dirty_decay_ms` before a purge (design section 8: "an Empty slab older than this is purged", a flat window), where jemalloc with the same setting purges dirty pages along its smoothstep curve, about half of a window's dirty pages by mid-window; the per-arena partial slabs and foreign stacks are the smaller half. A design decision for you: (a) implement jemalloc's progressive decay (purge the oldest Empty slabs so the arena's dirty bytes follow the curve; changes the meaning of `dirty_decay_ms` from "age at purge" to "window of the curve", jemalloc's meaning), (b) read the mstress row on x86_64 as the flat-window design's price and keep it, or (c) something narrower such as a per-arena dirty budget. I have not started any of them. Also: classes above `tcache_max` now come from arena 0 on the default path (design 13.3), so `stats.arenas.<i>.large` of a thread's arena no longer shows its >32 KiB allocations; `MALLOCX_ARENA` allocations are unchanged.
- Phase 1.5 acceptance run 3 (2026-09-18, iteration 28), the mstress x86_64 RSS row (+19.1%) is structural, not decay: see `bench/results/2026-09-18-phase-1.5-acceptance-3.md` section 3.1. mstress is a 0.63 s process; at `narenas:1` challoc's RSS equals jemalloc's (283 vs 286 MiB), at the default 17 arenas it is +60 MiB of per-(arena, class) `Empty` and partial slabs plus per-arena metadata, because an `Empty` slab in challoc can be reused only by its own class (per-class regions, design section 3) and its own arena (13.3), while jemalloc's per-arena dirty extent pool is reused by every bin and large size. The progressive decay (your option (a)) is correct and landed, but it is not what this row needed. Three ways forward, yours to pick: (d) an `Empty`-slab handoff between arenas on the commit path: when an arena's class would commit a fresh slab (`ClassState::commit_next`), it first takes an `Empty` (then `Purged`) slab of the same class from another arena (lock order by arena index, owner rewritten in the descriptor, sets updated on both sides; the hot paths keep their partitioning, only the fresh-commit path looks sideways). It attacks the +18 and +21 MiB terms, which would bring the row to about +8..10%, and it changes 13.3's "an arena owns its slabs" to "until it lends an Empty one". (b) a per-arena dirty budget (jemalloc 4's `lg_dirty_mult`; jemalloc 5 dropped it), purging `Empty` slabs beyond a fraction of the arena's active bytes at once, with the refault cost `dirty_decay_ms:0` shows (mstress 0.63 -> 1.12 s). (c) read the x86_64 mstress RSS row as the price of per-class regions at 16 arenas and record it as the one Phase 0 row outside the band (aarch64 is inside at +9.8%). I recommend (d); I have not started any of them. Everything else in the acceptance passes on both VMs.
- Phase 1.5 task 12 (2026-09-18): two readings for you. (1) The aarch64 scorecard line for 2a8c6b6 shows valkey1 at +15.4% behind jemalloc where the previous three lines had +8.8..9.5%; the old-vs-new quick rounds put 2a8c6b6 at parity with dd26516 on that row (+0.6, +1.0, -0.8, -1.8, +0.4, -0.0%) and jemalloc's own valkey1 figure on that VM ranged 7.00..8.34 M ops/s across the day's quick runs while challoc's sat at 6.87..7.34, so the trend movement is jemalloc's (or the VM's) variance today, not the seams; a rerun of the scorecard on a quiet day would settle it. (2) The x86_64 scorecard has two lines for this task, 1751d16 (the `Shared::cap` table) and 2a8c6b6 (final); both are real commits and the pair prices the table form against the final one on that architecture (xmalloc +0.9% -> -2.6% vs jemalloc).
- Phase 1.5 task 15 (2026-09-18): your accepted option (d), the whole-group handoff, is landed (01e15ad) and measured on both VMs, and it does not do what the review predicted: mstress RSS is unchanged on x86_64 (+18.6% over jemalloc with it on, +19.5% off, +19.1% at acceptance run 3) and 2.4% worse on aarch64 with it on (+12.0% against +9.4% off). I flipped its default to `false` (2f218f9) and accepted the row as the review's (c) under the task's own fallback text; `group_handoff:true` stays available and takes a fifth off xmalloc-test's RSS. If you would rather keep it on (Valkey's io-threads are a producer-consumer pattern the soak does not exercise), it is one line in `src/config.rs` plus design 12 and 13.3; the aarch64 mstress row would then be outside the band at the task 16 rerun. What would move the mstress row is outside the plan: a cross-class dirty pool per arena (jemalloc's model, against design 3's per-class regions) or a per-slab handoff (against the 4f invariant). Next plan item: task 16, the acceptance rerun; not started.
- Phase 2.5 task 1 (2026-09-19): the one new finding of the four-way matrix is the compacting flush's slot sort (M2 in both four-way files): `core::slice::sort::unstable::quicksort::<*mut u8>` plus `median3_rec`/`ipnsort` take 14.6% / 13.9% of the whole `python dict_list-malloc` process (aarch64 / x86_64; a third of challoc's own time there), 9.2% / 8.5% of glibc-simple and 4.0% / 2.5% of mstress. It is the reason challoc trails mimalloc by 18..30% and tcmalloc by 17..22% on the `PYTHONMALLOC=malloc` rows and by 31..40% on glibc-simple while the pymalloc rows and the larger-object rows are at parity. A fast-path task that attacks it (a cheaper order for the flush, or sorting only when the batch is spread over more than one slab) must follow CLAUDE.md's rule: quick-perf on glibc-simple and the python malloc rows first, the Phase 1 churn probe still compacting. Second reading: mstress on x86_64 has equal cycles under challoc and mimalloc (17.02 vs 16.93 G) and 13% more wall time, i.e. idle time under `mmap_lock` (kernel 7.9% vs 4.9%), which the Phase 2.5 batch-purge and prefault items would address. Third: tcmalloc-minimal is the strongest of the four stock allocators on the Valkey model on aarch64 (8.5 vs challoc's 7.1 M ops/s on valkey1, with fewer instructions per SET and more misses) and on erb_render; it is also the worst on RSS everywhere (7..18 MiB floors, keeps everything in the container tests) and 250x slower than challoc on xmallocN (2 M frees/s: cross-thread frees through its central list).
- Phase 2.5 task 1 (2026-09-19), measurement notes: the whole matrix took 70 minutes of VM time per architecture in one detached driver (ab 30 min, ruby 6, python 13, container tests 10, scorecard 9) plus 10 minutes of attribution; pyperformance installs in the container (recorded, not used, design decision above); the smaps sidecar at 3 ms catches peaks within a few MiB of `ru_maxrss` for steady processes but under-reads brief peaks (json_bench) and disagreed with `rt` on glibc's gc_churn by 200 MiB (its own mmap_lock traffic changes the GC timing), so RSS attributions cite challoc's own breakdown and the table's `rt` figures, never the sidecar's total for another allocator.

- Phase 2.5 task 2 (2026-09-19), the feature report's first data points, both negative for the phase's tagged-pointer plan on the reference VMs (`bench/results/2026-09-19-feature-report.md`): the C4A Axion does not expose MTE (`AT_HWCAP2 = 0x3f3ff`, bits 18 and 22 clear, no `mte` in `Features`; it does expose PAC, LSE2, BTI and SVE), and the C4 Xeon 8581C does not expose LAM (leaf 7.1 `EAX = 0x1c30`, bit 26 clear; Emerald Rapids predates LAM, so C3 Sapphire Rapids and C3D Genoa will not have it either; it does run five-level paging, `la57_enabled=yes`, and has CLDEMOTE; WAITPKG is hidden by the hypervisor). Task 6 on hardware therefore needs a box outside the current pair: Graviton4 or AmpereOne for MTE, Granite Rapids for LAM. Task 5 can still write the design and run the `arch_prctl` probe (it will fail with EINVAL here, which is itself a matrix cell). Also for the record: the longer report line moved `malloc_stats_print`'s 1 KiB callback-chunk boundary and exposed `tests/c/mseal.c` parsing a line from a single chunk; fixed to accumulate the report, as the other C tests do. Anything that grows the report can shift that boundary again, so a new C test that reads the report should copy the accumulate pattern of `features.c`.
- Phase 2.5 task 3 (2026-09-19): the floor holds on 5.10, 6.1, 6.8 and 7.0 kernels on both architectures (464 tests, fuzz, cgroup tests each) and the allocator runs on 64 KiB pages (464 tests, fuzz, churn), but the oscillation test is OOM-killed there by 7..11 MiB of page-rounding tail against an 8..16 MiB margin; the task stays open for that one criterion. Two things you may want to weigh in on: whether the 64 KiB fix is a policy change (headroom in pages) or an accepted limitation recorded in the design; and that `process_madvise` batching is a 6.13+ feature, not 5.10 as the design claimed, which makes the batched purge absent on Debian 12 and Ubuntu 24.04 GA hosts (they run the `madvise` loop; the numbers in `bench/results/` come from 7.0 kernels where it is on).

- Phase 2.5 task 4b (2026-09-19), the valkey1 row on non-reference CPUs in one paragraph: it is not the CPU, the cache or the layout, it is the length of challoc's sized path. Each SET of the Valkey model does one random dict-entry load that misses to DRAM and the out-of-order core hides that latency by starting the next SET's load early, which it can only do while both SETs fit its instruction window; jemalloc's SET is 266 instructions and fits, challoc's is 391 (+125 in two mallocs and two sdallocx, misses identical at every level on a bare-metal 8481C) and sits at the cliff, so on hosts whose L3 does not hold the table (SPR, CLX, an 8-vCPU slice of Genoa/Milan) each SET pays a serialized DRAM latency and the row reads +21..43%, while on the reference C4 (5 MiB L3 per core) the table mostly hits L3 and the row reads +4%. The `-x` probe flag reproduces it with jemalloc itself (+160 dummy instructions: 10.2 -> 6.5 M SET/s on the C3 VM, below challoc's 7.3). One instruction is a third of the excess: `is_guard_slab`'s `% guard_interval` on the sdallocx path is a `divl` by a variable, twice per SET; `guards:false` puts the C3 VM at 9.7 against jemalloc's 10.1. Plan task 4c (a power-of-two interval with a mask, then the instruction budget) is the fix; it needs `quick` on a C3 lab VM as well as the reference VMs, since the reference cannot see it. The T2A larson rows are a different mechanism (challoc's plain free is a six-dependent-load chain that the N1's small window does not hide; the LSE helpers are 0.25%), left as a note in the Altra file rather than a task. A PMU on these CPUs means bare metal on GCE (`c3-standard-192-metal`, about $10 per hour; 61 minutes were used and the instance is deleted).
- Phase 2.5 task 4f, for the human (2026-09-19): the 64 KiB post-delete RSS criterion (within 10% of bundled jemalloc) is not met, +20.5% / +20.0%, and the attribution (`bench/results/2026-09-19-aarch64-64k-defrag-attribution.md`) finds no challoc-side answer to correct: Valkey's defragmenter stops on `active-defrag-ignore-bytes` (100 MB, absolute) between sub-second cycles, jemalloc's heap re-crosses it once more than challoc's because its 192..448 KiB slabs waste 22 MiB more after the second cycle, and that third cycle (74k moves) is the whole gap; with the constant at 80 MB challoc lands at +1.9% (diagnostic only). The options are yours: (1) accept the 64 KiB criterion as failed by Valkey's constant and keep stock defaults (a stock-configured challoc Valkey at 64 KiB pages ends the acceptance's delete phase 30 MiB higher than jemalloc, the write phase and peak RSS at parity); (2) a `USE_CHALLOC` default for `active-defrag-ignore-bytes` in the contrib patch, which the 2026-09-17 rule forbids for this purpose (it is an absolute threshold, not slab geometry, and its only effect here is to pass the criterion) unless you lift it; (3) a challoc-side follow-up on the second-order term, the compacting cache not draining sparse slabs under a balanced churn (jemalloc -12 MiB vs challoc -4 over 210 s), a `heap.rs` pop/flush change with the usual per-operation attribution, worth at most a third of the gap; (4) re-scope the 64 KiB criterion to `active` at the end of the last defrag cycle, where the allocators are equal. Nothing has been changed pending your answer.
- Phase 2.5 task 4g, for the human (2026-09-19): 4f's option (3), a challoc-side fix of the compacting cache for the second-order term, is closed by measurement (`bench/results/2026-09-19-aarch64-64k-compaction.md`): the cache drains everything the churn frees at 64..128-region slabs (probe 469 -> 214..216, the Phase 1 figure), and the post-cycle residue is 2.1..2.3k values per class that memtier's fixed-seed stream does not select again for 60..150 s, sitting at 4..5 per 64 KiB slab in every slab of the class; only a defragmenter cycle moves them, and that cycle is the one Valkey's 100 MB constant grants jemalloc's 192..448 KiB slabs and not challoc's 64 KiB ones (4f). The 64 KiB post-delete RSS row stays FAIL by that mechanism (+17.6% at 300 s, +20.5% at 600 s, +20.0% at 1200 s; +1.9% with the constant at 80 MB as a diagnostic). Options (1), (2) and (4) of the 4f note remain yours; nothing has been changed.

- Phase 2.5 task 15 (lane C): the syscall numbers used are `rseq_slice_yield` 471 on both architectures (v7.0 `syscall_64.tbl` and `asm-generic/unistd.h`; confirmed by the call returning 0 on the custom x86_64 kernel with seccomp unconfined) and `sched_yield` 24 (x86_64) / 124 (aarch64), asserted against the `libc` crate in `slice::tests::layout_constants`. The lane VMs are deleted (`gcloud compute instances list --filter='name~challoc-lane-c'` empty); the custom kernel went with the C4.
- Phase 2.5 task 8 (lane C): lane A's implementation is not in this tree; the coordinator checks it against the model at merge by reading the diff for these properties, all of which the model assumes of `Section::Rseq` and shows the loss of otherwise: (1) `mm_cid` is read inside the rseq critical section (after `rseq_cs` is armed) and the cache index (and the per-CPU arena of a refill) is derived only from that in-section read, never from a value read earlier; (2) the section ends with a single commit store, the `len` write (a push's `slots[len] = p` before it is dead until `len` moves; a pop's entry load is speculative until `len` is stored); (3) the abort handler restarts at the section's start and re-reads `mm_cid`, never resumes with the old index or the old `len`; (4) no kernel-owned field of the rseq area (`cpu_id_start`, `cpu_id`, `node_id`, `mm_cid`, `flags`) is written; `rseq_cs` is the only field the code stores to, and only with a descriptor or zero; (5) the refill and the flush run outside the section: the batch is claimed under the class lock of the arena picked by the in-section read, then pushed back through a second section on whatever cid the thread is on then, and the part of a batch a fuller cache has no room for is released, not dropped; (6) a flush takes entries out of the cache in one committed `len` store and releases them slab by slab through the owner word's arena (`release_routed`), since a cache holds slots of another arena after a migration; (7) the zero prefix follows design 6.3 through both sections (a zero batch extends it, a push below it clips it, a flush drops it) so `calloc` through a per-CPU cache never reports a dirty slot zero; (8) the fallback (thread caches when `__rseq_size < 28` or the key is `off`) is the thread-mode protocol the model already covers. A change to the protocol (a second store in the section, a batch pushed back without a section, an index read before `rseq_cs` is armed) is a change to a step in `src/model.rs`.
- Phase 2.5 task 8 (lane C): the `MAX_STATES` budget (5 M) is about 120 s on the host, so the practical ceiling for a scenario under the 30 s rule is about 1 M states (40 k states/s: a `Node` clone and a `HashSet` insert per state). Three per-CPU threads with two pushes each is 2 M; if lane A's code needs a bigger scenario, the next reduction is a symmetry between the two cids/arenas, not a finer abstraction.

Cost on the product's host: valkey1 5% behind jemalloc on Axion (parity at `madvise`) and 11% on the 8581C (3.8% ahead at `madvise`). challoc's own `thp:always` key recovers it (parity / 5% ahead) for +9.5% RSS on that heap and +40% on larson's retained memory. Task 9 decided the default (`never`) on `madvise` hosts where it changed nothing; the decision wants revisiting with `bench/results/2026-09-19-chainguard-os-cells.md` section 6 (lane B: a key that follows the report's `thp=always`, or per class size).

- Phase 2.5 task 11 (lane C): the model test ("a fresh slab committed
  and populated by the background thread is taken by the next claim as
  any committed empty slab") was not added, on purpose. `src/model.rs`
  quotes the real `class.rs` lines for every step, and the step it would
  need does not exist yet: the prefault pass has to decide what a
  committed-but-unclaimed slab looks like to `next_slab` (in the `empty`
  set with its state left `Fresh`, so the claim still knows it is zero
  for `calloc`, which `MADV_POPULATE_WRITE` preserves; or state `Empty`
  and the zero knowledge lost; or a new state), and whether the cursor
  slab check in `take_group`/`commit next` still holds with a committed
  slab below the cursor that no claim has touched. That is the commit
  path's decision (lane B). Once it lands, the model op is small: under
  the class lock, `s = group_next; commit_slab(s); group_next = s + 1;`
  then the set insert, and the invariant is "no committed slab is in no
  set and unclaimed" (`model::check` line 1761 already has the leak
  check to extend). Lane C can add it in one iteration after the merge.

- Phase 2.5 task 11 (lane A, 2026-09-20), `prefault:true` alone does
  nothing in Valkey: the gate `Config::prefault_active()` is evaluated
  once at init from the configuration, so a server that turns the
  background thread on afterwards through `mallctl("background_thread")`
  (Valkey does at startup) leaves the pass off unless `MALLOC_CONF` also
  says `background_thread:true`. Every soak here used
  `prefault:true,background_thread:true`. If `prefault` becomes a
  default this is moot; if it stays an opt-in, the `background_thread`
  mallctl setter should re-evaluate the gate (`src/api/mallctl.rs` and
  `purge::prefault::configure`, lane B's files).

- The Cascade Lake's xmalloc row moved 3.5% (disjoint lists) and then 2.7% (overlapping) between `old` and the new lock with the same `pause` in both; the C4 and C4A (same code, PMU available) show nothing. The mechanism is one atomic load and a `match` on the slow path; the likeliest cause is code placement on the Cascade Lake (memory note on page aliasing). It is recorded as open in the results file; a sampler split on a C2 lab would settle it (no PMU there).
- The geometry numbers say the 1024 ladder is right on 1 MiB L2 parts for throughput, and that 512 buys 12..15% of larson's RSS; if RSS on small parts ever matters more than 1..3% of larson, the knob exists and the model in 6.2 says where it stops paying (256 loses 6% on larson1).
- Raw `ab` `.md` files on the VMs were deleted by the later `sync --delete` passes (they lived under `bench/results/` on the VM); the `~/logs/ab-*.log` copies, which carry the same tables from `## Sorted scores` on, are what is committed as `2026-09-19-lane-c-ab-*.log`. Worth a driver change: `sync` could exclude `bench/results/2026-*` on the receiver, or `ab` could write under `~/logs`.
- `spin_hint:umwait` has never run: no GCE guest exposes WAITPKG. The first bare-metal Sapphire or Emerald Rapids (or any host with `waitpkg` in `/proc/cpuinfo`) should run larson16 and xmalloc `auto` against `spin_hint:baseline`; the `UMWAIT_TICKS` deadline (1024) is a guess to be measured then.
- The model found nothing new in the current protocol; its value is the two replayed regressions and that the next protocol change (per-CPU caches, task 8; the two-advice purge, task 10) has a place to be checked before the VMs. Scenarios are a few lines each (`calloc_race`, `churn`, `handoff`, `three_way`).
- State space grows fast with a third thread and long programs: keep programs to three or four operations with three threads, or two threads with six; the `MAX_STATES` assert names the case.
- Not modelled: page groups (`spp > 1`), guard slabs, `slot_randomize`, the thread cache and `Heap::flush` above `claim`/`release`, the decay curve. The page-group purge (`page_group_is_idle`) is the one of these on a lock-held path and could join the model with `NSLABS = 4` and `spp = 2`.
- No reference VM and no GCE CPU can run MTE or LAM (design 18.1). What is not gated: the software tag over TBI on aarch64, implementable and priceable on the C4A today; it covers the stale-tag, forged and cross-class negative tests, the fuzzer and suites under the key, and the arm64 cost rows. The x86_64 half is one mask and one `arch_prctl` away when a LAM host exists.
- Cheapest MTE/LAM correctness: QEMU TCG (`-M virt,mte=on -cpu max` for aarch64; `-cpu max` with a 6.4+ guest for LAM, QEMU 8.1+) on the Mac container or a GCE VM; no numbers from it.
- MTE numbers need an AmpereOne bare-metal host (Ampere's documentation confirms MTE to Linux user space there; Oracle's A2 shapes unchecked in a guest). Graviton4 (`c8g`) and Cobalt 100 (`Dpsv6`) are each a `grep mte /proc/cpuinfo` on the smallest instance away; expect no.
- LAM numbers need a Granite Rapids / Sierra Forest host with kernel 6.4+ or a guest whose hypervisor passes `lam` (KVM 6.8+); AWS's 8th-generation Intel shapes are the candidates to check. UAI: nothing until Linux has an enable path.
- Section references to fix outside the lane: `src/sys/mod.rs:1` and `docs/plan.md:39` say "design.md section 18" for the source layout; it is 19 now.
- (c) decision: `background_thread` is not the lever for Ruby-shaped
  consumers (zero effect on both architectures); the automatic arena count
  is (16 idle-ish threads = 16 private slab sets). Measured option: ship
  `narenas:1` (or a small fixed count) for the Ruby package through
  `malloc_conf`/`MALLOC_CONF`, which the compat library honours today;
  src/ options (arena policy, idle-heap slab release) are listed in the
  aarch64 reading. The `narenas:1` run exists on aarch64 only.
- (d) `io-threads 4` FAILS two Phase 1 criteria on the same library that
  passes them at one io-thread (throughput/p99 up to -12.3% / +24.5%, peak
  RSS +12.3%). Attribution: allocator code equal, +10 L1D / +7 dTLB misses
  per command in Valkey and libc on memory placed across 4 per-thread
  arenas. Suggested next runs: the same soak with `narenas:1` under challoc,
  `io-threads 2` and `8`; Phase 2.5 task 12's CLDEMOTE has this soak as its
  workload. The `others` node count is not in the report (a print.rs line
  would help future runs).
- (e) Wolfi's gcc is --as-needed by default; a package must link executables
  with -Wl,--no-as-needed (or reference the allocator) and run
  scripts/check-needed.sh on every executable and the allocating library.
  Ruby already does the former (ruby/ruby#4627). Design 13.6 has the text.

  were copied into bench/results/ by hand; `commit:` in the headers comes
  from COMMIT=a3ddd6d (git says "unknown" in a synced worktree).
- Makefile help for `ruby-soak` and the .PHONY line landed with the (e)

- Lane VMs `challoc-lane-d-{arm64,amd64}` deleted (arm64 last, 19:2x UTC).
- Files outside lane B's list touched, one line each, for the wiring: `src/api/mod.rs` (init calls `purge::configure_advice(features.process_madvise_free, config.lazy_band)`), `tests/c/features.c` (the new feature-line key in `kernel_keys`), `Makefile` (`test-cgroup-lazy` target, in `test` and `test-cgroup`) and `scripts/cgroup-tests.sh` (`lazy` kind, `challoc-eager` allocator). `src/class.rs`, `src/huge.rs`, `src/heap.rs`, `src/arena.rs` untouched.
- Known behaviour: lazily returned pages stay charged, so the `memory.current` sample counts them and the band reaches 70% sooner than live memory would, flipping to eager and shortening the window while memory is plentiful (a conservative error; design 11 records it). A finer sample would subtract lazy-free-not-yet-reclaimed bytes, which needs `memory.stat` (`pglazyfree - pglazyfreed`) at the sampler; not done.
- The `process_madvise` lazy fallback (loop for lazy batches only) ran in the host wiring test; no kernel in reach refuses `MADV_FREE` through `process_madvise` while taking `MADV_DONTNEED`, so it has not run for real. The plan task 3 matrix would show such a cell.
- The VM copies needed the main repo's `.git` mirrored to the same absolute path (`/Users/.../challoc/.git`) for `ab`/`quick` (`git archive`) to work in a worktree checkout; `.tmp-gitsync.sh` did it (not committed). Both `challoc-lane-b-*` VMs deleted at the end.
- The task as written ("the background thread commits and
  `MADV_POPULATE_WRITE`s the next fresh slab of each class that refilled
  since the last pass, bounded by one slab per class and by the cgroup
  headroom") needs three things lane B cannot touch: which slab a class
  takes next (`ClassState`'s lowest `Fresh` slab, `src/class.rs`), the
  commit of that slab under the class lock (`class.rs`), and a per-class
  "refilled since the last pass" mark set on the refill path
  (`class.rs`/`heap.rs`). The purge/background-thread side (a pass in
  `purge::background::main` after `decay`, the `MADV_POPULATE_WRITE` probe
  in `sys/features.rs` with a touch fallback, the headroom bound from
  `policy`) is lane B's and is small; the class side decides whether the
  prefault lands on the slab the next refill really takes.
- Suggested shape when a class-side owner exists: `ClassState::prefault_hint()
  -> Option<(addr, len)>` under the class lock (commit the lowest `Fresh`
  slab, mark it `Fresh`+prefaulted so `take` still reports it zero), a
  `refilled: AtomicBool` per class set by `plan`, cleared by the pass; the
  pass issues `MADV_POPULATE_WRITE` (probed at init, `EINVAL` on < 5.14 →
  fallback: write one byte per page) bounded by one slab per class and by
  `policy` (skip in the scaled and immediate bands). Measure: the Valkey
  soak's write-phase p99 and the perf sidecar's page-fault count on the
  main thread; scorecard within noise.
- Files outside lane B's list touched, for the wiring: `src/lib.rs` (`pub mod thp;`), `src/api/mod.rs` (init: `probe_collapse` when `thp` is not `never`, `thp::configure`, `thp::force_never` after `Shared::init`), `src/api/print.rs` (the `thp:` line, the JSON object, the test needle for `opt.thp`), `src/api/mallctl.rs` (`stats.thp.*`, `auto` in the `opt.thp` string table), `src/api/ctests.rs` (the inert-key test now uses `stats_print`; drivers for the new C tests), `tests/c/features.c` (the three keys). `src/heap.rs`, `src/arena.rs`, `src/slab.rs` untouched; CLAUDE.md lists `src/config.rs` under lane C while the launcher put it in lane B's list: the edit there is the `thp` key alone (`Auto`, off the inert list) and its tests.

- x86_64: the kernel offers the 64 KiB folio there too (the knobs are architecture independent), `auto` works and is a no-op for the counters (no TLB entry for 64 KiB), and the `fork()` call is 29% slower on a 500 MiB heap (2.3 ms) with the burst unchanged; documented as not recommended on x86_64 rather than refused in code, since the knob is the host's to set.
- The `ab` result files on the VMs were deleted by the chain's following `challoc-bench quick` (`sync --delete`) before they were fetched; the three `-ab-*.md` files carry the run's printed tables (the same bytes ab.sh writes) and say so in their header. A RESULT path outside `bench/results/`, or a fetch before the next sync, avoids this next time.
- The fork test's jemalloc reference row does not exist: `tests/c/fork_cost.c` runs `require_challoc` like every program in `tests/c/`.
- Open: the larson dTLB gap to jemalloc is mostly not page geometry (2.68 refills per operation at 2 MiB pages against jemalloc's 1.75 at 4 KiB): the pages an operation touches (descriptor, bitmap, set table, cache, slab) are the next place to look, lane A's territory.
- Phase 3 task 1: the Wolfi PR must carry `contrib/wolfi/challoc.yaml` as `challoc.yaml` in wolfi-dev/os, with a release tag `v0.1.0` (or whatever the first tag is) on a public `chainguard-dev/challoc` and its commit in `expected-commit`; the `local-source` option can stay (harmless) or go. `update:` is `enabled: false` until there are tags. The Wolfi repository and key are passed on melange's command line there, as its Makefile does.
- Phase 3 task 1: open for task 3 (Ruby, KeyDB, RethinkDB): a consumer that includes `<jemalloc/jemalloc.h>` builds against `jemalloc-dev`, which conflicts with `challoc-jemalloc-compat` on `libjemalloc.so` and `jemalloc.pc` (measured: `apk add jemalloc-dev` is refused while the compat package is installed). Options: the compat package ships a `/usr/include/jemalloc/jemalloc.h` declaring the unprefixed API (`contrib/valkey/include/challoc/jemalloc.h` declares the `je_` one, so a second header or a generated one is needed, lane A/D territory), or the consumer keeps `jemalloc-dev` in its build environment and links against the compat library from a repository of its own. Decide before task 3; task 1 ships no header, per the Phase 1 task 6 decision.
- Phase 3 task 1: melange's `ldd-check` in the `challoc-dev` test is pointed at the `challoc` package on purpose (the -dev package has no ELF of its own); the link name is checked by `readlink`. If Wolfi CI's linters want `test/tw/ldd-check` on the package under test, change `packages: challoc` to `packages: none` with `files: /usr/lib/libchalloc.so`.
- Phase 3 task 2, for the Wolfi PR: (1) `valkey-9.1.yaml`'s challoc `git-checkout` carries `expected-commit: 5a1e715...` and `tag: v0.1.0`; set the release tag and its commit, drop the `local-challoc` option (or keep it, it is inert without `--build-option`); the same for `challoc.yaml` (task 1). (2) `epoch: 1` on 9.1.2 is the allocator switch; if 9.1.3 lands first it is `epoch: 0` on the new version. (3) `melange test` locally needs `--test-package-append=wolfi-base` (Wolfi's Makefile has it; the `-cli`/`-benchmark` environments are otherwise empty). (4) The `copyright` entry with `paths: [/usr/lib/valkey/libchalloc.so.0]` (Apache-2.0) is what melange's SBOM gets for the vendored library; the lint is clean, but Wolfi may prefer the license list without `paths`. (5) Valkey 8.0/8.1/9.0 streams: the same change applies once `contrib/valkey`'s patch is checked against those tags (the Makefile and zmalloc.h hunks are stable, the `memefficiency.tcl` hunk is a test-suite file the package does not run).
- Phase 3 task 2, a decision with two reasonable outcomes for the coordinator: `DT_RPATH` was kept (see the design decision); design 13.6's "RUNPATH" text could be amended to "rpath (`DT_RPATH` or `DT_RUNPATH`)" or the package could pass `-Wl,--enable-new-dtags`. And the client tools: a server image with `valkey-9.1-cli` carries both libraries (the private quantum8 and the system quantum16 for the cli's `so:` dependency, about 400 KiB); the way to remove it is a static link from `libchalloc.a` into the three binaries, which is a Phase 3 follow-up if wanted, not done here.
- Phase 3 task 2, the results file's numbers are traffic checks, not benchmark rows (`valkey-benchmark` at 137 k / 172 k SET/s on the lane VMs inside containers); nothing here goes to the scorecard, and `src/` is untouched.
- Phase 3 task 3 (Ruby), the header question from task 1 restated with what this task adds: Valkey sidestepped `<jemalloc/jemalloc.h>` because its patch includes challoc's own `<challoc/jemalloc.h>`; Ruby's `--with-jemalloc` configure checks `jemalloc/jemalloc.h` and links `-ljemalloc`, so it needs a header under that path. The options stand: (a) `challoc-jemalloc-compat` grows a `-dev` subpackage with `/usr/include/jemalloc/jemalloc.h` (declaring the unprefixed and `je_` API, `JEMALLOC_VERSION*` 5.3.0, the `MALLOCX_*` macros; no `JEMALLOC_MANGLE`), conflicting with `jemalloc-dev` by path so a consumer names one of them, the same alternatives-by-name rule the runtime already follows; or (b) Ruby's environment keeps `jemalloc-dev` for the header and the compat library is what the built `ruby` finds at run time (the link name `libjemalloc.so` would then be jemalloc-dev's, pointing at Wolfi's `libjemalloc.so.2`, replaced at install by the compat package's file with the same soname; `DT_NEEDED libjemalloc.so.2` is satisfied either way, `scripts/check-needed.sh` verifies it). (a) is the honest package; (b) needs nothing new but builds Ruby against jemalloc's header, which for the symbols Ruby uses (`malloc_conf`, `mallctl`) is the same ABI. Lane P's recommendation for task 3 is (a), with `tests/c/jemalloc_link.c` compiled against the new header as its test.

RethinkDB (`rethinkdb.yaml` 2.4.4, epoch 14) is Ruby's shape: `./configure` takes the system jemalloc from `<jemalloc/jemalloc.h>` and `-ljemalloc`, so `challoc-jemalloc-compat-dev` in place of `jemalloc-dev`, `challoc-jemalloc-compat` in `runtime:`, `NEEDED libjemalloc.so.2` on `rethinkdb` (it calls `mallctl` itself) and `rethinkdb --version` in the test; a large C++ build with `gcc-12` (tens of minutes per architecture), and its `mallctl` keys should be read against design 13.4's list first.

- Phase 3 task 5 (b): open item, owner the coordinator with lane A (heap/arena layout): `select500` on aarch64 is 9.4% behind glibc on the median tps (213.1k against 233.1k; jemalloc 229.3k) with overlapping sorted lists; three same-session 30 s A/B pairs gave -5.2%, -6.5% and (order swapped) +2.2%, with equal instructions (162k against 168k per transaction) and L1d/dTLB misses under both, idle 0%: the row is bound by pgbench's 16 threads sharing the 16 CPUs with 500 backends. Re-measure server-bound (fewer pgbench threads, or the client on a second VM) before treating it as a throughput regression. On x86_64 the same row is +3.5%.
- Phase 3 task 5 (b): the `connect20` gap is a property of challoc's per-process layout, not of a code path: a fresh process owns 79 class regions and 251 metadata regions as separate mappings (plus their guards) in a 54 TiB span, and PostgreSQL forks a process per connection. Each connection pays 58 `mprotect` (slab commits), 2.4x the fork time (VMA and anon_vma duplication), 400 more page faults and the teardown of sparse page tables: 5.3 against 3.9 ms of kernel CPU per connection on aarch64, 4.6 against 3.6 on x86_64. Any mitigation (fewer, contiguous reservations per process; lazily created class regions; a cheaper commit than `mprotect`; larger first slabs so a fresh backend touches fewer regions) is lane A/B territory (`src/arena.rs`, `src/class.rs`, `src/layout.rs`) and a design decision (the isolation of size classes in separate ranges with guards is goal 1 of the README); this lane changed nothing under `src/`. The same shape costs 1.7 MiB of Pss_Anon per backend against glibc's 1.2 (+15% of the Pss sum at 500 backends). mimalloc pays the same connect penalty and 9 MiB per backend.
- Phase 3 task 5 (b): `MALLOC_CONF=stats_print:true` is accepted by challoc but "has no effect (not implemented)"; the attribution used `/proc/<pid>/smaps` by mapping name instead. If the plan wants `stats_print` for consumer attribution, it is a lane A/B item.
- Phase 3 task 5 (b): the `Dockerfile.dev` block and the Makefile (`postgres-bench` target, `.PHONY`, help text) and `bench/README.md` (target list line and one paragraph) are the only files touched outside `bench/postgres-bench.sh`, `bench/probes/pg-rss-sampler.sh` and the two results files; union the Dockerfile block with the other consumer lanes' blocks. The pass-duration field the sampler now writes (`S ... <seconds>`) and the "samples per run" column exist only from 97f84f6 on; the two results files were re-rendered with it.
- Phase 3 task 5 (a): the verdict on "is it really better than glibc?",
  from the soak's numbers only (the files end with the three-sentence
  form): better than glibc as shipped on every RSS figure on both
  architectures (x86_64 peak 1237 against 1260 MiB, tail mean 1119 against
  1161, 294 against 563 MiB held beyond the JVM's request, flat against
  +2.2 MiB/min; aarch64 peak 1271 against 1433, tail mean 1175 against
  1328, 398 against 613 MiB) and far better than jemalloc; not better than
  glibc tuned the way a good Java image ships it: glibc-trim's tail mean
  is 16% / 22% below challoc's and glibc-arena2's 14% / 13%, at equal
  req/s and 6..9% less server CPU per request in the high windows. The
  gap is the ~300 / ~400 MiB challoc holds at the end of every lull in
  dirty pages inside its 10 s decay window and half-occupied slabs,
  against a `malloc_trim(0)` every 5 s that returns everything: a
  decay-policy question for lane B (`src/purge.rs`, `src/policy.rs`),
  e.g. whether a JVM-shaped process should decay faster or purge the huge
  allocator's empty extents eagerly (`huge_retain_max` is 64 MiB and 222
  MiB of empty extents were resident at the final sample).

- Nothing from the task list was skipped or reverted: no cleanup moved the `.text` hash. `src/sys/errno.rs` needed no change (it already names every code the api uses; `Errno(n).0` is the `c_int`).
- The `cstr_of` test varies a `Config::DEFAULT` copy field by field and reads it back through `Config::get` (the `opt.<key>` read) and `cstr_of`; it cannot go through `ctl()` because that reads the process's one live `shared.config`. The live path is covered by `static_and_opt_keys_report_the_build_and_configuration`. The real fix (each enum's `name()` returning a C string so `cstr_of` disappears) touches `config.rs` and is a batch-three item.
- Later-batch candidates seen while reading, none attempted here: the `#[cfg(not(test))] mod exports` wrappers (a macro, batch three), a table-driven `ctl()` (batch three), splitting `print::json_report` (batch three), and `malloc_trim`'s `(purged > 0) as c_int` (glibc returns 1 whenever memory was released; challoc's answer counts purged slabs and extents, the same meaning).
- `docs/design.md` edits for the coordinator (design 13.4, `mallctl` namespace): the key list lacks `stats.thp.{mode,class_ranges,class_bytes,huge_ranges,meta_ranges,failures,process,nohuge_ranges}`, `stats.prefault.{enabled,populate_write,passes,slabs,bytes,skipped,fallbacks,wakes,active}` and `stats.percpu.{enabled,ids,headers,cached,aborts}` (all read-only `size_t`, `stats.percpu.enabled` a `bool`), and the `stats.cgroup.{purge_advice,lazy_purges,eager_purges}` trio that reads without a tracked cgroup; `arena.<i>.destroy` needs its semantics stated (a flush of the caller's caches plus a purge of arena `i`; the index stays registered and serves `MALLOCX_ARENA(i)` afterwards; `EFAULT` for 0 and `MALLCTL_ARENAS_ALL`, unlike jemalloc's retirement); `thread.arena` needs a note that a read builds the calling thread's heap if it has none (`heap_for_thread`, `EAGAIN` while it is being created or torn down) and that a write to a new arena builds that arena's cache node on first use (`Heap::set_arena`, swapping the header arrays, falling back to dropping the home caches when no metadata is available); `opt.tcache_max` reads as `unsigned` (`Value::Uint` -> `c_uint`), where jemalloc's is `size_t`, and the same holds for every `Value::Uint` key (`narenas`, `tcache_nslots_small_max`, `tcache_compact_shift`, `guard_slab_interval`, `huge_retain_max`).
- `MALLCTL_ARENAS_DESTROYED` (4097) is defined in both shipped headers (`contrib/jemalloc-compat/include/jemalloc/jemalloc.h:113`, `contrib/valkey/include/challoc/jemalloc.h:62`) and implemented nowhere: `arena.4097.*` and `stats.arenas.4097.*` fall out of `arena_index` (`MAX_ARENAS` is 4096) as `ENOENT`. Either drop the define from the headers or say in design 13.4 that it resolves to `ENOENT` (there are no destroyed arenas to sum).
- Not done, `Shared::release_routed`'s "changed arena during a flush" abort has no unit test: reaching it needs `release_owned` to stop at a slab whose `SlabTable::owner` word then reads the arena it just refused, i.e. the owner word rewritten between two loads by another thread, and the tables have no test-only setter for the group owner array (`class.rs`, lane B's file). A later batch that adds one (or a `#[cfg(test)]` hook in `SlabTable`) can drive it with a two-arena `Shared` and a hand-built batch.
- The per-CPU GC leg of the run test is skipped on the Mac's Docker VM (`__rseq_size` 20, no `mm_cid`, as every per-CPU test there); it runs on the reference VMs' `make test`. The coordinator's next VM run of the suite exercises it for the first time.
- No docs/design.md or README edits needed: nothing user-visible changed, no printed text changed.
- Later-batch candidates seen while reading, not touched (out of scope or another lane's file): `Heap::stats`, `set_tcache_enabled`, `destroy`, `flush_thread_caches` and `cache_in` each walk the `others` list by hand (a private iterator would fold five loops; batch two); `checked_slot_any` is `checked_slot` minus the arena test and could be the latter's body; `Shared::alloc_direct`'s one-element array claim.
- `PIDFD_SELF` is 6.15, not 6.14 (constant doc) nor 6.16 (the old test gate): v6.14's `include/uapi/linux/pidfd.h` defines no `PIDFD_SELF*` and its `kernel/pid.c` does not handle the sentinel; v6.15 defines `PIDFD_SELF_THREAD -10000` (`PIDFD_SELF` is its alias) and `pidfd_get_task` switches on it. Docs still saying 6.14, for the coordinator: `docs/research.md:95`, `docs/design.md:1010` (and 2178), `README.md:150`; `src/purge.rs:180` ("Linux 6.14", lane B's file) too.
